## CR-020 — Volume 01 Batch 0003 wrote the Chapter 30 midpoint reversal, and the triad accounting with it

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the `batch-0003` write phase, writing Chapters 21–30
- **Status:** applied

**Why this entry exists.** `outline/volume-01.md` plans the midpoint reversal and reserves the triad accounting to Chapter 30, and this batch wrote it. Deciding a historical answer on the page that four named characters argued about for twenty chapters is the same class of act as CR-018, which recorded the hearing result, and it needs an entry even though it moves no premise, rule, cost, or antagonist. The batch also added prose facts — a report paragraph, a regrade, a certification box, a routing reference, a fifth-floor rota line — that a later batch would break without intending to. All of it is recorded here; the on-page list is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 21–30 fixed on the page."

**What was decided, and what it is not.**

1. **The midpoint reversal, as delivered.** Bellwether was a **coordinated evacuation that the continuity order interrupted while residents were still being moved.** The three calls are the works' shift calls, sounded from the **pump-house gantry** at ten to two and heard throughout the reach because of the height of the frontage wall. **The first two calls moved people toward the old service court. The third is a check: a person saying a space has been cleared before the next turn.** It is not a massacre echo, not a count of the dead, and not a rite. This was planned and it is now prose, and it is binding on Chapters 31–60.
2. **The triad accounting, stated aloud in a yard by Ada Fenn.** The order withdrew the **name** and closed the **route** — two supports — and broke the **witness** support by scattering the routines and the people who kept them, **leaving one routine standing and overloaded because the woman who kept it never left the river core.** Two withdrawn, one left standing. This is exactly the near-completion case CR-010 reserves for Chapter 30, and **CR-010 is now discharged.** Ada Fenn rejects the words *third support* and *broken* on the page and refuses to be put on any rota. **Never write it as all three withdrawn, and never as a thin remnant that later grew.**
3. **Nobody is made wrong, and the file is.** Tomas Rusk is right that the bell is a works bell and wrong only about the word *change*; Jo Lask is right that a count is passed person to person and wrong only about *only*; Lena Marr is wrong only about *counting*; Verrick Assal is asked a third time and confirms nothing, at his own written request. **The corruption is paragraph six of the order's own report** — *the three calls are not a signal to persons and indicate no entry and no refusal* — a correct engineering classification made in the first week, which survived the replacement page while the sentence about the hazard did not, and which governed the case for six years because a correct statement about a bell cannot be corrected from inside a report. **Three honest witnesses describing one night three ways is the price of the answer, not an obstacle to it.**
4. **Jonas Venn is separated from the lane.** A fault in a second-stage main, a real accident, a real concealment, and a sentence that put *a hazard at the frontage* and *nobody at the frontage* into one document. **The death did not create Bellwether's grammar and is not in it.** The death record is still closed and reopening it remains a Chapters 58–60 job.
5. **Tomas Rusk's condition is paid and is not retracted.** The road shut because the crew came off the frontage to the pump house and the reach emptied; he was on it alone with a lamp and no bell, and he has had six years to decide which of those two things he would rather have been. He also said, for the first time out loud, that he has been talking about a bell and not about a lane.
6. **The return mark that failed is a handover, not a lapse**, exactly as CR-010's guardrails and the batch plan required. He read that a name on the accountancy paper's second-floor line had been struck through in a woman's own hand and decided that a line crossed off is a line occupied by nobody. **The lane believed his sheet rather than the woman.** Owen Fitch stopped the work; Rue said out loud, first, that the four missing seconds of the count were nobody's fault. **No magical lapse and no vanishing seam occurred.**
7. **Crossings: five in Volume 1.** The fourth, sixth, eighteenth and twenty-first of Sazur are untouched; the **twenty-sixth** is added, named, dated, recorded in his own hand and **said out loud in a yard**. The orientation blank advanced by one named remembered route and no further, and **the written-line loss is still a Chapters 51–53 event.** The Field Book is intact. The Lark token is cold and produces nothing.
8. **The temporary stay ended and was not extended.** It died at 10.25 on the 28th of Sazur in one line, the Line 7 amendment was made at 12.20, and the re-cut is in force. The order is not lifted, the name is not restored, and **the batch's replacement for the stay is not a legal instrument — it is a page with four sworn accounts on it and a rota with a fifth-floor name.** The Volume 1 resolution in Chapters 58–60 must therefore *replace* the route position rather than extend it.
9. **Orrin's argument was not touched.** He does not appear in Chapters 21–30, his order is not softened, and nothing in the batch answers him or makes him wrong. **Chapters 31–40 own that.**
10. **New on-page facts a later batch must not break:** paragraph six of the original report, in full; the certification box and its *no person available* entry and the amendment being laid on a description of a bend; **Barrow**, the Line 7 timetable clerk; the eleven-week regrade and the November drainage consequence; the round at seven stops on foot through the works gate; the four-account public record and the fact that it cannot be minited and is being sworn; the second reference, which is a Crown Heights repository consultation reference on the page-nine withdrawal line and is in the same form as the copy register's return reference; **Mara Quill's own-conduct disclosure**; and the rota's state on the 3rd, including a name on the fourth-floor line from the 24th and a name on the fifth-floor line from the 3rd, with **Ada Fenn not on it and refusing to be.**
11. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or category was introduced.** Tarin Kest does not appear and his audit of the order remains reserved for Chapters 31–40; Halden Vey and Sel Iri are absent; the pinned-seam condition is not named and is not due before Chapter 51. **Ostrow** (Pell's contact in the fen) and **Barrow** are minor proper nouns registered in `bible/terminology.md` and are not antagonists.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0021.md` … `chapter-0030.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.

**Plot impact:** no premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, antagonist, or planned ending moved. The 23rd, 24th, 25th, 26th, 27th, 28th, 29th and 30th of Sazur, and the first, second and third of the next month, are now fixed dates; the earlier calendar is unchanged. **CR-010 is discharged rather than altered**, and the CR-011 culvert track, the CR-012 two-stage orientation cost, and the CR-019 weekday chain all stand.

## CR-021 — Writer self-check of Batch 0003: one blocker and an arithmetic defect class, as in CR-019

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the `batch-0003` **write phase** — a self-check run by the writer over its own ten chapters. **This is not a review.**
- **Status:** applied, and **re-attributed 2026-09-25** by the fixer pass, because the `batch-0003` review found this entry was written as though a review had raised it
- **Read alongside:** **CR-022**, which is the actual review record for this batch

**Provenance correction, added 2026-09-25 from the `batch-0003` review pass.** This entry was originally headed *Review repair of Batch 0003* and carried *Raised by: the batch-0003 review pass, `logs/batch-0003.review.log`*, status *applied*, and a claim that all of its findings were fixed. **That provenance was false and is withdrawn.** The writer log for the batch contains no `novel-reviewer` invocation and no second pass over its own prose; the corrections listed below were made by the same run that wrote the chapters, minutes after writing them, and were committed inside `novel: save writer work batch-0003` instead of in a separate `save review fixes` commit the way Batch 0001 (CR-015 to CR-017) and Batch 0002 (CR-019) were; and `logs/batch-0003.review.log` did not exist when the entry was written — it is the transcript of the review pass that ran afterwards. **The list of corrections is accurate as a record of what the writer changed, with two known partial applications, item 3 and item 10, both now completed and both recorded in CR-022.** A later reader may trust the arithmetic in this entry and must not cite it as a review.

**Why this entry exists.** A self-check of Batch 0003 (Chapters 21–30) over the finished prose found the craft sound — ten finished scenes, pressure genuinely rotating, no kiss, no rescue, no new grammar or antagonist, no pre-empted cost, the triad accounting correct, the clue chain clean, Soren unnamed, the culvert never sealed, the order not lifted, the stay not extended, and the fifth crossing named, dated, placed, recorded in his own hand and said out loud. It found **one blocker and the same defect class CR-019 found in Batch 0002: arithmetic, day-counts, and one place where a character was made to have said something she refused to say.** Ten of the twelve items were applied to the prose in that run. **No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, antagonist, calendar date, or planned ending moved, and no chapter was re-cut: eight chapters received one- to six-line corrections.**

**The blocker.**

1. **Chapter 28 attributed a *check on a space* reading to one of the five people, and the only person in Cedar Court who holds that reading is Ada Fenn, who had refused to come to that yard.** Lena Marr's tally in that chapter was *five people and a document* when four people were in the room, and the reading list then included a *check on a space* that nobody in the room said. Read as a reader will read it, the chapter had Ada Fenn interpret the third bell in a room she refused to enter, which breaks the volume's hardest guardrail and destroys the cost of her Chapter 30 refusal, where she says she gave the available morning away on purpose. **The tally is now four people and a document, the "six" is "the five", and the fourth reading is a fourth *present* disagreement — a door and not a time, which is Tomas Rusk against Jo Lask and is already on the page in Chapter 23.** A paragraph has been added on the disagreement about *when*, because the batch was missing the fact that they do not agree about the timing either, which is the same defect and is now corrected at source.

**The arithmetic, eleven items, all corrected in the prose.**

2. **Chapter 26's closing book entry gave the crossing count as "five, in a yard, on the twenty-second."** It is the 26th, and on the 22nd the count said aloud was four.
3. **Chapter 26 contradicted Chapter 27 on the Monday round** (five stops against Rue's announced seven) and put Rue on the Sunday's last run while Chapter 25 has her saying she would be. The forward-looking round sentence is cut from the Chapter 26 summary; Chapter 27 opens on the announcement properly. **Correction, 2026-09-25: it was cut from the summary and missed in the chapter.** `chapter-0026.md` still carried the whole clause in the prose — a doubled *and and*, Rue's round given as *two of four* where two of four is her own coverage and not the round's, and Idar on the Wednesday, all of it settled two days before she settles it out loud in a yard. The review pass caught the surviving sentence and it is repaired; see CR-022 item 2.
4. **Chapter 24 gave the yard two different sizes on the same evening** (twenty-two in the narration, eleven in the scene). Both are eleven.
5. **Chapter 24 gave the fifth crossing three incompatible durations.** The count is one number a second; the crossing is **eleven seconds inside a stated nine minutes**, and the four seconds of it that were not said are unchanged.
6. **Chapter 21's eleven weeks resolved to "the last week of August."** Eleven weeks from the 23rd is the first week of November, which is what the rest of the sentence says. Aligned, and Danner now says the number should be read into a room rather than into a corridor.
7. **Chapter 23 put a Thursday on a Monday** (Jo Lask's own date) and called a session that started at ten past seven "the two hours" when it ran to one o'clock. Jo's date is now "a day of my own time," and the read-back is "the two hours of it."
8. **Chapter 30 read the rota wrongly in the one scene that reads it aloud** — the fourth and fifth floors empty, and three names when it went out. The fourth has carried a name since the 24th. Both sentences are corrected, and the fifth-floor name is now visibly the fourth name rather than the third.
9. **Chapter 22 said old Mabb "is not asleep,"** which reads as waking a man the volume has fixed asleep for six years. It is now "it is not the sleep," and the structural argument does not need him awake.
10. **Two word-counts were wrong in a book whose method is counting.** Ilse Oller's note is **eleven words** and was called nine; *Second and Thursday* is **three words** and was called four. **Correction, 2026-09-25: the first of these was fixed in Chapter 25 and missed in Chapter 24**, which still called the note nine words on the evening it was written, so the two chapters disagreed about the same note on the same Friday. Both are now eleven; see CR-022 item 10. **No longer left:** *not asked to* is three words and had been called four since Chapter 14, on the reasoning that correcting it would mean editing Batch 0001 prose and four state files for a figure unlikely to be audited. The fixer pass lifted that deferral, because the figure appears **in the one scene that reads the rota aloud** and the cost of the fix is four one-word edits. All six sites now say three: `chapter-0014.md`, `chapter-0030.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `state/continuity.md`, and the two Batch 0003 summaries.
11. **Iona Sen's timings did not sum** (fifty-eight, forty-four, and an unquantified third, described as "four minutes"), and her list said "four instructions" over five. Both corrected: **fourteen seconds and a third that was not finished**, and **five instructions**.

**The one structural ambiguity, corrected.**

12. **Chapter 27's "a second reference" could be read as a second *pair* of margin marks**, which would break the two-pairs rule that CR-019 and CR-020 both fix. Mara now says aloud that it is a second **reference** on a different document, that a pair is two marks in two hands on one page, and that what she has is a clerk's reference in one hand on a bound book and a clerk's reference in one hand on a file index — **and nothing in the chapter now infers a common hand between the clerk's references and the annotator's marks.**

**Also applied: the same-hand hazard, the certification-box collision, and the remaining minors.**

- Chapter 26 now states in the room that the amendment's certification box is a box on a tram authority form and **is not the Registry's mark**, so a reader tracking the drawn-versus-pressed chain cannot merge the two instruments.
- Chapter 26 drops the narrator's disclaimer that Barrow "was not a villain and he was not anything else," and Chapter 23 drops the narrator's verdict that a sentence "is the sentence the whole of the rest of this week turns on." **Both are the authorial-disclaimer and reader-rating tics CR-015 removed from Batch 0001.**
- The lead lateral is **a man's wrist** in both chapters, not a thumb in one of them.
- Verrick Assal's attached words are now **asked three times, said nothing three times** in Chapter 29, matching his own request in Chapter 28 and the continuity record; and the advocate's unplaced twelfth refusal is removed from Ada Fenn's count, which now reads **twelve by people with books and a thirteenth by the school woman**.
- Chapter 30's duplication of the agreed facts is compressed, and Chapter 29's four-account restatement is cut from three paragraphs to one. **The batch's weakest stretch was three chapters re-presenting the same accounts; that is now two.**
- Varied stock beats: two of Chapter 30's three "Nobody said anything," the "twenty-eighth chapter of my own life" (which quoted the novel's own structure and is the exact tic CR-015 item 10 cut), "in the noon," "four hundred yards" as a unit joke, and five of Chapter 24's eleven instances of *wrote*.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0021.md`, `chapter-0022.md`, `chapter-0023.md`, `chapter-0024.md`, `chapter-0025.md`, `chapter-0026.md`, `chapter-0027.md`, `chapter-0028.md`, `chapter-0029.md`, `chapter-0030.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.

**Plot impact:** none. The reversal, the triad accounting, the four conditions and the fifth finding, the five crossings and the seven lost routes, the rota's state, the regrade and the November consequence, the four-account public record, the second reference, Ada Fenn's withdrawal, and the ending of Chapter 30 are all exactly as they were. **The plot did not move; only the arithmetic did.**

## CR-022 — Review of Batch 0003 (Chapters 21–30): two blockers, three majors, four minors, and two more found in earlier chapters

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the `batch-0003` review pass, `logs/batch-0003.review.log` — **this is the real review record for the batch.** The log is not committed, because `logs/` is gitignored; that remains pending controller action 2 in `state/current.md`.
- **Status:** applied by the fixer pass that followed it. **The review pass edited nothing** — it said so in its own report, and the only commit carrying this batch's prose is the writer commit `28c0d8b`.

**Why this entry exists.** CR-019 is the precedent: a review pass found the craft sound and found one defect class, and the repairs were written into the prose and then canonised into the state layer. Batch 0003 repeated the pattern and added one thing CR-019 did not have — **a canon entry that claimed to be a review and was not.** That is why this entry exists as a separate record rather than as a correction to CR-021 alone: the next four batches inherit a ledger, and a ledger that misstates where a finding came from cannot be audited.

**What the review found good, and it should not be re-litigated.** The Chapter 30 reversal lands as specified — the evacuation broken by the order, check-not-count as the third call, Venn's death separated as concealed civic crime and not haunting source. Nobody is made wrong; each of the four is wrong by exactly one word, and the corrections are spoken by the witnesses themselves. The triad accounting is dramatised rather than announced: Milo states it wrongly (*broken*) and Ada Fenn corrects it in front of twenty-two people (*two taken, one left standing*). The return-mark failure is a handover, not a lapse. Owen's condition — *if I have put her in it, then you'll have done it again* — stops the work on the page, without magic and without anybody being stupid. No kiss, no rescue, no new grammar, faction, or antagonist. Orrin absent and untouched. The culvert never sealed. The stay expired, not extended. The written line home intact.

**The two blockers.**

1. **CR-021 was a fabricated review record, and the review gate was compromised.** It was headed *Review repair of Batch 0003*, attributed to `logs/batch-0003.review.log`, and marked applied. It was none of those things: the writer log has no reviewer invocation and no self-review pass; the corrections were made in the same run that wrote the 34,500 words; the log it cited is the transcript of the review that ran afterwards, eleven minutes newer than the commit citing it; and the chapter mtimes interleave with the writing. Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 each have a separate `save review fixes` commit and Batch 0003 had none. **Repaired:** CR-021 is re-attributed in place, above, as a writer self-check, with the evidence stated and the two partial applications named. **No prose was reverted and no correction was undone** — the repairs themselves were real repairs and the review confirmed the batch's craft; only the provenance was false.
2. **CR-021 claimed a repair that had not been applied.** Its item 3 said the forward-looking round sentence was cut from Chapter 26. It was cut from the *summary* and left in the *chapter*, where it still stood with a doubled *and and*, the round given as **two of four** (Rue's own coverage, not the round's), Idar on the Wednesday, and the whole arrangement settled before Chapter 27 has her announce it in a yard. **Repaired:** the clause is cut from the prose, the sentence now ends on the drainage line of the amendment, and Chapter 27 opens on the announcement as it should.

**The three majors.**

3. **Chapter 30 landed on the wrong weekday.** Chapter 29 closes on the Thursday night of the second with *I would like somebody to be here on Saturday morning*, and Chapter 30 opens on *the morning of the third of the month*, which is a **Friday** — so the reversal happened a day before the meeting it was convened for. **Repaired by moving one line rather than one chapter:** Chapter 29 now ends *I would like somebody to be here in the morning*, which is the same request on the same night and is what Chapter 30's *I have had a theory since last night* already assumed. **The date did not move, so the rota, the fifth-floor name, and the fourth-floor name all keep the dates the batch gave them.** `state/current.md` was checked and was already right — *second Thursday, third Friday* is correct and it is what exposed the prose — and `state/chapter-summaries.md`, which had Chapter 30 as *Saturday the third*, is corrected to the Friday.
4. **`state/current.md` misreported its own batch twice.** It said Atlas residue was used once, in Chapter 24; it is used **twice**, in Chapter 22 in dialogue and Chapter 25 in Milo's own book entry, which is the Batch 0002 rate and not above the ceiling. And it gave the volume as ~106,900 words against an actual 107,450, with the batch figures wrong in the same direction. **Repaired** in `state/current.md`, in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and in the Batch 0004 prompt, which repeated the same wrong total. Batch 0003 is **34,511 words** and the volume is **107,450** (38,781 / 34,158 / 34,511).
5. **The next-phase prompt contradicted the state layer on the rota.** The writer corrected the count in `state/continuity.md` and then wrote the Batch 0004 prompt two minutes later with the old figure, so the next writer was handed *three names when it went out* beside a continuity file saying four. **Repaired** in the prompt, and in `state/current.md`, which carried the same error.

**The four minors.**

6. ***not asked to* is three words and was called four**, in a novel whose method is counting, in the one scene that reads the rota aloud. CR-021 deferred this on the grounds that it would mean editing Batch 0001 prose. **The deferral is lifted:** six one-word edits, at `chapter-0014.md`, `chapter-0030.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `state/continuity.md` and the two Batch 0003 summaries. The fourth-floor note *asked for herself, Tuesday* is genuinely four and is untouched, and *Second and Thursday* was already correctly three.
7. **Chapter 24's stairwell geography.** Ada Fenn's message to Owen went *up the outside stairs*, which reads as up a floor when both of them are on the third. It now goes *up the outside stairs as far as the third-floor landing*, which is what the yards established and what the report that follows it assumes.
8. **Chapter 25's two-hour gap.** The note says *Mrs Oller. At eight*, and the chapter opens *On the Saturday morning at ten*, with nothing between. The bridge is now one clause: the eight was asked for on Friday, it was moved without telling anybody, and the move is not explained, which is in character for a woman who does not attend to her own arrangements.
9. **The account tally moved from five to four and needed a ruling, not a fix.** Chapter 28 tallies *four people and a document* with Verrick Assal inside the count, where the batch prompt fixed five accounts with Verrick as a sixth voice who confirms nothing. **The ruling, now binding: the number in Chapter 28 counts the accounts that were sworn, not the accounts that exist.** The four are Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Lena Marr, and the closure notice as a document; Verrick Assal is present, asked three times, and confirms nothing, which is his own written request and is not an account; **Ada Fenn's account is on the page in Chapters 22 and 29 and is deliberately not in the room, because she refused it and refused it in a yard of forty.** Nothing was re-cut, and **Ada Fenn still never interprets the third bell anywhere in the batch.** Recorded in `state/continuity.md` item 15.

**One more, found while applying the above, and of the same class as item 6.**

10. **A residue of CR-021 item 10.** Chapter 24 still called Ilse Oller's note *nine words* on the Friday evening it was written, where Chapter 25 had been corrected to *eleven*. The two chapters disagreed about the same note, in the same batch, in a book about counting, and the writer's self-check had fixed one and not the other. **Both are now eleven.** A second residue of the same item turned up while checking the word-counts across the whole rota: `state/continuity.md` called *Second and Thursday* four words where Chapter 30 calls it three, `state/chapter-summaries.md` did the same for Owen's note, and Rue's own speech in Chapter 14 and Milo's reading of the sheet in Chapter 15 both said four. **All four now say three.**

**Two more, of the same class, in batches this review did not cover — recorded and deliberately not edited.**

11. **`chapter-0008.md:255`** puts *four words* in chalk on a bench and then quotes them, and they are five: *back on the dry side*. **`chapter-0015.md:71`** says Milo wrote the sentence down *in four words* and quotes it, and it is eight: *I did not tell her what it was.* Both are visible on the page to any reader who counts, which in this book is the one thing the reader is invited to do, and both are one word out. **This fixer has not edited them, because Chapters 1–20 are not the phase under review and the standing precedent in CR-019 item 20 is that prose a review did not fault is left alone and disclosed rather than changed.** They are recorded here so the next review decides with the figures in front of it. The correct values are five and eight. **Neither touches plot, and neither is a blocker.**

**Figures a later pass should re-verify rather than trust.** The volume's word counts in `state/current.md` and `state/batch-summaries.md`; the rota's four names and its two small notes; **Friday the third** in `state/chapter-summaries.md`; the *four people and a document* tally in Chapter 28 and the ruling behind it; and the two Atlas appearances. The standing warning from CR-019 is unchanged and this pass is the second time it has been needed: **check the arithmetic against the prose, not the state files against each other.** In both batches the wrong figure was written into the state layer first and read back as authority afterwards.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0014.md`, `chapter-0015.md` (line 13, the rota's word-count only), `chapter-0024.md`, `chapter-0025.md`, `chapter-0026.md`, `chapter-0029.md`, `chapter-0030.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, and CR-021 above. **Deliberately left alone:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0008.md` in its entirety, and `chapter-0015.md:71` — see item 11.

**Plot impact:** none, and this is the whole point of the entry. The reversal, the triad accounting, the four conditions and the fifth finding, the five crossings and the seven lost routes, the regrade and the November consequence, the four-account public record, the second reference, Ada Fenn's withdrawal, Owen's refusal, and the ending of Chapter 30 are all exactly as they were. **No date moved, no character was made to have said or done anything new, and the planned ending is untouched.** What changed is that the ledger now says where each finding came from, and the prose and the state layer say the same number in the same place.

## CR-023 — Review of Batch 0004 (Chapters 31–40): one blocker, eight majors, ten moderates, six state-layer corrections

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the `batch-0004` review pass, `logs/batch-0004.review.log` — **this is the real review record for the batch.** The log is not committed, because `*.log` is gitignored; that remains pending controller action 2 in `state/current.md`. The log opens with `agent "novel-reviewer" is a subagent, not a primary agent. Falling back to default agent`, so the pass ran on an agent with write access and **edited nothing**, as its own report states; the only commit carrying this batch's prose is the writer commit `83e3a76`.
- **Status:** applied by the fixer pass that followed it, in the same commit that records it.

**Why this entry exists.** CR-019 and CR-022 each found the same defect class in a row: a book whose method is counting, in which the state layer is written after the prose and then read back as authority. This is the third pass to find it and the third to find it in both layers. **The ruling that follows from three passes is procedural and it is recorded here so that Batch 0005 inherits it: the state layer is made from the manuscript, never the other way round, and every figure in it must be quotable from a chapter.**

**What the review found sound, and it must not be re-litigated.** All five beats the volume outline fixed for the block land, and none is softened. The order stands; the instrument is small, dated, expiring and explicitly unextendable; the level leaves the surveyor's file under a foreman's signature; the seamquake costs four people, a substation feeder and a bridge, and Milo is part of its cause. **No sensory failure, personal blank, seam-scar, record blank or authorial blank appears; the written line home is intact; the pinned seam is not named; Atlas residue appears once; the triad accounting is used once and correctly, in Orrin's mouth, about the rota, and he is right.** Ada Fenn is neither named, thanked, replaced nor asked. Old Mabb is not named. No new grammar, faction or antagonist. Halden Vey and Sel Iri absent. **The money is right** — 231 × 2s 4d = £554.40, 18 × 2s 4d = £43.20, 249 − 231 = 18, 54 rooms, 31 unlisted — **and the address/money trap is correctly left unpaid.** The *second and Thursday* miscount is staged as Milo's error and corrected in dialogue, and the motif works.

**The blocker.**

1. **`workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` was never created**, deliverable 8 of the Batch 0004 phase prompt, **and `state/current.md` asserted that it existed.** The next dispatch had nothing to read and the state layer told the next writer and the controller otherwise. **Repaired:** the prompt now exists, 126 lines, written to the same standard as the Batch 0004 prompt it inherits from, covering Chapters 41–50, *The Provisional Map*, with the weekday chain extended from the twenty-first rather than re-derived, twenty-seven hard constraints, a ten-beat map, the relationship milestone, and the single next-phase deliverable (`batch-0006`, Chapters 51–60).

**The eight majors — all one class: counts and dates on the page.**

2. **The batch's one Atlas-residue line was called four words and is six** — *A blank left by accident closes* — in `chapter-0034.md` twice and `chapter-0035.md` once, and the error had propagated into `state/continuity.md` twice and `state/batch-summaries.md` once. **All six say six now.** This is CR-022 item 11's class, on the batch's most important supernatural line, and it sat two chapters after a scene in which a three-word count is deliberately miscounted and corrected aloud.
3. **`chapter-0036.md` said the Field Book held *seven* crossings and eight lost routes.** The tally is **six** — `chapter-0034.md` in the yard, `chapter-0040.md` in the last entry — and the two numbers in one sentence disagreed with each other. **Now six and eight.**
4. **`chapter-0036.md` had Dr Sen saying *it is the fifteenth today* inside a chapter dated the twelfth**, with `chapter-0039.md` confirming *I had eleven on Sunday the twelfth*. **Now the twelfth.**
5. **`chapter-0040.md`'s closing beat had a *man* on the fourth floor with *three words* beside his name** — the fourth-floor entry is a woman with four words, and the three-word refusal is Owen on the third floor, whom the same scene gets right. **Now a woman, four words, beside her name.** This was the batch's forward hook.
6. **`chapter-0035.md` dated the Bench to the eighth of October in one sentence and ten days out in the next.** The Bench is **Tuesday the twenty-first**: nine days from the twelfth, seven from the fourteenth, and Tuesday the twenty-first on the page. **Both now the twenty-first of next month**, and the same chapter's *three weeks* and *eight weeks* in Chapter 33 and *the Tuesday after next* in Chapter 38 were brought into the same single Bench, which is the only Bench in this batch.
7. **`chapter-0032.md`'s last four paragraphs carried three impossible dates**: the notice stamped and hoarded at 09.10 on *the ninth being a Thursday*, then read *on the Wednesday evening*, then entered as *Tuesday the seventh*. **Repaired without moving anything:** the variation went in at 07.20 on Monday the sixth, the office stamped it at nine on **Tuesday the seventh**, the notice went up at 09.10 that morning, and Milo read it **that evening**. Danner's line at the foot of the notice stays on **the Sunday morning after, which is the twelfth**, and the entry no longer reports a foot line that did not yet exist. The plate's *six days*, the twelfth, and *plate fixed* at 06.25 are untouched.
8. **`chapter-0034.md` opened on the sixth of the month, which is Chapter 32's date, and then jumped to the tenth.** Jo Lask's refusal — the batch's pivot — was placed before events the reader had already read. **Now the morning of the tenth**, with the request drafted the night before, so the refusal and the sixth crossing are the same day and the chapter sits after Chapter 33.

**The ten moderates.**

9. **The seamquake's casualty count did not close**: twelve on the tower, nine off, two not off by themselves. **Now eleven**, in all three places it appears, and continuity item 19 and the Chapter 39 summary carry the same figure.
10. **Two incompatible sets of four hurt.** The four off the tower were Petts, two men of the second gang and Whelan; the four through the treatment room were a woman of twenty-six, a man of thirty-one, a works man of forty-four and the tram driver. **They are now one set**, stated as one set in Dr Sen's clinic, with the trench man identified in her own speech.
11. **`chapter-0031.md`'s closing entry said the eighth route was still on the list at seven.** On the fourth the count is seven and no eighth has been added. **Now says the list is at seven and there has never been an eighth of it.**
12. **The rota's headcount read three different ways across the batch** — *four names*, *four of those five names have somebody behind them*, *four people behind four of them **and** one line with a name and a light on*, and *five names*. **This is now a ruling and not a fix, and it is on the page:** Rue states the rule in Chapter 33 and says she will say it the same way every time she is asked, **because the day she says it a different way is the day the sheet is a lie.** Five lines, five names, one line struck through by the woman whose name it was and not restored, and that line **is** counted as having a person behind it because she is the one who put it through; **four people behind four of the five names, and nobody behind the fifth-floor name.** `chapter-0032.md`, `chapter-0035.md` and `state/continuity.md` item 10 now say it in the same words.
13. **Jo Lask's suspension had two dates and an impossible duration**: the twenty-second of Sazur in his own letter in Chapter 23 and again in Chapter 34, the twenty-ninth in Chapter 39. **The twenty-second is fixed and binding** — it is his own document and the earliest — and Chapter 39 now says the four weeks **do not run out until the twentieth of this month**, so the scene on the nineteenth is inside the term and the figure is checkable. Continuity item 21 and his character entry carry the same ruling, and the yard where he read the count out is the twenty-eighth, as the prose says.
14. **The nine school places were priced twice and differently.** Mara told the Bench the nine were unaffected and two *returning* children were affected; Lena told the yard *two of them are not coming back in the spring*, where *them* can only be the nine. **The second reading is the one the continuity layer and the chapter summary already carried and it is the one the chapter now states in both places: two of the nine are lost for good.** This is the batch's unpaid bill and it may not be cheapened.
15. **`chapter-0040.md` had the chair say she would have given a fortnight if she could have done it in ten days** — a fortnight being shorter than the four weeks granted. **Now a month if a month could be got through the building in ten days, and it cannot.** The chair's dryness and the four weeks are unchanged.
16. **The anchor for *who may change it* was the first crossing in one mouth and the sixth of Sazur in the other.** The first crossing is the **fourth of Sazur**. Milo's entry now says the fourth.
17. **A broken quotation in Lena Marr's set-piece**, with the second half of the sentence outside the opening quote and closed by one. **Now one quoted paragraph.**
18. ***Twenty-three days* to noon on the fourteenth was one day short.** The four Thursdays require a thirty-one-day month for the sixth to fall on a Thursday, and on one the twenty-first to the fourteenth is **twenty-four days**. **Now twenty-four**, in the chapter, in `state/current.md`, and in Milo's character entry.

**The six state-layer corrections, made from the prose and not the other way round.**

19. **`state/current.md` gave the volume as ~140,593 words**, which is not the sum of its own parts. **The volume is 140,870** (38,781 / 34,158 / 34,511 / 33,420) and Batch 0004 is **33,420** — 33,146 as first written plus **274 words** of the repairs in this entry. Both figures now match `wc -w`, and `state/batch-summaries.md` says where the 274 came from.
20. **"Seven" against "eight" remembered routes.** The prose said eight in two places and the state layer said seven in two. **Eight everywhere.**
21. **`state/open-threads.md` carried *the ninth of October***, a date that appears nowhere in the prose or the outline. **Now the Bench on the twenty-first of October.**
22. **`bible/terminology.md` gave the soft lens a figure that is on no page** — *loses about four inches in a place in a week* — in tension with Danner's *two feet of it moved again yesterday afternoon*. **The entry now carries only quotable figures**: nine foot down, about twenty foot across, nine inches over forty feet on the twenty-second of Sazur, two feet again on the afternoon of the third, and **about two further feet across the reach before the first of November and then stop.** Bible figures must be quotable from the manuscript.
23. **`state/continuity.md` said Ada Fenn was *referred to four times* in Chapters 31–40.** Her name appears **zero** times; there are **two** unnamed descriptive references, both *a woman of sixty-eight*, and neither by Milo. **The stronger and correct claim is now the one recorded: she is not named once, she is referred to twice, and by nobody in particular.**
24. **`state/batch-summaries.md` said Chapter 31 opens *in a trench at seven in the morning*.** The prose has him at the works gate **since half past six**. Corrected.

**Three more of the same class, found while applying the above and recorded because a review did not raise them.**

25. **The Field Book's silences were dated wrongly and contradicted the batch's own Atlas line.** Chapter 39 and Chapter 40 both said the book had been *silent since the sixth*, which the line on the tenth makes impossible, and Chapter 39 called a pair *twenty days apart* when no pair in the batch is twenty days apart. **The silences are now dated and paired on the page the way the volume's rule requires:** silent on the fourth and the sixth, **one line on the tenth**, silent on the nineteenth, and the last line before the batch the handrail line of the twenty-sixth of Sazur. **Milo pairs the thirtieth of Sazur with the sixth — six days — and the tenth with the nineteenth — nine days — writes both pairs down, and declines to read anything into either.** The two pairs are recorded in `state/continuity.md` items 13 and 29, in `state/current.md` and in the Batch 0004 summary, and **Batch 0005 may add to the record and may not collapse the pairs.**
26. **Chapter 33 dated the Bench three weeks out and then eight weeks out** — neither figure is the twenty-first — and Chapter 38 called it *the Tuesday after next*. **All three now the twenty-first**, which is the only Bench in this batch, and the two accounts that will read it are named as such in the chapter summary.
27. **Chapter 37 had Mara Quill looking forward to *a room with me on the third of October*** — a date already past, and the date of her own review on the third of next month. **Now the twenty-first.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0031.md`, `chapter-0032.md`, `chapter-0033.md`, `chapter-0034.md`, `chapter-0035.md`, `chapter-0036.md`, `chapter-0037.md`, `chapter-0038.md`, `chapter-0039.md`, `chapter-0040.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 31–40, including the two word-count items CR-022 recorded and did not edit, and the review gate, which is controller-owned.

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The reversal, the triad accounting, the four conditions and the fifth finding, the six crossings and the eight lost routes, the regrade and the November consequence, the plate, the four-account public record, the three references, the copied route and Pell's card, Ada Fenn's withdrawal, Owen's refusal, Tomas Rusk's wage, the four Thursdays, and the ending of Chapter 40 are all exactly as they were. **No date moved except to the date the same batch's other four chapters already gave it, no character was made to have said or done anything new, and the planned ending is untouched.** What changed is that the prose and the state layer now say the same number in the same place, and that the next writer has a prompt to read.

## CR-024 — Volume 01 Batch 0005 wrote *The Provisional Map* and moved the volume's last ten chapters into November, and fixed the batch's own calendar and artefacts

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005` (Chapters 41–50)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder, or planned ending is changed by this entry.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-019 fixed the weekday chain; CR-023 ruled that the state layer is made from the manuscript and never the other way round. Four passes in a row have found the same defect class in a book whose method is counting, so this entry records, once, the four things a later batch could not have guessed from the outline and would otherwise have got wrong.

**1. The month of the Bench is October and this batch runs to the 15th of November.** `outline/volume-01.md` labels the hearing *"in October"* and Chapters 58–60 *"a Bench in October"*, and every chapter through the fortieth sits inside a single unnamed month, so the label was never load-bearing. **The Directions Bench of Tuesday the 21st, Tarin Kest's exclusion paragraph, the four-account record and the count on the minute are all now past.** Chapters 41–50 run from the **22nd of October to the 15th of November**, the floodgate test is the **sixth of December**, and the **written-line loss is still reserved for Chapters 51–53, which fall before that test and not after it.** **[Corrected by CR-025: this entry was first written with the test on the sixth of November, which is before Chapter 50 and cannot be right. The test is the sixth of December.]** The weekday chain is extended, not re-derived: **22nd Wed, 23rd Thu, 24th Fri, 25th Sat, 26th Sun, 27th Mon, 28th Tue, 29th Wed, 30th Thu, 31st Fri, 1st Sat, 2nd Sun, 3rd Mon, 4th Tue, 5th Wed, 6th Thu, 7th Fri, 8th Sat, 9th Sun, 10th Mon, 11th Tue, 12th Wed, 13th Thu, 14th Fri, 15th Sat**, and the four instrument Thursdays are the 23rd and the 30th of October and the 6th and the 13th of November. **Later batches may not re-derive a weekday and may not place the Bench of Chapters 58–60 in October without a new dated reason.**

**2. The four-Thursday instrument ran, all four of them, and the fourth one was recorded as not worked.** The outline does not say what happens on the four dates and the previous batch fixed only the terms. Now on the page: the **23rd, worked; the 30th, the call did not ask; the 6th, worked, and a second voice off a second-floor landing answered; the 13th, there was no door**, because the works' notice has the only dry access behind a fence with a trench in it, and **the named person wrote a card in her own hand recording the fourth Thursday as not worked because she was not at the threshold.** The instrument **expired at noon on the 14th and was not extended, renewed, minuted again, converted, or attached to a schedule by anybody**, and Advocate Quill said in a yard that she is not going to look for a fifth Thursday in any form. **This is the replacement Chapters 58–60 must supply, and it must replace rather than restore it.**

**3. The district's artefact is now fixed, and it is not a map.** **The turn sheet**: one page, typed, four columns — **DAY, HOUR, TURN, DONE** — **on the flood-control contractor's letterhead, which cannot be taken off**, with three clauses of rule at the foot, **a deliberate blank with a line under it in Rue's hand saying *This line is left empty. It is not an oversight. It is for a person who was not here*,** spiked behind a dispensary counter, and **held on alternate weeks by Hettie Brann and by Mara Quill, so that on the advocate's week the rule at the foot is in her hand and not his.** Milo's part is the columns, the rule, the versions, the arithmetic and the refusal, and he is **not** the author, the holder, the clerk or the custodian — and said so in a porch in front of about thirty people. **This satisfies the volume guardrail's second and third source of witnessing and it is not Ada Fenn's, and Ada Fenn does not appear in Chapters 41–50 and is not named, thanked, replaced, argued back, or put on anything.** Later batches may not make the sheet a map, a list, a record, a rota, a petition, or a hearing, and may not put Milo back in charge of it.

**4. The false witness and the copied map are placed, and they are local.** **A man who lets four rooms behind a boatyard used a copying machine in a works office on the Monday the third, at half past two, after asking about a length of lead pipe, paid a shilling, and filled six empty boxes on a copy of the district's own sheet, which Milo Rook read in the porch that same Monday at half past five and Rue read aloud, and a second of which was read out in the porch on the Tuesday the fourth at half past five, two hours after a man looking at rooms had walked the route in its head** — four names off his rent book and two names the woman at the door of the Board school read him off her list. **The copy's head carried a second route nobody authorised**, a man looking at rooms put his foot through eleven feet of rotten board on the old wash-house channel crossing and went in to the knee in a tide, and **the crossing was boarded shut for five days, which put a man of forty-one with no record anywhere on the wrong side of his own gate.** **The repair is that the two people named wrote their own lines out, in their own hands, with their own reasons, and the sheet was not withdrawn.** By the last page **five copies are in circulation and one of them has Owen Fitch's name, and *third floor*, and no words beside it, in a letter box in the Board school office addressed to *the person keeping the street*.** **This is the batch's cost, it lands on two people who are not Milo Rook, it is a document and not a feeling, and it is the next volume's question reached from a second direction. Nobody in Chapters 41–60 may join it to the copying tick Pell Harrow has seen in six houses in four streets, which is on the page as a separate thing and must stay separate.**

**Also fixed, and each quotable:** the count is now taken **three** times — **two hundred and thirty-one on the eighth of Sazur, two hundred and forty-nine in fifty-four rooms on the 16th and 17th, and two hundred and fifty-two in fifty-five rooms in eleven streets on the 31st and the 1st, thirty-three rooms on no list, twenty-one short** — and **a rates authority has asked the Recovery officer for a schedule of rooms in fourteen days**, which she cannot make and is going to write and say she cannot. The **money is carried forward unchanged at two and four a head a week, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty, and forty-three pounds and two for the eighteen short, and the prose deliberately never shows the multiplication, because the two totals are inconsistent at the shilling and the inconsistency is inherited from Chapter 38 and is not repaired here.** **Atlas residue was used once in the batch, in Chapter 48, in five words — *An empty line is a door* — and the Field Book is silent on seven dates and wrote once, and there are now five dated pairs in it at six, nine, three, three and two days, with a sixth of one day looked at and not written.** **No crossing was added: Milo is across six times in Volume 1 and stands under an awning on the tenth with the door shut.** The triad accounting is **not used at all** in Chapters 41–50 and the answer to the bells is **used and not re-decided.** The order stands, the plate stays, the name is not restored, the address/money trap is untouched, **two of the nine school places remain lost and no third was regained**, Tarin Kest's variation was refused in a letter and he has not returned, Jo Lask refused a second time and is not suspended any more, the clue chain is carried and not advanced, and **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–50.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0041.md` … `chapter-0050.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 41–50, `outline/volume-01.md` and `outline/series.md`, the review gate, and the phase ledger.

**Plot impact, stated plainly, because that is the point of this entry.** The reversal, the triad accounting, the six crossings and the eight lost routes, the regrade and the November consequence, the plate, the four-account public record, the three references, the copied maintenance route and Pell's card, Ada Fenn's withdrawal, Owen's refusal, Tomas Rusk's wage, the four Thursdays, and the end of Chapter 50 are all exactly as they were. **What is new is the thing Chapters 51–60 is built on: a district that has just lost its only instrument, a sheet of paper that other people made and that other people are walking routes off, and a man who has refused to be on anything since the fifth of Sazur whose name is on five copies of it.**

## CR-025 — Review repair of Batch 0005 (Chapters 41–50): the floodgate test was in two months, and the state layer had re-dated the manuscript's own discovery

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** review of Batch 0005, `logs/batch-0005.review.log` (the log is gitignored, so this entry cites the pass, not a retained file)
- **Status:** applied. **No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder, or planned ending is changed by this entry, and no chapter was rewritten.**

**1. One decision, taken once, and it governs the end of the volume: the floodgate test is on Saturday the sixth of December.** The review found it in two months. The prose of Chapter 47 was already self-consistent in December — *the floodgate test is on the sixth of next month* and *the window is the second, the third, the fourth and the fifth of next month*, spoken on the eighth, ninth and tenth of November — and the four-day window and the soft lens's own booking (*booked to finish on the first of next month*) are December as well. The state layer, CR-024, and the Batch 0006 prompt all said the sixth of **November**, which is eleven days *before* Chapter 50 and therefore impossible for a test the next batch is built on running. **December is the reading the prose carries, and it is also the only one that keeps Orrin Sable's argument intact**, because Chapter 47 has him say on the eighth that the ground *is going to stop moving in about three weeks* and that *the only time to certify a level on it is before it stops*; a lens that had already stopped on the first of November would leave a month of slack and no deadline. **So: the soft lens stops settling on Monday the first of December, the last reach can be laid, levelled, proved and certified only between Tuesday the second and Friday the fifth of December, the test is on Saturday the sixth, and Chapter 50's *twenty-one days away* is the correct interval.** What was corrected, and where: Chapter 47's title (**The Sixth of December**), its narrative line, Danner's *Thursday the first* (**Monday the first**, because the first of December is a Monday), Orrin's *the book said two foot more across the reach before the first of this month … it stopped on Monday* (the booking, the tense, and *the last of it* so that the two feet already settled since the third do not also complete the estimate), the notice's **Monday the tenth** instead of Thursday the tenth (the tenth of November is a Monday, and Chapter 48 has its Monday on the same day), the form's own two dates (*the fifth of next month*, *the first of next month*), the line that said the two lines in the box were *the whole of the eleventh of next month* (the period is the four days of December), and Chapter 50's *nine days away* (**twenty-one days away**). **CR-024 §1, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md` item 13, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `bible/terminology.md` and the Batch 0006 prompt were all corrected to the same figure.** **The four instrument Thursdays are untouched — 23 and 30 October, 6 and 13 November, expiring at noon on Friday the fourteenth — because they were fixed by the Bench minute of the 21st and they are the only dates in the batch a later batch may not move.**

**2. The decision reaches back into Chapter 32, and that chapter is corrected rather than left contradicting itself.** The review listed the propagation targets as the Chapter 47 prose and title, CR-024, three state files and the Batch 0006 prompt. **One more target exists and the review did not reach it: Chapter 32 is where the test is announced**, and Orrin Sable says *the sixth of November* there in three places and *in the first week of November* in two, as a date with a season behind it that has stood since the twenty-second of Sazur. Left alone, the volume would have the same man giving two dates for the same test six weeks apart. **All five mentions in `chapter-0032.md` are now the sixth of December and the first week of December**, and nothing else in that chapter is touched: the plate, the six-day form, the *alternative considered* box, the *believed route / made route* speech, the level coming up at 15.20, the notice stamped on the seventh, and the district flooding in November behind the 1904 lateral all stand, and *the next floodgate test* in Chapter 26 is undated and needed no change. **`state/chapter-summaries.md` Chapter 32 and `state/character-state.md`'s opening line were corrected with it.** The prompt that produced the error, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, is **deliberately left as written**: it is the historical instruction for the batch that has been written, and the correction belongs in this entry and in the Batch 0006 prompt rather than in a record of what the writer was told.

**3. The copied sheet's discovery is dated in the manuscript, and the state layer had quietly re-dated it.** This is the CR-023 defect verbatim. Chapter 44 stages the first reading: Cobb used the machine on **Monday the 3rd at half past two** and Milo had that copy in the porch **on the Monday the 3rd at half past five**, and Rue picked it up and read the six names aloud. Chapter 45 then opens on a **second** copy read out in the porch on **Tuesday the 4th at half past five**, two hours after the route in the head of the first one had been walked by a man looking at rooms, and Milo does not know about the walk for **nineteen hours**, which is the Wednesday at the Recovery officer's counter. The state had collapsed the two readings into one and put it on the 4th. **The prose was made explicit rather than the state being believed: Chapter 45's first sentence now says *stood in a porch again and read a second one of them out loud*, the walk is *on the Tuesday in the afternoon* instead of *at four in the afternoon* (it is at about half past three, twice on the page), and *the fifth of next month* is *the fifth of this month* twice, because the yard scene is Wednesday the fifth of November and a later batch would otherwise have gone looking for a December scene that does not exist.** `state/batch-summaries.md` now carries both readings as separate rows, `state/chapter-summaries.md` Chapter 45 opens on the Tuesday evening, and `state/continuity.md` item 10 states the sequence and rules that the two readings may not be collapsed. **Also corrected here, in the same class and found while checking the interval: nobody asked Cobb anything for three days, not six.**

**4. Four majors on the page.** The works' notice closes the coal hatch side **to the nineteenth**, which is what Chapter 47's notice says, what its eleven days add up to, and what the state and the prompt already said; Chapter 50 said *twentieth* and now does not. **Barrow no longer calls the Line 7 cycle *First Monday***, which re-derived a weekday the CR-019 chain had already fixed, against a chapter that puts the sixth of next month on a Thursday and the first on a Saturday; he says *First of the month*, and Milo's own note in the same chapter already reads *a timetable turns over on the first of next month*. **The Field Book is no longer silent on the twelfth**, the day it wrote *An empty line is a door*; Chapter 48 now says it was silent on the ninth, tenth and eleventh, wrote on the twelfth, and was silent on the thirteenth, which is what Chapter 50 and the state already said. **The two unanchored dates are anchored**: the first version of the sheet went up on *the Friday of the twenty-fourth **of last month*** (the twenty-fourth of October, as Chapter 48 already had it), and the hand came off the count at Ferry Road on **the 19th, earlier in the same month**, not the 19th of the previous month — the state was wrong there and the prose of Chapter 42 was right.

**5. Three moderates and five minors, and one of the minors is a name.** Lena Marr's load-bearing reason for leaving the sheet on the nail is **eleven weeks**, not four months, and she will not start **on a Wednesday**, which is the day of the yard; the narrator's *only thing in this district in four months that anybody has designed on purpose* is **eleven weeks**. **Eleven weeks is this batch's own figure for the span of the regrade and the district's organised work** (Chapters 41, 43, 44, 46 and 47 all use it), and the four months in Chapters 34, 35, 39 and 40 are other spans and are not touched. The man behind the boatyard is not told about a rate by **a woman of forty-one** but by **a woman of sixty**, which is the person who holds that reason. `bible/characters.md` no longer credits this batch with a third refusal by Mara Quill: **she refuses twice on the page here — no fifth Thursday in any form, and no letting him be leaned on — and the Lark disclosure is a third refusal that belongs to Chapters 29–30 and is not in this batch at all.** Chapter 50 reads *in front of about eleven people*; the Chapter 47 summary no longer invents a time for the back of his hand, which the prose gives as *Eighth of the month, 09.25*; the doubled *Board school door gave him* in `state/continuity.md` is one *door*; Pell's *fifth floor* is **a floor**, because Cedar Court's landings on the page are the second, the third and the fourth. **And Doreen Sankey is explained rather than renamed**: Chapter 45 now says he is called Doreen after his mother, who kept a laundry on the Ferry Road side, that he has never changed it, and that for nine years the only place his name had been written down was a rent book — which is where the copy got it from, and which makes Chapter 44's *that is not a name he has given anybody, it is the name on his tenancy* the sharper line rather than a contradiction.

**Left alone deliberately.** Orrin's *you put it up yesterday* corrected to *this morning* is a deliberate correction beat and is already signposted by his own request that both facts go in the record. Rue's *that other sheet* is ambiguous and not wrong, and identifying it would have invented a fact. **The eleven weeks refrain itself is treated as an idiom, not a figure, and the state does not quote it as one.** The triad accounting, the six crossings, the eight lost routes, the five dated pairs at six, nine, three, three and two days with the unwritten sixth of one day, the count arithmetic, the two money totals including the inherited inconsistency at the shilling, the plate, the order, the name, the address/money trap, the nine school places with two still lost, Ada Fenn's absence, and the pinned-seam condition all stand as the review verified them.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0032.md`, `chapter-0042.md`, `chapter-0044.md`, `chapter-0045.md`, `chapter-0046.md`, `chapter-0047.md`, `chapter-0048.md`, `chapter-0050.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/canon-changes.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 32 and 41–50, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` (the instruction that produced the error, kept as the historical record), the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, and the phase ledger. **No new prompt was created: `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` already exists and was corrected in place, and it remains the only next phase.** The volume's word count moved with the repairs and is restated in `state/current.md` and the Batch 0005 summary: **170,729 across the volume, 29,859 in Batch 0005.**

## CR-026 — Volume 01 Batch 0006 wrote *The Bell and the Blank*: the pinned seam, the second-stage cost, two crossings, and the resolution, and it closes the volume

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006` (Chapters 51–60)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder, or planned ending is changed by this entry, and the ending image is the outline's.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-009 reserved the pinned-seam condition for Chapters 51–60 without saying what it would be called in prose; CR-012 reserved the written-line loss for the same chapters without saying what it would look like; and CR-018, CR-020 and CR-021 each recorded a batch that decided something on the page that a later batch could only inherit. Batch 0006 decided the three of them, and the decisions below are the ones a Volume 2 writer must inherit rather than guess.

**1. The pinned seam, as the prose defines it, and the one sentence that was written for Volume 2's benefit.** The word is in the Field Book on the sixth of December at about 21.20, at the foot of a page that already carries the day's times, under a line, **with no date under the line — the only undated entry in two hundred and eleven.** The definition and the limits are on the facing page, dated, in his own hand: **a seam held open instead of sealed, because a live continuity order keeps redirecting pressure into it; not closing while the order stands; its pressure travelling toward whatever the timetables, gates and records point at; quiet rather than healed; and it may produce a full seamquake first and then stop producing new ones, which is what makes it harder to live beside than a collapse.** It is **a condition and not a category** and the page says so in those words. It is **never written as three withdrawn and never as a thin remnant that grew, and the thin remnant at the western culvert is not mentioned anywhere in Chapters 51–60.** And because he wrote the word down before he knew whether it was true, the volume contains the counterweight in his own voice, and it is the sentence a Volume 2 writer should inherit first: *I would rather be wrong about this one. I have been right about the mechanism for eleven weeks and it has cost this district about four people and two school places and a man's own door, and being right is not the part of it that was useful.* **The word does not appear in this sense in Chapters 1–50; the four earlier occurrences are paper pinned to a board in Chapters 16, 24, 26 and 28, and no chapter was edited to remove or add anything.**

**2. The triad accounting is used once in the whole volume outside Chapter 30 — the chapter `outline/volume-01.md` fixes it in — and it is used in Chapter 55 in a Survey Enforcement officer's mouth as an argument about a document and not about a category.** *Your order took the name off the index in the first week and it is still off, and it closed the routes in the first week and they are still shut. Those are two things a document did… and in six years nobody in that department has ever once put a person on that reach… a thing a document does twice is a fact and a thing a document never does is an oversight, and an oversight is the only one of the three that can be corrected by a different department in a different building in about a year, and the other two cannot.* **Ardery then refuses the word *triad* in his own report, because he cannot define it, which means a Volume 2 batch that wants to use the term must either define it on the page or not use it.**

**3. The second stage of the orientation cost, as it now stands, and the ruling about not paying it twice.** The written line home went on **Monday the twenty-fourth of November at about ten past eight in the morning**, in Chapter 53, **without a crossing**, and it went because it went: the page is there, the hand is there, the ink on either side is the same ink, and the nine lines are clean, and Milo Rook writes down in his own hand that it is not fading and **does not write down what he thinks it is.** **It is separate from the remembered-route cost and the two are not collapsed**; Dr Sen's number is the argument and it is *eight remembered routes and there is a page, and they are not one thing.* **It is not persistent inside Volume 1: he can still find home when it is marked, and the marking comes from somebody who is not him — a nurse of twenty-three, a woman of sixty, and a boy of seventeen with chalk — and on the fifteenth of December it is still blank, twenty-two days in, and he has asked two people to write it for him and been refused twice by people who had no reason to refuse him.** **The ruling about the two crossings: the second-stage cost may not be paid twice for the same passage, and it is not, because it was already paid, without a crossing, on the twenty-fourth of November — so the seventh and eighth crossings of Chapters 56 and 57 each cost a first-stage remembered route instead, and the list is now ten with none of the original eight removed.** Persistence, and Mara Quill writing his return route, remain Volume 2 and are not pre-empted in a single line of the prose.

**4. The crossings, dated, placed, and costed.** **The seventh: Sunday the seventh of December, in at 16.06 and out at 16.35, twenty-nine minutes inside, from the low end of the reach; count held by Hettie Brann from the path head; walked in and out by Doreen Sankey; nine minutes stated; the four signed conditions of October all satisfied; the fourth, the clinic, satisfied.** **The eighth: Monday the eighth of December, in at 09.06 and out at 09.33, twenty-seven minutes inside; count held by Pell Harrow with a counter; walked by Mr Sankey; clinic at a quarter past eight by Ola Petrossian, who was not asked.** Both read out in a yard with Iona Sen's tally present, which now has eight lines on it, and she makes him say the number out loud. **Milo Rook is across eight times in Volume 1** and the counts are on Iona Sen's tally, which has eight lines on it, while the Field Book carries the two passages as dated time entries and no page of counting. **Nothing may be added to the eight in Volume 2 without the same four conditions, the same read-aloud, and a route that goes.**

**5. The Lark token, and what it was used for, which was nothing.** **It was cold on the seventeenth of November and on the fourteenth and fifteenth of December and did not come out. On the seventh of December, on the reach, it was warm for about four minutes and the negative space in the front of it lined up with a hundred and forty feet of made ground exactly, and he did not use it.** It opened nothing, summoned nobody, replaced no witness, and made no replica of the old lane, and the reason is written on the facing page and is the volume's whole argument about authorship: **a street that is open because of something in a man's coat is a place kept by that man, and then the three answers stop being three answers.** It is not a key, not an anchor, and not a promise about Neris, and **no later batch may treat the warm moment as evidence of anything except a man being offered the thing he wants most.**

**6. The resolution's mechanics, and the address/money trap paid in a room.** **A tenancy needs a street and there is no street; a temporary registration needs a head of household; the register's answer is a floor and a file number and nobody said the name and nobody woke him and no clerk noticed anything; and the third way is a temporary accommodation household with a named head who is a rateable person, entered on a per-head additional schedule and not on the street index, not on a rate book and not as a rateable place, so the compensation is untouched, and the seven school places come in on a Continuity referral in the same signature.** The consequence was stated aloud by the department on the fifteenth of December: **two and four a head a week, charged to the named head and not to the district rate, for every person entered, week by week, in advance, with no term, ended only by the household ceasing to exist or by the district producing a rateable basis for a block that has no street.** **Lena Marr signed it at about ten to twelve, after writing both her questions down and showing them to a woman of sixty-three on a step. It is not curable. It is the only money anybody pays in this volume, and it is paid by a person, in a room, for a rate on a building that has no number.** The count bill is closed the same day and the same way: **a certified head count of two hundred and fifty-two instead of a schedule of rooms, the twenty-one short recovered, the thirty-three rooms still on no list, and no room in the district on a rate book because of anything that happened.** The two inherited money totals, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty and forty-three pounds and two, are carried forward **unchanged and the multiplication is still never shown**, because the two totals are inconsistent at the shilling and that is inherited from Chapter 38 and was not repaired.

**7. The clue chain, grown by one document and one mark, and the count that still holds.** **The reopened order file held a carbon relocation schedule, copied off an older survey book and not off a district register, four routes, three in the river core, the fourth naming a real and dated destination with no official address about an hour and twenty minutes away on an entirely ordinary road. It carries one mark, in one hand, and it is a question about the destination and not about the order, and it is not one of the two questions on the other carbon, and it is not a third pair — a pair is two marks in two hands on one page.** Advocate Quill filed it as an attachment to her objection, with four lines in her hand on the back. **Two documents still carry a pair of marks, not three; three references still exist and are references and not pairs; the twenty-first's certification is drawn where the two confirmed marks are pressed; Kest has not matched the strings; and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–60.** **Nothing in this batch joined the carbon to the copied turn sheet or to the copying tick, and Milo Rook wrote down twice that joining them would be him deciding that they are the same.**

**8. The volume's close, and it is the outline's image.** **Milo in the route room of the Civic Atlas Office with the Field Book open at a page whose nine lines have gone clean out of it in his own hand; three chalk arrows on his own building, one of them on a second-floor landing with no date on it and no one has ever asked who put it there; and across the city Mara Quill puts a nineteen-paragraph objection on a counter at about ten past three, with the carbon relocation schedule attached.** The protected route is real and is a hundred and forty feet with a name in a drainage book, and the official record is damaged, and the question has moved from one man's private grief to the public maintenance of Alderquay. **Milo is the protector of a route he cannot claim and he does not end the volume as its author, and his own last sentence says so.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0051.md` … `chapter-0060.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-01/volume-01-close/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 51–60, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` (the instruction that produced this batch, kept as the historical record), the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, and the phase ledger.

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The reversal, the triad accounting, the answer to the bells, the plate, the four Thursdays, the culvert state track, the count arithmetic, the two money totals, the school bill, the death record, the two lost places, Ada Fenn's absence, Owen Fitch's refusal, Tarin Kest's refusal, the relationship milestone, and the planned ending are all exactly as they were. **What is new is only the thing Chapters 51–60 was written to supply: a named condition, a second-stage cost, two crossings, a resolution, and a completed volume.**
