## CR-027 — Review repair of Batch 0006 (Chapters 51–60): a spliced chapter, ten sentences that broke the fiction, and a state layer that had drifted from the manuscript in twenty places

The Batch 0006 review found the same defect class CR-019, CR-021, CR-023 and CR-025 exist to record, in its worst form yet, and this entry fixes it and canonises the three facts the prose had invented without registering them: **Bridge Reach**, **the month of the closure**, and **the date of Ardery's classification**. **No planned beat, no reversal, no resolution, no relationship step and nothing in the planned ending is touched.**

**1. Chapter 58 was structurally broken and is repaired.** Three consecutive paragraphs had been spliced: a time-and-place lead-in had been run into the middle of a reflection, a `**` emphasis marker had been opened and never closed, a second had re-opened mid-line, and the third line had ended on an orphan `**`. The reflection now stands as its own paragraph, the Field Book entry it reports now has a date the content can support (**the evening of the eighth of December, in a yard, with the second half added on the morning of the twelfth**), the Sable paragraph now reads as one continuous scene in the Crown Heights room that the following paragraph refers back to as *the room*, and the count is now **three sentences out of the ten he set himself** — which is what Sable says in his own mouth, what the narrator's line before it requires, and what *and the third*, said Rue, needs. **The bell silence is six days, from the thirteenth to the eighteenth**, which is what the six days enumerated in the next paragraph actually are, with the ask on the Friday the nineteenth and none on the twentieth. The earlier *eight days* and the earlier *nine days* in the 23.10 entry are gone, and no state file may print either again.

**2. Ten sentences of meta language are out of the prose.** Nine of them are in Chapters 51–60: the batch named itself, the chapter, the volume and *the outline* — including one sentence that made a fictional Prefect's paragraph the fourth paragraph of a document that has six. The tenth was in Chapter 44, written in Batch 0005 and missed by that batch's review, and it was found by running the same grep over the whole volume rather than over the batch. Every one is rewritten as something a narrator inside the book could know: the batch is the district, the chapter is the afternoon, the volume is the case, and the outline is a six-paragraph letter. **No batch after this may name its own structure in the prose, and the review for the volume-close phase should grep for it first.**

**3. Ada Fenn's referred gender is fixed and her absence is now recorded truthfully.** She was twice *a man of sixty-eight*, once in a sentence that contradicted itself four words later. She is **a woman of sixty-eight** in every one of the four places she is referred to, and the state layer no longer claims she is referred to once, or that the reference was not Milo's. **The truth is: four references in Chapters 51–60, never by name, always as *a woman of sixty-eight* or *a name of sixty-eight*, of which two are inside Milo's own Field Book.** That is inside the prohibition — she is not thanked, argued back, put on anything, or solved — and it is not a licence for a later batch to add a fifth. **The prohibition is a standing note and not a problem to be solved.**

**4. Three dates that contradicted themselves, corrected at the source and not in the summary.** The instruction *a seal* was carried *since the fourth of Sazur* in one sentence and *since the fourth of October* in another, and nine weeks supports October only. The classification Ardery withdraws is **the eleventh of last month** — the eleventh of November — which is what *I wrote it on the eleventh* means in his own mouth on the seventh of December, and the Prefect's letter's *classification of the site dated the eleventh of the current year* is the same document. Dr Sen's number is given on **the Tuesday of the twenty-fifth of November**, the day after the written line went, which is what the state layer had always said and what the chapter did not.

**5. Three unregistered inventions are now canon.** **`Bridge Reach`** — a hundred and forty feet of frontage between the pump house and the low end, in the district's 1904 drainage book in a hand four inches high, a *maintained frontage* since before Tomas Rusk was at the pump house, and from the ninth of December unstruck, on the works maintenance schedule with a gang and a fortnightly inspection, and on a tram diversion panel as *Bridge Reach (works frontage)*. It appears nowhere in Chapters 1–50 and a later batch may not rename it, shorten it, or give it an address. **The closure is in the first week of June, six years back**, and the crew came off the frontage to the pump house on the second of that June; no state file had ever recorded a month for the order, and this is the month. **The fourth Thursday instrument expired at noon on Friday the fourteenth of November** and is not renewed, converted or minuted anywhere in Chapters 51–60.

**6. The state layer is corrected against the manuscript, item by item, and the classes are named so the volume-close phase can check them.** **Chapter count: sixty planned chapters in six written batches, not fifty in ten.** **The bells are used on six days, not five** — the twenty-eighth of November, the sixth, seventh, eighth, twelfth and nineteenth — and the sentence that used to say *five doors* while listing six is gone. **Jo Lask's guarantee has three terms and not four**, in the chapter, the summary, the continuity list and the bible. **The arrow went down on Saturday the sixth, not the fifth**, which is what *Saturday night* and the Sunday morning it was reported both mean. **The first gang on the reach on the sixth was about eleven of ours, not nine.** **The fence is about four feet high with a hole in it about eleven feet along, and there has never been an eleven-foot hole.** **Milo is across eight times; the counts are on Iona Sen's tally, which has eight lines, and the two passages are dated time entries in the book — there is no page four or page five of a counting anywhere and the phrase is not to be reintroduced.** **The triad accounting is used once, at Chapter 55, and the chapter that fixes it is Chapter 30, not Chapter 32** — `outline/volume-01.md` says so under *Midpoint reversal — Chapter 30* and the state layer had been deferring to a chapter number the manuscript never used. **The Continuity referral is signed in Lena Marr's own hand**, in the same minute and with the same signature that puts the levy on her, which is the whole point of it; Advocate Quill's certificate is the head count and nothing else. **Mara Quill's objection is lodged at 15.10 on the fifteenth**, once, and the cross-cut to the counter is the same hour, so the manuscript no longer files one document at two times. **The written line home has been blank twenty-two days at the last entry, not nine.** **Owen Fitch's fourth position has its record**: the line that went into the record of the seventh carries no figure at all, Rue has written down in her own book that he asked for a number, was not given one, and was not going to be given one, and **nobody explained his position to him** — which is the promise of Chapter 52 kept in the only way the batch's rules allow.

**7. Two date reconciliations inside Chapter 57, made because the chapter contradicted itself and not because a reviewer was watching.** **The count for the eighth crossing is read out on the fifteenth, at 19.40, in a yard of about eleven** — because the hall was that afternoon and because Dr Sen says *you told me about it at about eleven o'clock tonight*; it was read out on the eighth in an earlier state of the chapter, which put a ninth-of-December marking into an eighth-of-December scene and put the hall a week early. **The sixth reading of a count in a yard is the one on the sixth of December and the seventh is the one on the fifteenth**, a week after the passage and on the day of the resolution, and both counts are in that yard and both are the ones the tally records. **The Field Book page carrying *A road is kept, not made* is the seventh, at 21.10**, because he was on the reach at about four o'clock that afternoon, because the hundred and one steps came to him at ten to four that same Sunday, and because the entry says *I am not going to take this book near the works compound on the ninth* — a date that has to be in the future. **The seventh pair in the Field Book is the ninth of December to the twelfth of December, which is three days**, and the entry no longer calls the ninth and the eleventh three days, which is two.

**8. One spelling, normalised.** **`Wragg Street`,** which is the spelling in Chapters 8, 19, 40, 42, 46, 49 and 58, against five uses of *Wragh* in Chapters 50, 53, 57 and 60. Every one is now *Wragg*, in the chapters and in the state layer, and no later batch may revive *Wragh*.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0051.md` … `chapter-0060.md`, and `chapter-0050.md` and `chapter-0044.md` outside the batch (the spelling and the one meta instance respectively), `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 50–60, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, every `PROMPT.md`, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, and the phase ledger.

**Plot impact: none.** The pinned seam, the two crossings, the four hundred answers, the four positions, Rusk's condition, Lask's guarantee, the tri-partite referent, the answer to the bells, the culvert state track, the address trap paid in a room, the death record, the two lost school places, the contested name, the material changes that stay, the blank on the page, and the relationship milestone are all exactly as Chapter 0006 left them. **What changed is that the manuscript is now internally consistent and the state layer agrees with it, which is the only thing this entry is for.**

**Left to the volume-close phase, on purpose, and named here so it is not lost:** Chapter 60 opens with five unnumbered recap paragraphs and then an eight-item bolded inventory, and repeats a carbon-copy inventory that Chapter 58 has already given at close to verbatim; Chapter 58's material-state paragraphs are in the same ledger register; the tenth route is recited three times near-verbatim; and *wrote* runs at roughly nine a chapter across the ten. **These are structural and are the volume-close phase's work, not a repair pass's, because the outline fixes Chapter 60's inventory shape and a fixer that rewrote it would be changing the plan.** The three one-line refrains that repeat verbatim across chapters — *Nobody said anything for about four seconds*, *Nobody said anything*, *Go on* — are left as they are: they are this book's deliberate flat beats and the count of a refrain is not a defect.

## CR-028 — Volume 01 close: the recitation is cut, the inventory's shape is put back, four numbers stop disagreeing with the manuscript, and one reviewer finding is refused on the evidence

The volume-close review found the CR-027 hand-off only half discharged and, in the prose the close pass had already touched, four fresh contradictions. **The class is the one CR-023 exists to record and it is now in its eighth form: a state figure written after the prose and read back as authority.** Everything below is made from the chapters. **No planned beat, no reversal, no resolution, no relationship step, no power rule, no antagonist and nothing in the planned ending is touched.**

**1. Chapter 60's inventory shape is restored, because the outline fixes it and the close pass had changed it.** The volume outline resolves the case with four things and then, separately, a name: *a temporary route, a temporary stay against demolition and forced relocation, access to their housing and compensation files, and a public hearing on the order*, and *Bellwether's public name is restored only as a contested community and memorial name*. The first close pass ran four bolded paragraphs into two and welded the fifth item onto the Bridge Reach paragraph, which is a change of plan and not a compression. **The five are five paragraphs again — a supervised passage, a temporary stay, access, a hearing, and a name** — with a lead-in that says where the page came from, and the paragraph on the things that changed and are not going to change back is untouched. Two clauses the compression dropped are back: **the reporting officer on the investigation is to be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order** (which is the fourth paragraph of the Prefect's letter, quoted in Chapter 58, and is now stated as what it is), and **Advocate Quill's answer of the fourth of December is on the file with her name and her own date under it, and the box is still empty and it is going to stay empty.**

**2. The recitation is cut rather than moved, which is what CR-027 asked for and what the first close pass did not do.** The longest verbatim run between consecutive chapters in Chapters 51–60 was **137 words**, Chapter 58 to Chapter 60, and there were four runs over 60 words from Chapter 59 into Chapter 60 alone. **There is now one run over 40 words between any two chapters in 51–60, and it is the nine lines of the written route home, which are the same nine lines in Chapter 53 and are the object of Chapter 60.** The cuts: Chapter 58 loses the rail-in-a-skip clause and the quotation of the culvert form, because Chapter 60's fixed inventory carries the material state and Chapter 58 was reciting it a chapter early; Chapter 57's yard reading of the eighth crossing refers to the tenth route instead of reciting it a second time in the same chapter; Chapter 59's objection paragraph is split at the seam so that the thesis sentence stands alone as a deliberate one-line beat, and the clause about a store that does not open on Sundays is out, because a store's Sunday hours have nothing to do with why a file came out on the Saturday; **Chapter 60's attachment paragraph no longer re-describes the fourth destination in ninety words, and no longer re-quotes the definition of a pair of marks.** Every fact the cuts removed is still on the page in the chapter that owns it, and every figure in the state layer remains quotable.

**3. Four numbers that contradicted themselves are corrected at the source.** **Chapter 57's Field Book said *eleven hundred yards* of the reach had gone out of true; Bridge Reach is a hundred and forty feet and the movement is eleven inches over forty feet, and the sentence now says a hundred and forty feet.** It also said the movement was about a quarter of an hour before the form that sent the crew off, which put it at 13:51 and one minute *before* the call; **it is about six minutes before, which is about two o'clock, which is what Chapter 54 says in its own words.** **Chapter 54's aperture line said *sixteen minutes after the check went out* and the check is the call that asked at eight minutes to two, so it is fourteen minutes, and the sentence now names the call and states the number.** **Chapter 53 said Rennick was brought back for five days and then that a man *is paid for six more days than the window needs*; the window the reading needed was the second to the fifth, the extra day is the first, and the clause about signing on the first *out of nerves* is gone, because the narrator has no source for a foreman's motive.** And **Chapter 58's sentence about the third call answering it had lost the name**: Lena Marr is named again, because the sentence needs her and because she is the reason the reader knows which door.

**4. One age is out of a Field Book entry, and it was the only one.** Chapter 53's *four attempts* passage called the woman on the fourth floor **a woman of sixty**. She is **a woman of sixty-eight**, in her own mouth, on the seventh of December, in Chapter 57, and the phrase *a woman of sixty* belongs to the second landing and to nobody else in the volume. **The age is cut from Chapter 53 rather than harmonised, so that no second value is invented.**

**5. The Ada Fenn reference count is restated, in CR-027's own file and in every place that quoted it.** CR-027 said *four references, always as a woman of sixty-eight or a name of sixty-eight*. **The truth is five places across four chapters and seven age tags: Chapter 54 twice in one paragraph, Chapter 57 in an unknown man's mouth and then twice in Lena Marr's, and once each inside Milo's own Field Book in Chapters 59 and 60.** Two of the seven are his own sentences, which CR-027 already said is on the page and is not a reason to remove them. **There is an eighth sixty-eight tag in the volume and it is not Ada Fenn: it is the woman on the fourth floor, in her own mouth, and she is a different resident of the same building.** Corrected in `state/continuity.md` item 19, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and `state/character-state.md`, and registered in `bible/terminology.md`. **CR-027's sentence is left standing as the historical record and is superseded by this one; a reader of CR-027 alone is wrong by one, and this entry is the authority.**

**6. Reviewer finding 10 is refused, and the manuscript is right.** The review reported a load-bearing contradiction: *Ada Fenn is the woman on the fourth floor in Chapters 40, 48, 49, 50, 53, 54 and 57, and a woman on a third floor at 54:49 and 57:63 and 57:69, and three flights of stairs supports fourth*, and proposed moving her a floor. **It does not, and nothing in Chapters 1–60 is changed for it.** *The woman on the fourth floor* is a **different, unnamed resident of Cedar Court** — she is Milo's own floor, she came down on the tenth of the month and again on the seventh, she walked the frontage for ninety minutes, and in Chapter 48 she is distinguished from *the woman of sixty* in the same sentence. *Ada Fenn* is **on the third floor**, and the manuscript says so four times in its own voice: Chapter 18, *the third floor took forty minutes, because Ada Fenn did it her way and at her speed and would not be hurried*; Chapter 30, *the third floor of that building has got four people on it and two of them are over seventy* and *nobody has ever walked up three flights of stairs to ask an old woman whether she would mind stopping*; Chapter 57, *then go and ask the old woman on the third floor* and *there is a person on the third floor of that building who has been answering for the whole of it every evening*; and Chapter 12, where she says *he is asleep on the third floor*. **Three flights of stairs is the third floor, not the fourth.** Owen Fitch is on the third floor too and is a different man, and Chapter 50's *Owen Fitch / third floor* is his own record and not Ada's. `state/continuity.md` items 18 and 19 and `state/character-state.md` already had this right. **This is recorded because the failure mode is the one this repository keeps producing: a phrase in a state file, read as a claim about a person, and then used to correct the manuscript. The rule is CR-023's and it cuts both ways — the chapter wins, and so does the chapter.**

**7. Word counts, made from `wc -w` on the chapter files after this repair.** **Volume 01 is 217,132 words and Batch 0006 is 46,404** (4,137 / 5,680 / 5,362 / 3,373 / 5,205 / 3,600 / 5,546 / 4,406 / 4,010 / 5,085), and the six batch figures are 38,781 / 34,158 / 34,511 / 33,420 / **29,858** / 46,404. Three different figures were in circulation before this entry — 217,384 in `state/current.md` and 217,388 two lines below it, 46,487 by `wc -w`, and a per-chapter list in `state/batch-summaries.md` that matched nothing — and one of them, Batch 0005 at 29,859, was a year old and wrong by one. **All four are now the same four numbers, and they are quotable.**

**8. One prose instruction carried out in part, and the rest is a standing note.** *Wrote, written and writes* ran at eighteen to thirty-three a chapter across the ten and the first close pass did not touch it. **It is down in Chapters 53, 57 and 60** and unchanged in the rest, because in Chapters 52 and 55 the density **is** the subject: a surveyor who has written a rule at the foot of a sheet, a mechanic who has asked for a sentence in writing, and a chapter that ends on *it is not a restoration*. The count is not a target. Nothing else in the review's prose list was found: no duplicated paragraph has been added, no meta language, no outline-shaped scene, no sentence cut in the middle.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0053.md`, `chapter-0054.md`, `chapter-0057.md`, `chapter-0058.md`, `chapter-0059.md`, `chapter-0060.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter outside 53–60, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` and `workspace/volume-01/volume-01-close/PROMPT.md` (the instructions that produced this work, kept as the historical record), CR-027 and every earlier CR entry, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, and the phase ledger.

**Plot impact: none.** The pinned seam, the two crossings, the four hundred answers, the four conditions and the contested name, Rusk's condition, Lask's guarantee, the tri-partite referent, the answer to the bells, the culvert state track, the address trap paid in a room, the death record, the two lost school places, the contested name, the material changes that stay, the blank on the page, the relationship milestone, and the planned ending are all exactly as Chapter 0006 and CR-027 left them. **Chapter 60 still ends with a real protected route, a damaged official record, and a question that has moved from Milo's private grief to the public maintenance of Alderquay, and he still does not end the volume as the author of the route and he is not cured.**

## CR-029 — Volume 02 Batch 0001 wrote *A Car That Is Not Late*: the missing tram, the back length, the ninth crossing, the return route, the record that cannot be made, and the Stage 2 cost's first two instances

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0001` (Chapters 61–70)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, and the ten-volume structure are untouched. One world rule and one power fact are added below, and the additions are additions and not changes: nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted, and no planned beat is re-decided.**

**Why this entry exists.** Four of the things this batch fixed on the page were not in the bible and could not have been guessed from the outline, and three of them are the kind of fact a later batch would either contradict by accident or mistake for something it is free to change. They are registered here once. The whole of the batch's prose facts, item by item and quotable from a chapter, is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 61–70 fixed on the page," and this entry does not repeat that list.

**1. A length is a third kind of place, and it is not a remnant.** This is the addition, and `bible/power-system.md` and `bible/world.md` have both been extended. *A road is a place and a crew keeps a road. A gap is a place where a road stopped being a place and a crew does not keep a gap. **And that length out there is not a road and it is not a gap, and that is why it has got a car on it. It is the third thing and nobody has a word for the third thing because nobody has ever needed one.*** A **maintenance length** is a piece of railway that exists, is inspected, is walked by a named gang on named days, and is shown on the running diagram as a maintenance length only with no booked service. It has lost the name and the route **on paper** and kept the witness in practice, and **the practical consequence is that a length is somewhere a tram can go that the map has not got, and it remains a distinct kind of place for exactly as long as the people who walk it keep walking it.** **A length is not a remnant type, is not a thin remnant, is not a new failure state, and is not a form of any of the seven grammars; it is a kind of place, and the triad still governs it.** It cannot be sealed or pinned, because there is no edge on it to seal. A later batch may not convert it into a remnant, may not give it a new failure condition, and may not use it as a new category in a report.

**2. A standing instruction of the office of the Prefect can amend a public map through a maintenance schedule, and the reference number is 11/D/6.** `bible/world.md` and `bible/terminology.md` carry this. **Service amendment 12/44, effective Monday the thirteenth of October, removed about six hundred yards of public tram route from the running diagram, and therefore from the Civic Atlas Office's public sheet, the district street index and five public route readers, in the same week, on a justification that is a filing number and not a reason, with the *requested by* and *instructing officer* lines both blank.** The cited document, the third edition of the quay section maintenance schedule dated the eleventh of September, **did not contain the length it was justifying; the fourth edition, dated the twenty-second of November, does, with a margin note referring back to the amendment.** Nobody lied and there is no man to charge. **The same reference, 11/D/6, is on the works' notice of the sixteenth of November, and the district continuity order of six years back cites a standing instruction of the same office without giving the number.** **Three documents, one reference number, and a count of three is a count of three: no batch may convert it into a pattern, a pair, or a fourth instance.**

**3. The Stage 2 cost's first two instances, and their exact form, and what they are not.** `bible/power-system.md` has been extended. **The onset is now on the page: a sound he needed and could not call up (the pitch of the third gong of a Line 3 morning car, four seconds into a gong on the back length) and a taste he did not need and could not remember (tea made at five in the morning on the twenty-third).** **Neither is a face, a place, a person, a name, or a relationship dropping out of an autobiographical route, and the first personal blank may not appear before Stage 3.** He has written both in the order they happened rather than the order they frighten him, and has decided nothing about whether it is one thing or two. **Also fixed: the ninth crossing cost no first-stage remembered route, the reason is unknown, and the list stands at ten with the original eight intact and none removed; and the written line home, recoverable when marked throughout Volume 01, is not recoverable when marked and has not been since the twenty-fourth of November, which makes the orientation blank persistent inside the volume.** Atlas residue was used once in ten chapters, in five words.

**4. Milo's first stable field map, and what "stable" was allowed to mean.** Registered in `bible/power-system.md`. It is three feet of accountancy paper headed with **who keeps what and on which day**, in which the frame, the heading and the columns are his and **every line with anything in it is in another person's hand with a date against it**, and the corner reads *content not mine, do not correct it, ask the person whose hand it is*, and the rule at the foot says that if any of them stops, cross the line out, date it, **do not take the line off, and do not ask anybody to start again.** **It is stable because it will still be right next week if the people change on Tuesday, and it is a method change and not a new capability.** A later batch may not describe it as a power, and may not have a later map simply assert a place and hold it.

**5. The two things a later batch is most likely to get wrong about this batch, both of which are figures, both of which are quotable, and both of which the state layer has already had to correct once in this run before it was written.** **First: thirty-one and thirty-three are two numbers and not one.** Thirty-one passengers on the manifest; the fare register counted 31 fares plus the conductor's pass plus the driver's pass; **thirty-three people on the car.** The alighting arithmetic is **twelve at the Alder Quay interchange, nine at the water stairs, ten at Crown Heights**, and the thirty-first name is read out twice and struck through twice in the depot clerk's own hand. **Second: the weekday chain is extended, not re-derived — the amendment is effective Monday the thirteenth of October, the routing slip is dated Friday the tenth, the twelfth of October is a Sunday, and Volume 02 Batch 0001 runs from Tuesday the sixteenth of December to Sunday the twenty-eighth, with the first of January a Thursday.** A review pass found three of these figures wrong in the prose itself before the state layer was written and corrected them at the chapter, per CR-023, and the corrections are in the chapters and not in a summary.

**Plot impact, stated plainly, because that is the point of this entry.** The reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the lost Atlas record, the relationship milestone, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the four-Thursday instrument's expiry, the culvert's material state, the count arithmetic, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the hearing that has not been set, and the planned ending are all exactly as Volume 01 and `outline/series.md` left them. **What is new is the thing Chapters 61–70 was written to supply: a third kind of place, a municipal machine that removes a road with a filing number, a cartographer whose first stable map is a map of practices, a persistent orientation cost, an advocate who has copied a record and is not going to use it, and a forecast for eleven weeks of weather from an office that entered one region of the city as stable because nothing had ever been written down in it.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0070.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/world.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter in `chapters/volume-01/`, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` and `workspace/volume-01/volume-01-close/PROMPT.md` (the instructions that produced Volume 01, kept as the historical record), CR-001 through CR-028, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, and the phase ledger.

## CR-030 — Review repair of Volume 02 Batch 0001 (Chapters 61–70): a chapter that broke the weekday chain four times, two men welded into one, six figures that did not match the manuscript, and a state layer that had copied four of the prose errors

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the fixer, from the review of Volume 02 Batch 0001, `logs/volume-02-batch-0001.review.log` (the log is gitignored, so this entry cites the pass, not a retained file)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **No planned beat, no reversal, no resolution, no relationship step, no power rule, no antagonist, no calendar anchor and nothing in the planned ending is changed.** The batch is not restarted, no chapter is rewritten, and no scene is cut or added; every change is a sentence, a figure or a state line.

**Why this entry exists.** The review of this batch found eight blockers, of which the class is the one CR-023 exists to record: **a figure written after the prose and read back as authority, in both directions.** In four of the eight the state layer had copied the prose error instead of catching it, which is why this pass read the chapters first and wrote the state second. **The chain that makes the repair checkable is CR-029 §5's own: 1 January is a Thursday, therefore 31 December is a Wednesday, therefore the twenty-seventh is a Saturday and the twenty-eighth a Sunday — and Chapter 70, the last chapter of the batch, said Friday twice and Sunday once in the same three paragraphs.**

**1. Chapter 70's calendar is repaired at the source, and the sheet boundary the review flagged is closed.** The gang is on the back length **on Mondays and Thursdays** — Pryce's own mouth in Chapter 62, the Field Book the same night, and the Chapter 66 map all say so, and Chapter 69's record of the ninth crossing carries a 07.30 and an 11.06 on a Monday. So in the week of the twenty-first the second walk is the **Thursday the twenty-fifth**, and the chapter's "Thursday the twenty-fourth" was a Wednesday. The sheet for the week of the twenty-first went into the rack on **the Friday the twenty-sixth** and not on a Saturday, and the courier who asks for the second tin comes in at a quarter to eleven **on that same Friday**, with Dilley Farr signing for the tin the same afternoon. The next walk is **"on the Thursday of the week after this one"**, which is the same fact without naming a date, so this batch still creates no calendar obligation for the block that opens in January. **The knock-on is closed in the prose and not in a note:** the next walk cannot be on the sheet for the week of the twenty-first, and the chapter now says it "will be on a sheet of its own, and that sheet goes into the same rack at the end of that week." The three marks on the written line home — a nurse on the Wednesday afternoon, a woman who keeps a round on the Thursday morning, a boy of seventeen on the Friday night — were already correct as a Wednesday–Thursday–Friday run and needed nothing, but the state layer had dated the boy to the twenty-seventh and is now the twenty-sixth.

**2. One more weekday slip the review did not find, in Chapter 67.** Tomas Rusk's sentence said the gang "still came on Tuesday" and, nine words later, "the gang has been on it every Monday and Thursday for four years." It is **a Monday morning** now, which also fits an amendment effective on Monday the thirteenth of October. No speech, no beat, no argument and no fact about anybody is altered.

**3. The route count is ten in Chapters 61 and 62, and the ninth crossing's sentence no longer claims a loss that never happened.** The two Field Book entries said eight remembered routes; the list has stood at ten since the ninth of December, and Chapters 68 and 70 both said ten, so the batch jumped eight to ten with nothing on the page acquiring the two routes. **The ledger was right and the two chapters were wrong, and the sentence in Chapter 68 was rebuilt rather than the ledger touched** — *I have gone eleven weeks expecting to lose one every time and nothing has come off the list* — because the ledger says the original eight are intact and none was ever removed, and one loss at each of eight crossings would be eight. **He has never lost one, and no later batch may say he did.** The same item's other half: the sentence in the imperative is **thirteen** words, and the later reference to it in the same chapter now names it as the imperative half of the two, so the two figures in one chapter no longer disagree.

**4. Chapter 65's inventory is two men again, and this is the entry a later writer most needs.** The flat inventory had welded old Mabb's floor to Owen Fitch's document problem into one man on the third floor, which contradicted `bible/terminology.md` (**Owen Fitch is on the fourth**), contradicted the chapter's own next paragraph, and — worst — **ratified the forgery**, because *third floor* is the thing somebody else filled into a space left empty on purpose in Chapter 50. The line now reads: a man on the fourth floor, not been on that street since the night of the closure, not been asked, **whose name and whose third floor are still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a contractor's head on it** — the floor is the paper's, in the inventory's own flat register, and nothing else changes. Mabb's line is now **a man asleep** on the third floor, not a woman, and it stays its own line. `state/character-state.md` had assigned the single sentence to both characters; the two Volume 02 entries are split and each now quotes its own line. **CR-028 §6's clause that "Owen Fitch is on the third floor too" is superseded by this entry and is left standing as the historical record** — it is the sentence that produced the error, CR-028 §6's own Ada Fenn ruling in that paragraph is unaffected, and the quotable lines are `bible/terminology.md` and Chapter 65 as repaired.

**5. Four figures in Chapters 63, 66 and 70, and a fifth the review did not find.** A stair of **thirty-four** steps and not forty-one, which is Amos Trench's years at the index desk, eleven lines away. No coffee sold within **a hundred and ten yards** of Saltmarket Row and not a hundred and forty, which is Bridge Reach's frontage. **One six and four threes** in the eight pairs, not two of each — and the chapter's own list is unchanged, so the sentence now counts it correctly. The imperative sentence is **thirteen words**. And in Chapter 66's three-men passage, **the man in the basement in Crown Heights has forty-one years in it and not thirty-one** — thirty-one is Dilley Farr's years in the route room and Ivy Marsden's years with the scale, and Amos Trench is forty-one in Chapter 63 twice, in `state/continuity.md` item 7, in `state/character-state.md` and in `bible/terminology.md`.

**6. The three medium findings, and the unflagged seam behind one of them.** "**And that is the batch's question**" — production vocabulary in the chapter's hinge paragraph, and the only such leak in 40,000-odd words — is now "the question this case opens on". "**On the morning of the afternoon of the seventeenth**" is "on the afternoon of the seventeenth", which is the afternoon of Wednesday the seventeenth at twenty past two. And the Chapter 61 yard briefing is placed at about eleven o'clock while Rue's speech has her holding her conclusion "since about two o'clock this afternoon", in one unbroken scene, with Milo's promise at line 115 that he will be told again "this afternoon" standing in front of it: **she has had it since about half past eight this morning and has not said it in this yard until now.** The beat is unchanged — she arrived at it before she said it and she sat on it — and the reviewer was right that the promise and the speech could not both stand.

**7. The coat in Chapter 63 is paid off, and a pronoun is fixed.** The chapter set up a man in a coat, said he had come in from a court and had not taken it off, said Milo noticed and did not know for about two hours what it meant, had him take it off slowly, and never returned to it. **The Field Book entry now says what the coat was**: he had it on because he had spent his morning in a building where he was somebody's witness, and that is why he had the answer ready before the question was asked and gave it in the wrong order on purpose, and Milo did not see any of it for about two hours. Nothing new is in the world, no character is added, and the refusal is still the refusal. **Bettina Sallow's coat on the stairs at ten to four is now her coat.**

**8. Word counts, made from `wc -w` on the chapter files after this repair.** **Batch 0001 is 40,749** and the ten chapters are 4,619 / 4,078 / 3,901 / 4,077 / 3,866 / 4,255 / 3,895 / 4,032 / 3,967 / 4,059. CR-029's 40,604 and its per-chapter list are the pre-repair figures, are left standing as the historical record, and **these are the quotable ones**; `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and `state/open-threads.md` now carry them. Volume 01 is untouched at 217,132 words across sixty chapters.

**9. One count was checked and deliberately left alone.** The reason in the hours column of the gang's sheet is called **nine words** in Chapter 69, in `state/continuity.md` item 26, in CR-026 and in the review, and the sentence as printed is longer than nine. **Nothing in this repair touches it**, for three reasons: it is canon-fenced in four places, it contradicts nothing inside the manuscript, and the review listed the protected arithmetic as intact. **It is recorded here so that a later batch does not quietly re-count it**, and if it is ever corrected it is corrected at the chapter in all four places at once, per CR-023.

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md`, `chapter-0062.md`, `chapter-0063.md`, `chapter-0065.md`, `chapter-0066.md`, `chapter-0067.md`, `chapter-0068.md`, `chapter-0070.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** Chapters 64 and 69 and every other chapter in the batch, every chapter in `chapters/volume-01/`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` (its calendar section was checked and is already correct, and it is the one place in the repository that had this right), CR-001 through CR-029, `state/phase-ledger.json`, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, and `AGENTS.md`.

**Plot impact: none.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing and its four conditions, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the hearing that is not set, the reporting officer who is not named, Cass Ardery's refusal, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column, the three clues unjoined, the Stage 2 cost's first two instances, the persistent written line, the relationship milestone, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-029 and the batch left them. **Chapter 70 still ends on the same last line, the batch still wins something and produces no document, and Milo Rook still does not end it as the author of anything.**

## Open items carried forward

- The phase ledger (`state/phase-ledger.json`) and any completion marker for `phase-000-bootstrap` and `phase-001-outline` are controller-owned. Writers and fixers must not mark a phase `done` or `blocked`.
- `bible/terminology.md` now carries a “Volume 01 local register” for cast and places introduced during volume planning. Later batches must reuse those names, roles, and wants instead of renaming or replacing them.
- An annotating hand is confirmed on two documents: the Chapter 1 timetable’s old-ink margin correction — a wet mark pressed onto a folded, damped sheet, so the ink is older than the paper although the press is not — and the Chapter 6 annex notation. The Chapter 10 carbon copy carries two unsigned marks in *different* inks, so at least one is a different author and Volume 1 does not settle which. The chain is registered in `outline/volume-01.md` and is not resolved in Volume 1. Later batches must carry it and must not name it as Soren without evidence, and must not treat the Chapter 10 pair as a confirmed third instance.

## CR-031 — Volume 02 Batch 0002 wrote *The First Of January*: the hearing on a register, the seventh column that goes in a tin, the box about persons, and the batch's answer that nobody is responsible for a length

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002` (Chapters 71–80)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, and the ten-volume structure are untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, or category was changed, and nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-029 recorded what Volume 02's opening block fixed on the page; CR-030 recorded the review repair of it. This batch fixed five things a later batch would either contradict by accident or mistake for something it is free to change, and **one of them is the first use on the page of a Stage 2 gain, which is the kind of fact that gets inflated the moment two writers have it.** The whole of the batch's prose facts, item by item and quotable from a chapter, is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 71–80 fixed on the page," and this entry does not repeat that list.

**1. The Stage 2 gain, used once, and the limits on it, so that it cannot be inflated.** `bible/power-system.md` has always said that Stage 2 can *stabilize a seam, redirect a remnant pressure, and protect a small group for a limited time.* **This batch is the first in which any of that is on the page, and it is a single holding, of one small pressure, at one place, for about ninety seconds, and it is a Keepsake remnant — a market of eleven habits — and not a seam, and not a street, and not anything anybody asked for.** *There was something to hold. It was not large and it was not pressing and it was not in the wrong, and it was about the size of a smell.* **He cannot tell whether his stopping and a man's hand coming off the stone were a quarter of a second apart or four minutes apart, because he was not counting, and has decided not to decide for about a fortnight whether he did anything.** **The binding limits, registered so that a later batch cannot widen them: one holding, one place, under two minutes, no effect on anybody who does not know it happened, not repeatable by a second person, and not schedulable — and a schedule is the only thing anybody in this district would want to do with it.** He **redirected no remnant pressure at all** in this batch, which is inside the cap. **The Stage 2 sensory cost recurred once, as a sound, and acquired no face, place, person or name.**

**2. A length is still a third kind of place, and the batch's addition to that is a fact about labour and not a rule.** The prohibitions CR-029 registered are restated and were honoured: **a length is not a remnant type, is not a thin remnant, is not a new failure state, is not a form of any of the seven grammars, cannot be sealed or pinned or stabilised as if it were a seam, and may not be merged with the Keepsake remnant on the other side of its wall.** The two things the batch added are both true on the page and neither creates a category: **the writing down is what lets a department find a length, and the walking is what makes it a place — two different jobs, done by about fourteen people, none of whom is paid for any of it** — and **a length is a distinct kind of place for exactly as long as the people who walk it keep walking it.** The batch's answer to *who maintains a length that has no name* is that **nobody does, and there is nobody, and that is why it is still a place** — which is a statement about the city's instruments and not a change to the power system.

**3. The hearing, set, held and adjourned, and the new named officer.** **The order is suspended and not discharged; the public name is not restored to the street name index; the reporting officer is Ivo Sarratt, Survey Enforcement, fifty-one, twelve years, and he is a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order, and nobody above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel has been added.** Cass Ardery remains second-in-command and remains not the reporting officer, for the reason he gave in writing in Volume 02 Batch 0001. **The adjournment is Tuesday the fourteenth of April, which is a departmental list day, and a batch may not move it and may not supply a different authority for it.** **The batch's permanent gain is a line with a reporting officer's name on it and not a department's, refusing any schedule of persons from this district in any proceeding while the answer of the fourth of December stands — bought with a document and paid for with twenty-six names that are in a Crown Heights file for good, and the price was said out loud in a hall by the woman who paid it before she paid it.** The hearing's three matters are the grounds, the classification of the eleventh made correctly on the day it was made and left withdrawn, and a sentence removed from a six-year-old report that **cannot be restored by anybody in this city**, because the withdrawal reference is held in the office of the First Surveyor and has been requested twice and acknowledged twice and not answered. **Volume 01 is not re-litigated and the hearing does not reopen a settled question.**

**4. The two documents of this batch that are permanent and are about people, and the two that are not.** **A page with a woman's name struck through it, undated and unreasoned, on the bottom shelf of a plan room in a building in Alderquay, unread by anybody and unreadable out, which went there because a foreman's rule in grease pencil about not taking a line off applies to every sheet in this district including the surveyor's own.** **An annual maintenance return with a fifth box headed *persons observed on the length during the year*, which is a good box, which is filled nil every January, and which will be filled in again in January and in every January after this one.** These are additions to the world, in the sense that they are now objects in it, and **they are not a new category, a new faction, a new antagonist, or a new failure condition.** The batch's own sentence for the first of them is *a box that wants to know why is the amendment*, and the sentence for the second is *a nil on a length is not a nil on a street*.

**5. The five figures a later batch is most likely to get wrong, all of them quotable and all of them made from the manuscript, per CR-023.** **First, the weekday chain, extended and not re-derived:** 28th Sun, 29th Mon, 30th Tue, 31st Wed, **1st Jan Thu**, 2nd Fri, 3rd Sat, 4th Sun, 5th Mon, 6th Tue, 7th Wed, 8th Thu, 9th Fri, 10th Sat, 11th Sun, 12th Mon, 13th Tue, 14th Wed, 15th Thu, 16th Fri, 17th Sat, 18th Sun, 19th Mon, 20th Tue, 21st Wed, 22nd Thu, **1st February Sun, 1st March Sun, 1st April Wed, 14th April Tue.** **Second, the day-counts.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November and **that is day one**, so Volume 02 Batch 0001's thirty-fifth day on the twenty-eighth of December is unchanged and is the anchor, and this batch's figures are **1 Jan 39, 5 Jan 43, 7 Jan 45, 8 Jan 46, 12 Jan 50, 13 Jan 51, 14 Jan 52, 16 Jan 54, 19 Jan 57, 21 Jan 59**, **of which seven are quotable from a Field Book entry in Chapters 71–80 — 39, 43, 45, 51, 52, 54 and 57 — and three (46, 50 and 59) are carried arithmetic with no count written in the chapter that owns the day, and a later batch may carry them and may not quote them as a chapter's words** — and **the Field Book's last residue line is the twentieth of December, so the silence in this batch is 12, 16, 18, 19, 23, 24 and 25 days at the first, fifth, seventh, eighth, twelfth, thirteenth and fourteenth of January, and only the fourteenth is written.** The writer's own pass found and corrected six classes of figure error in this batch's chapters before the state layer was written, **which is the class CR-023 exists to record and which has now happened in nine passes out of nine.** **Third, the ten dated pairs in the Field Book: one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, one eight and one twenty-five, plus a single-day interval from the tenth of December that is looked at and not written — the four threes are not collapsed, the twenty-five is the largest interval in the book, and all of them are joined to nothing.** **Fourth, the crossing count, which did not move: nine, eight of them in Volume 01 and the ninth on the twenty-second of December, and Milo Rook crossed nothing in Chapters 71–80 at his own decision, and the remembered-route list stands at ten and he has never lost one, and the ninth crossing's failure to cost a route is still unexplained.** **Fifth, two names: Ivo Sarratt is the reporting officer and is named in Chapter 75 only, and Ada Fenn is on the third floor of Cedar Court, does not appear in Chapters 71–80, is not named once in them, and this batch adds exactly one reference to her, in Chapter 80's Field Book, as *a woman of sixty-eight on nothing* — the fourth across four chapters of Volume 02, recorded as an addition in `state/character-state.md`, and Ada Fenn is not the woman on the fourth floor, who is a different and unnamed resident of the same building.**


**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page, the first stable field map, the returned names, the relationship milestone, the three unjoined clues, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the Stage 2 cost's first two instances, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the four-Thursday instrument's expiry, the culvert's material state, the count arithmetic, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-029 and CR-030 left them. **What is new is the thing Chapters 71–80 was written to supply: a hearing on a register, a permanent line with a name on it bought with twenty-six people, a seventh column in a tin, a return form with a box about persons, a version two held by a woman who is neither of the two people who made it, and the volume's standing answer that nobody is responsible for a length and that is why it is still a place.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0071.md` … `chapter-0080.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/world.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter in `chapters/volume-01/`, `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0070.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` (the instructions that produced this work, kept as the historical record), CR-001 through CR-030, `state/phase-ledger.json`, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, and `AGENTS.md`.

## CR-032 — Review repair of Volume 02 Batch 0002 (Chapters 71–80): one elapsed time that contradicted the chapter it came from, two dates that did not exist, three word counts, a pair-list that had become eleven items under a label that said ten, and a state layer that had copied the prose errors forward into the next batch's brief

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the reviewer on `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002` (Chapters 71–80), `logs/volume-02-batch-0002.review.log`
- **Status:** applied. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image and every premise, world rule and power rule are untouched. Nothing in `outline/series.md` (whose Volume 02 block at lines 214–222 is the volume outline for this batch), `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted, and **there is no `outline/volume-02.md` in this repository and none was invented**, no chapter was restarted, and no beat was removed.** The repair rule applied throughout is the batch's own: **where a claim and a chapter disagree, the chapter wins**, and where two chapters disagree, the earlier inherited chapter wins.

**Why this entry exists.** The reviewer found fourteen items. Twelve were real defects in quotable figures and in prose that had been written after the figures and read back as authority — the CR-023 failure mode, on its tenth pass — and **eight of them had already propagated out of the batch and into `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, which is the next writer's brief and was being read as authority.** That is the whole reason this pass was necessary rather than tidy: three of the batch's errors were about to become Chapter 81's canon. The reviewer also named eleven things that are right and were not touched, and they were not touched.

**1. The elapsed time on car 214, which is the one figure in the batch with a source chapter.** Chapter 69 has **Nessa Bray say "five days and two hours" five times in her own mouth**, and `state/character-state.md` and `bible/terminology.md` both carry the **07:52** departure and the **16.41** return. Chapters 71, 73 and 78 each invented a different departure — **half past seven, twenty to eight** — and a different figure, **five days and about eight hours**, **five days and eight hours**. **All three now read five days and two hours from seven fifty-two on the morning of the sixteenth of December**, which is the carbon, the run number and Chapter 80's own quotation. **A later batch may not substitute a different elapsed time or a different departure, and this repair does not re-derive Chapter 69's own arithmetic, which is Batch 0001's and is inherited as it stands.**

**2. Two elapsed-day figures for one date, inside one chapter.** Chapter 75 gave **7 January** as both **twenty-six days** after the suspension and **thirty-one days** after it. **The suspension is ten o'clock on Friday the twelfth of December, so twenty-six is the figure and thirty-one is now twenty-six.**

**3. A reply that arrived before the question was asked.** Chapter 79 had the query filled in on **Monday the nineteenth** and posted on **the Tuesday**, and the card coming back **on the Friday the sixteenth**. The chapter's last three scenes and its Field Book entry are all Monday the nineteenth, and Pell Harrow's return is the nineteenth, and neither can move. **So the query moves instead: filled in at about half past two on the Friday the ninth in the route room and into the internal post the same afternoon, the card back on the Friday the sixteenth, seven days later, and the drainage-section conversation in that Friday afternoon.** The Atlas Office's "did nothing at all" is now scoped to the four pages, which is what its own clause describes. **The finding is unchanged — a query asks a district office a question about a quarter and there is no district office and there is not a person to be rude** — and Chapter 80's schedule item now reads *a query written on the ninth, a card back on the sixteenth*.

**4. Twenty-two days and eleven days for one journey.** Chapter 79 said both. **Twenty-two days is the figure everywhere**, from the twenty-eighth of December to the nineteenth of January.

**5. Four weeks of carbon, and the seventh column in the tin.** Chapter 78 has four weeks as of Friday the sixteenth, which is correct, and Chapter 80 said three weeks on the twenty-first. **The week of the nineteenth is not signed off until the Thursday the twenty-second and does not reach the rack until the Friday the twenty-third, so on the twenty-first Chapter 80 must say four weeks of gang carbon and it now does**, and it agrees with `state/continuity.md` item 16 and with Chapter 78.

**6. Sunday the seventeenth, which is a Saturday, and a quoted document the state layer had altered.** The chain is **17th Sat, 18th Sun**, the document in Chapter 80 is dated **R.P., 18.i.**, and the narration said Sunday the seventeenth. **Chapter 80 now says Sunday the eighteenth**, which is what the document in it has always said, and **the six state files that had rewritten the quoted document to *R.P., 17.i.* and moved the weekday have been corrected back to the manuscript**: `state/continuity.md` item 26, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` (two places) and `state/current.md`. `state/continuity.md` opens that list by saying every figure in it is quotable from a chapter, and **a quoted document's date is not a figure a state layer may adjust.**

**7. Two word counts, both wrong, and the sixth line's date.** The condition summary was called **about nine words** and is **twelve**. The sixth line was called **nineteen words** and is **fifty-four**, and the chapter's subject is a true sentence surviving in a form, which is precisely the class of thing this manuscript does not get to be approximately right about. **Both counts are corrected in Chapter 78, in Chapter 80's Field Book, and in `state/continuity.md` item 24, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `bible/characters.md` and CR-031.** **And the sixth line's own date was wrong: the narration said the clerk wrote it on the Tuesday the thirteenth and the document is signed *15.i.*, which is a Thursday, in a chapter whose whole subject is a true sentence surviving in a form. The clerk wrote it on the Wednesday the fourteenth at about ten past ten and the document is signed *C. SALE, clerk, 14.i.*, and the foreman signs the same Wednesday at about eleven**, which is what the chapter already said about him.

**8. A foreman of sixty-one, which is the same error class and was not in the reviewer's list.** Chapter 78's reason for the nil said that in four years **a foreman of sixty-one** would be asked in a room what he observed. **Roy Pryce is fifty-four, everywhere, including in the same chapter and in the same speech.** Corrected in the chapter and in the two summaries that had copied it.

**9. Two eights, which is eleven items under a label that says ten.** Chapter 77 reads **one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, one eight, and now a twenty-five, and that is ten pairs**, and `state/continuity.md` item 21 agreed. **`state/current.md`, CR-031, `bible/power-system.md` and two places in the Batch 0003 prompt all read *two eights*. All now read one eight**, and the Batch 0003 brief carries a note that the earlier figure was wrong against Chapter 77, because a brief that silently changes a number is how the same error returns.

**10. Three day-counts that are arithmetic and not quotable, described as quotable.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November and is day one. **Seven of Batch 0002's ten day-counts are on the page — 39 in 71, 43 in 72, 45 in 73, 51 in 76, 52 in 77, 54 in 78, 57 in 79 — and three are carried arithmetic with no count written in the chapter that owns the day, which are 46 on the eighth, 50 on the twelfth and 59 on the twenty-first.** All ten are still carried forward, because the arithmetic is right and the next batch needs it, and **`state/current.md`, CR-031 and the Batch 0003 brief now say which is which**, and the brief's instruction not to quote the three as a chapter's words is the operative part.

**11. Chapter 80 narrated three weeks past the end of its own batch and pre-empted Batch 0003.** It said the run-number card was still under the brick **on the Wednesday the eleventh of February** and that a man had walked past it four times a day for three weeks without picking it up, in a batch that ends on the twenty-first, in a brief that makes the first week of February its own hinge. **Chapter 80 now stops inside its own batch: the card went under the brick on the Wednesday afternoon, it was still there on the Thursday morning, and nobody chased it. The plant is intact and untaken and the undertaking on the card is still *not before Friday*.**

**12. A rule on the facing page that was not on the facing page.** Chapter 80 referred to a reason written in eleven words on the facing page of the Field Book, and no such line existed. **It exists now, in the Field Book entry of Chapter 80, under the item it belongs to, and it is eleven words and they are these: *a card under a brick is a thing, not a job.*** The reference points at the entry, the count is eleven, and the sentence after it — *nobody chases it, everybody can read it* — is the same rule said again in the voice that says everything else in that book.

**13. The tally, which had no handover on the page.** Chapter 80 said Hettie Brann **holds** a tally with nine lines on it; Chapter 68 has Dr Sen writing the ninth line on it and putting it in her apron pocket; and `state/character-state.md` had it in **Hettie Brann's** apron pocket, which is wrong in the other direction. **All three now agree and the distinction is registered so it cannot drift: the paper is Hettie Brann's, a clinic counter form in her own hand since November, and it is Dr Sen's in her apron pocket, where it has been since the twenty-ninth of December.** **It is not handed over in this batch. The return-route holder and the tally holder are two different women in two different rooms.**

**14. Seven walks against six.** `state/current.md` listed seven walks in the batch; `state/continuity.md` item 3 and `state/character-state.md` said six, and **six is right, because the walk of the twenty-ninth of December is referred to in Chapter 71 and not depicted.** `state/current.md` now says seven sheet entries across four sheets and six depicted walks, which also keeps the four-weeks-of-carbon count in item 5 honest.

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page, the hearing and its adjournment to the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the custody refusal that is never confessed to, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the Stage 2 cost's two instances, the one stabilisation, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the run number and the card under the brick, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031 and the manuscript left them. **The run number is still not looked up. The card is still under the brick. The volume's final image is still unwritten and was not this pass's to write.**

**Untouched, on the reviewer's own list of what is right:** Ivy Marsden at seventy-four with thirty-one years; Chapter 72's three refusals and the fourth answer; Lena Marr's paragraph-three answer; Hester Coombe's half-filled fault box; Dilley Farr's *a box that wants to know why is the amendment*; Milo ending the batch as the author of nothing; the recovery being Rue's column and the down-loop man's first line; the residue line, its eight words, its single appearance and the four threes; the fourteenth of April; Sarratt as reporting officer; and Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable, Tarin Kest, Amos Trench, Bettina Sallow and Soren Rook all absent and unnamed.

**Files updated across all three passes of this repair:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0071.md`, `chapter-0072.md`, `chapter-0073.md`, `chapter-0074.md`, `chapter-0075.md`, `chapter-0076.md`, `chapter-0078.md`, `chapter-0079.md`, `chapter-0080.md`, **and not `chapter-0077.md`, which was already right on the only figure it was cited for and was left alone**; `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter in `chapters/volume-01/` and `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0070.md`, `outline/series.md` (including the Volume 02 block this batch was written against), `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, CR-001 through CR-031 except for the two figure corrections inside CR-031 named above, `state/phase-ledger.json`, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, and `AGENTS.md`.

**No next phase was created.** The single next phase for this batch, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, already exists and is the write phase for Chapters 81–90; this pass corrected its brief rather than adding to it, and dispatching is the controller's action.

**Addendum to item 13, on the same pass.** The tally's ownership was wrong in **two** places and not one, and one of them is older than this batch. `state/character-state.md` had it in **Hettie Brann's** apron pocket in the **Volume 02 Batch 0001** section as well as in the Batch 0002 section, and **Chapter 68 puts it in Dr Sen's apron pocket on the twenty-ninth of December, in the middle of Batch 0001 itself.** Both entries now read as Chapter 80 and Chapter 68 read: **the paper is Hettie Brann's, in her own hand on the back of a form, and the pocket is Dr Sen's.** No chapter was touched to achieve it and Volume 01 is not re-opened, because Chapter 56 already establishes that the tally was Hettie's hand and the pocket was never the point.

**Addendum to items 1–14, on the second pass of the same repair.** A verification pass over the repaired batch returned the fourteen findings above as twelve resolved and two partial, and turned up eight further defects, five of them pre-existing and of the same class as the fourteen. **They are repaired here, and they are recorded because a repair pass that fixes what it was given and leaves the rest of the class standing has not fixed the class.**

**15. The batch's spine was three days out of order, and it is the reviewer's most serious finding.** In Chapter 76 the form was written **on the Tuesday the thirteenth** and the six lines in its seventh column were said to have been written **on the Saturday morning** three days earlier, in a chapter whose Field Book says *I have had three days* and whose doctor says *on Saturday you filled it in* — **so the content of the column existed before the page and the column it was written on, and the whole of the batch's cost rested on a document that could not exist.** The chapter's own state layer already headed the beat **Sat 10th, 10.30**, and the repair takes the Saturday as the true day: **the form is written on Saturday the tenth at about ten past ten in the route room, carried to the north end of Saltmarket Row in the inside pocket of a coat, and the six lines are written there at about half past ten.** The six gang columns are copied off a weekly sheet **he had looked at on Monday the fifth**, which is the Monday he was in that office in Chapter 72, and not the Monday of the hearing. **And the form then lies face up on the second table under the north window for three days, which is why Dilley Farr finds it at about eleven on the Tuesday — a document left where about nine people a day walk past it, which is the district's own argument and is now made by the object rather than by the narration.** Propagated to `state/continuity.md` item 15, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `state/current.md`. **The beat is untouched: it is still his, it still took four seconds, the strike is still undated and unreasoned, the name is still legible under the line, and the page is still in the tin on the Tuesday at about a quarter past nine.**

**16. Two more counts written after the list.** Chapter 80's schedule is **eleven** blockquoted items and the chapter says **nine** in three places; the state layer had inherited nine in four files. **All now eleven**, and `state/current.md` records that this was the third count in the batch written after the list rather than read off it. And the Lark token: **the twenty-fifth day since the seventh of December is the first of January, which is what Chapter 71 says, and the twenty-first of January is the forty-fifth day** — `state/current.md`, `state/character-state.md` and the Batch 0003 brief all said twenty-five at the end of the batch, and all now say forty-five with the two distinguished.

**17. Two figures that are not the figure.** Chapter 80 said **the eleventh** seam event was in a week and one day, when the eleventh of eleven runs to the fourteenth of April; it is **the first of the eleven**. And the interval to that first event was stated two ways in one chapter — *nine days* in Dilley Farr's mouth and *a week and one day* in the Field Book — and is now **a week and one day** in both. **The separate nine-day interval in Chapter 79, which is the nine days the three people acted in, is a different interval and is unchanged.**

**18. A quoted document, a rule, and a weekday.** Chapter 80 called the foreman's note **seven lines** and set it as four; **it is now set as seven lines**, which is what the chapter, Chapter 80's Field Book, the beat list and four state files have always said. The **eleven-word rule** Chapter 80 pointed at did not exist and now does, in the Field Book entry, under the item it belongs to, and it is eleven words: *a card under a brick is a thing, not a job.* And **"Sunday the seventeenth" is a Saturday**, corrected in chapter and state together, as item 6 records.

**19. A chapter that broke the fiction.** Chapter 80's fourth paragraph referred to **"Chapters 41 to 50 of this district's other life"** — a structural reference inside the prose, which `AGENTS.md` does not allow. It now says the failure happened **twice** in that other life and was not caught either time and is still on the paper, which is the same fact and is a sentence.

**20. Three small clock errors.** Chapter 79 had a boy of seventeen **on the round** at five o'clock when the round is in at four — he is **in the yard**. Chapter 78 had the annual return coming back from head office **on the Wednesday** while the clerk read and filled it on the Monday — it came out on **the Friday the ninth** and was on the section's desk on the Monday. And Chapter 76 put the plan-room drawer at **twenty past three**, before the clinic scene at four, and it is now **half past five**, between them. Three headers in `state/batch-summaries.md` that put Chapter 78's yard speech and Field Book at times the chapter does not use now match the chapter, and `state/current.md`'s Chapter 78 entry has been split across its three real days.

**21. Three claims about the state layer itself.** `state/continuity.md`'s blanket *every figure here is quotable from a chapter* now carries its three stated exceptions. `state/current.md`, CR-031 and the Batch 0003 brief said **Ivo Sarratt appears in Chapters 75 and 80**; **he is named in Chapter 75 only**, and all three now say so. And this entry, on its first pass, named **`outline/volume-02.md`, which does not exist in this repository** — the Volume 02 outline is a block in `outline/series.md` — and it now says so rather than citing a file that is not there.

**Still untouched, and it should be:** the eleven things the reviewer called right, the planned ending, the volume's unwritten final image, the run number still unlooked-up, the card still under the brick, `chapters/volume-01/`, `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0070.md`, `outline/`, and every controller-owned file. **No next phase was created.**

**Addendum to items 15–21, on the third pass of the same repair.** A second verification pass over the repaired batch found the twenty-one items above resolved with four exceptions, and turned up eleven further defects, of which one was introduced by the repair itself. **All are repaired here.** They are recorded in one entry because they are one class: **a figure, a date or a quotation written after the prose and read back as authority**, which is what CR-023 exists to record and which has now happened in this batch on three separate occasions.

**22. The repair introduced a Friday the twenty-second, and the chapter it contradicted was its own.** Chapter 80 has a clerk saying her returns are **off her desk on Friday the twenty-second**, eleven lines after the same chapter has said **Next walk Thursday the twenty-second**. The twenty-second is a Thursday. Corrected in the chapter, and the *not before Friday* undertaking on the card is now **the twenty-third**, which is the next Friday and the day the week of the nineteenth reaches the rack — in the chapter, in `state/open-threads.md` and in this entry.

**23. The repair introduced a claim the chapter does not make.** Having moved the form to Saturday the tenth, the repair had it lying face up in the route room **for three days** — which is impossible, because it went to a clinic at four o'clock on the Tuesday and because nobody has established weekend footfall of nine people a day in that room. **The form came back to that table on the Tuesday morning and lay there face up from the morning**, in Chapter 76, in `state/continuity.md` item 15 and in `state/current.md`.

**24. The failure's date survived in three places that are not Chapter 76, and in one state file.** **Chapters 78 and 80 both still said the seventh column was written on the Tuesday**, and both now say the Saturday before. **`state/continuity.md` item 15 did not receive the Saturday at all** — the substitution that carried it was lost with a failed write, which is why a verification pass was worth running — and it now reads Saturday the tenth.

**25. The seventh column was written both first and last, inside Chapter 76.** The page carries the heading, then the six column names, then the seventh, and the prose said the seventh was written *before he wrote anything else on the page*. **It is now before he wrote a single line under any of the other six**, which is the same beat — a seventh column written first, in four seconds — and is what the page can actually support.

**26. A year that is eighty years wrong.** Chapter 74's Field Book said a man of about forty told a man of twenty-six to listen to a drain **in 1971**, in a novel set in 2051. **The mark on the drain cover is in about 1971 and the telling is recent**, which is what the same chapter says twenty lines earlier, and the entry now says that and nothing more.

**27. Three refusals, two days, and a Tuesday that was not a walk day.** The gang walks Mondays and Thursdays. Chapter 72's third refusal is at the wall **on the Thursday morning**, and the speech said *now it is a Tuesday practice* and *I am not having that on a Monday in January*, and the Field Book said *three times in two days* for refusals that span the Monday and the Thursday. **The practice is a Thursday practice, the refusal is a Thursday, and it is four days.** `state/continuity.md` item 6 quotes the Tuesday phrase as binding on every later batch and now quotes the Thursday.

**28.
