Eleven days, three months, and a fishmonger at three different hours.** Chapter 73 called a minute read out that morning **eleven days old**; the amendment it records is from the thirteenth of October, so it is **three months old**. The lot prices on Saltmarket Row were called at **half past five** in Chapter 71, at **twenty past five** in Chapter 77 and at **17.20** in the form in Chapter 76, and `state/continuity.md` put the thumb in the crack ten minutes *before* the prices it follows. **Everything is now about twenty past five**, which is the figure with the most support, and the thumb follows the prices. **This matters beyond tidiness: the batch's closing answer rests on that woman doing it twice a day for nineteen years.**

**29. Nine words that are seventeen.** Chapter 76 called Milo's own rule *a map with no rule at the foot is an invitation to whoever has got a pen* **about nine words**, and it is seventeen. Corrected there. **The parallel *in about nine words* in Chapter 68 is Batch 0001's and was not touched**, for the same reason Chapter 69's arithmetic was not re-derived.

**30. A structural reference left standing in the fiction after item 19 removed one.** Chapter 78 said **He will not find out in this volume.** It now says there is nobody in this city he could ask and why. **The state layer's own phrase about the same fact stays**, because a state file is allowed to say what a chapter may not.

**31. Four places where the state layer had drifted off the manuscript.** `state/batch-summaries.md` said there **was** a name or initial on the down-loop man's strike where the chapter says there is none and that he would refuse to add one; it said **eleven returns with one reason written in eleven years** where Chapter 72 says **eleven rows** and **four years**; it quoted Dilley Farr with an inserted *the fifth — the last —* and a dropped clause that is the whole point of the speech; and it headed Chapter 79's forecast row **Fri 16th** where the nine days it claims run from the tenth to the nineteenth. **All four are now the chapter's text.** A quotation adjusted in a summary is the same offence as item 6.

**32. Two small clock errors and one damaged sentence.** Chapter 76's plan-room drawer was at **twenty past three**, before the clinic scene it follows, and is now **half past five**. Chapter 78's Field Book was headed **17.00** against its own **20.40**, and its yard speech **Fri 16th, 10.10** against the chapter's **Thursday evening at about four**. And `state/current.md`'s Chapter 78 bullet ended in a half-deleted clause with a duplicated *Field Book at 20.40*, which is what a failed write looks like, and it is now three clean bullets on the three real days.

**Nothing in this addendum changed a beat, a date the outline fixes, a character, a rule, or the planned ending.** The form is still his, the strike is still undated and unreasoned, the page is still in the tin on the Tuesday at about a quarter past nine, the name is still legible under the line, the run number is still unlooked-up, the card is still under the brick, and the volume's final image is still unwritten.

## CR-033 — Volume 02 Batch 0003 wrote *Nobody Can Name The Day*: the first two weather-driven seam events, the allocation sheet, the column headed *scheduled*, the second side of the map, the twenty-third day, and the batch's answer that a blank is a distinction that cannot be made

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0003` (Chapters 81–90)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image and the volume outline's own midpoints are untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, grammar, remnant type, category or antagonist was changed, and nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted. There is no `outline/volume-02.md` in this repository and none was invented; the Volume 02 outline is a block in `outline/series.md`. No chapter was restarted and no beat was removed.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-029 recorded what Volume 02's opening block fixed; CR-030 repaired it; CR-031 recorded what the second block fixed; CR-032 repaired that. **This block fixed six things a later batch would either contradict by accident or mistake for something it is free to change, and one of them is a new printed object in this city, which is exactly the kind of thing two writers add to.** The whole of the block's prose facts, item by item and quotable from a chapter, is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 81–90 fixed on the page," and this entry does not repeat that list.

**1. Two weather-driven seam events are now on the page, and neither is in a file, and this is the volume's larger question one step closer without being answered.** **The first** is a cast-iron drain cover on the back length, two marks on it from about 1971, up out of its frame by about two inches at about twenty past two in the morning on Thursday the twenty-ninth of January with the ballast washed from under about a foot of it and a clean hole the size of a dinner plate at the north side. Nobody was awake, nobody was hurt, nothing collapsed, and **the foreman's nine words are the finding: *It filled… I did not come down here, because it is a drain and it is not a road and nobody was on a car.*** **The second** happened on the afternoon of Thursday the nineteenth of February, in rain, in daylight, in front of about nine people, on a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street: **fish scales out of a wet stone floor for the first time in nineteen years, about nine thousand of them, in the run of a gutter; coffee at about ten to five, forty minutes early, with no coffee sold within a hundred and ten yards of that Row since the year before the war; and seven marks under a rail with six irons on it, measured, with no iron absent that anybody can name, and no list of irons anywhere in this city because nobody has ever needed one.** Nobody was hurt, nothing fell down, there is no seam, and **there is not one thing in either afternoon that could be put in a box.** The four pages forecast eleven between the second of January and the fourteenth of April, four of them in the river core, one in the Outer Reach, the rest unallocated and allocated on the day; **two of the four in the river core have now happened, ten remain, and no calendar anywhere in this city has a day on it.** **The event is weather-driven in the four pages' own sense — rain and wind load a route, a loaded route fails at a boundary, the boundary is a place somebody has been using — and this block shows a boundary failing with a gang on it and a woman of about forty-five's thumb in a crack in the same wall.**

**2. The SEAM EVENT ALLOCATION sheet is a new object in this city and it is not a new institution, and its limits are registered so that a later batch cannot widen it.** A single printed side, **out of the same machine as the district returns cards, about nine hundred of which come off it a month**, issued with the seasonal outlook **under the office of the First Surveyor**, five rows — **river core 4, Crown Heights, South Salt, Outer Reach 1, Mireward 0** — and columns for *events forecast*, *event*, *allocated to* and *officer responsible*. **The *events forecast* column is not typed; it is the four pages' figures transferred by hand with a pen.** Its line at the foot — *an allocation which cannot be returned is not an outstanding allocation; it is a sheet in a drawer* — is the source of the seven-day query. **Its whole purpose, in nine words, is that somebody is told on the day, and it has never once told anybody what happened, and the gap between being told and being looked at is about eleven months and a filing cabinet, and that gap is where this season is going to happen.** Registered limits: **a later batch may not make the forecast column typed, may not add a row, and may not have anybody add a row** — because the one thing this sheet is for is a body having to put a number under a heading, and the whole of the batch's argument is that a number under a heading is a claim that somebody was there.

**3. The Stage 2 gain was used zero times, and this is a fact and not a withdrawal.** The one holding of Batch 0002 — one holding, one place, under two minutes, no effect on anybody who does not know it happened, not repeatable by a second person, not schedulable — **was not exercised on any day of Chapters 81–90. Milo Rook went into nowhere, held nothing, and crossed nothing, and the reason is on the page twice.** Stage 2 continues and does not complete, **and a later batch may not read this block as evidence that the ability has gone or that it has grown.** The Stage 2 sensory cost recurred **once, as a taste**, on the thirty-first of January — nine cups from the same pot and at the ninth he put the cup down, because he had wanted to know whether he had been awake and had used taste as an instrument — **and no face, place, person or name acquired it, and the first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in these ten chapters.** The remembered-route list **stands at ten with nothing added and nothing removed**, the ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained, and the written line home is **the eighty-ninth day of it on the twentieth of February, blank, three marks in three hands, and nobody asked since the twenty-seventh of December — and the district's not asking is now on the page as a refusal and not as a kindness.**

**4. A length is still a third kind of place, a remnant is still a remnant, and this block refused to make a fourth.** What the block added is a fact about the city's paperwork and not about any place in it: **there is no row anywhere in Alderquay for a place that people use and that no department can enter** — a maintenance length has no address and cannot be given one, and a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street has no address and cannot be given one, and **the one entry in the district index that might have served was put in by a different department in the autumn in the only box there was and says a works frontage, and nobody is going to have it tidied and nobody is going to add a second one.** The batch says in the field, in a footnote to its own finding, that a row of frontages **is not a fourth kind of place**, and a length **is not a remnant type, not a failure state, and not a form of any of the seven grammars**, and a Row and a length remain two kinds of place. The three things added are all statements about labour: **a column headed *wrong* is a column about a person and the ground is not somebody; a side of a sheet that records a day is not a side that records a practice; and a form that says a round will run does not know whether it did and will say it did, which is the twenty-second of December rebuilt by the district's own hand.** The two events are the first thing in this volume that a Keepsake remnant has been shown doing, and **he could not classify it, wrote down that he was not going to invent a category for it, and observed in the same entry that inventing one would be the same mistake three times over.**

**5. The two documents of this block that are permanent and are about people, and the two that are not.** **A form in a surveyor's own coat with a column of days in it and about nine days of another woman's round in it**, struck, dated, unreasoned, with four people's objections on the back in their words, and it stays on him. **A piece of accountancy paper in the second tin with a woman's name struck through it, undated and unreasoned**, which has now **stopped him writing down a second thing** — a beam scale at a north end with its poise off nothing — and the reason is on the page: *I am not going to be the reason there are two.* Both are additions to the world in the sense that they are now objects in it, and **neither is a new category, a faction, an antagonist or a failure condition, and the second is the batch's standing cost and may not be removed from the tin, read by anybody, or told to its subject in this volume.** The fourth object, **a card under a brick with two lines on it about a run number that belongs to a crew and not to a car and can mean two things, with the line on its front not taken off**, is permanent and is about a number, not about a person, and **no destination is named on it, no passenger's name appears, and the volume's final image is not written in this block and may not be written by a later one without the outline.**

**6. The five figures a later batch is most likely to get wrong, all of them quotable and all of them made from the manuscript, per CR-023.** **First, the weekday chain, extended and not re-derived:** 21st Wed, 22nd Thu, 23rd Fri, 24th Sat, 25th Sun, **26th Mon, 27th Tue, 28th Wed, 29th Thu, 30th Fri, 31st Sat, 1st February Sun, 2nd Mon, 3rd Tue, 4th Wed, 5th Thu, 6th Fri, 7th Sat, 8th Sun, 9th Mon, 10th Tue, 11th Wed, 12th Thu, 13th Fri, 14th Sat, 15th Sun, 16th Mon, 17th Tue, 18th Wed, 19th Thu, 20th Fri**, then **1st March Sun, first working day Monday the 2nd, 1st April Wed, 14th April Tue.** **The nineteenth of February is a Thursday and is a walk day**, and the writer originally had it as a Wednesday, which would have put the second event on a day the gang was not on the length. **Second, the day-counts, all ten quotable from the chapter that owns the day:** the page home, day one being the twenty-fourth of November, **67, 68, 69, 70, 71, 71, 74, 81, 88, 89**; and the token, last out of the coat on the seventh of December, **53, 54, 55, 56, 57, 57, 60, 67, 74, 75**. **Third, the second tin's contents: four weeks of carbon plus the one other page on the sixteenth of January, seven weeks plus that page on the sixth of February, and nine weeks plus that page on the twentieth of February.** **Fourth, the Field Book: THIRTEEN dated pairs — one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, ONE eight, one twenty-five, and three added in February which are a three and two sevens — plus the single-day interval from the tenth of December, the four threes not collapsed, the February three being a fifth three and not one of the four, one eight and not two, and no thirty-six and no eleventh pair. *(Corrected at CR-034: CR-033 originally recorded eleven pairs and a thirty-six, which Chapter 89 asserted over a batch in which the book had been written nine times.)*** **Fifth, three counts inside the block: TEN Field Book entries on nine dates with two on Monday the second of February and none on the twenty-first of January; ELEVEN doors, being the original seven plus an allocation sheet, a district returns card, four pages on a table, and a form in another body's internal file; and THREE things under the brick.** **The writer's own pass on this block found and corrected seven defects before the state layer was written — two weekday errors, two day-count slips, an arrival date that contradicted its own seven-day interval, an interior distance that should have been a hundred and ten yards, an uncounted Field Book list, and twelve sentences of meta language — which is the class CR-023 exists to record and which has now happened in nine passes out of nine. That self-audit is UNVERIFIED and one of each class survived it: a weekday error in Chapter 90, a meta-language sentence in a bolded header in Chapter 85, and a miscounted word-count in Chapter 89, all three corrected at CR-034. Treat every figure this block asserts as a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns it and not as an authority.**

**7. The new thing this block was written to supply, and it is one sentence and it is partial.** **A season of weather-driven seam events is kept, if it is kept at all, by turning up on the days it does not come, and the honest price of that is a blank, and a blank is a record of a distinction that cannot be made.** It is in the mouth of a man of about twenty-six on a length of tram route in the open on the twelfth of February, and he gave it away and then gave its price in nine words and said he was not going to say it twice, and the surveyor has written it down twice and improved it none of the two times. **And the standing question is one step closer and not answered: three nils about Mireward in nine weeks, the third of them in a man's handwriting; a man of fifty-eight with a rod and a chain who goes out in the summer and writes four lines about about nine feet of drain into a pad nobody has ever asked him for; a list of districts a hundred and forty years old that has never been printed from a list of offices; and eleven events with ten of them unallocated and allocated on the day, and no form on the earth for a place that people use and that no department can enter.** **Nobody went to Mireward in this block and nobody is going to and the volume is not sending anybody.**

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing and its adjournment to the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the permanent line with his name on it, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the Stage 2 cost's three instances, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031, CR-032 and the manuscript left them. **What is new is the thing Chapters 81–90 was written to supply: the first two of the eleven events, a printed allocation sheet with a zero in it and a line under words that nobody filled in, a form for a season that a woman of sixty-one and a man of about twenty-six would not have, a second side on a sheet of accountancy paper with a rule about blanks in a fishmonger's hand, a woman of sixty on a second landing who cannot see the file, a district's permanent win discovered to be a protection and a gag at once, and the fourteenth of April sitting quietly in two documents that nobody has put next to each other except a woman at a counter.**

## CR-034 — Review repair of Volume 02 Batch 0003 (Chapters 81–90): a weekday contradicted by Volume 01, a Field Book silence that five chapters of the same batch refuted, a miscounted residue line, a name that merged two women, a promise a chapter never paid, an allocation sheet dated three ways, a day-count list with one entry dropped, one list described at three sizes, and a self-audit that had verified nothing

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the reviewer, `logs/volume-02-batch-0003.review.log` (Sections A–C), applied in the fix phase
- **Status:** applied. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image, both weather-driven seam events, the batch's answer and the batch's larger question are all untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, grammar, remnant type, category, faction or antagonist was changed, and nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted. There is no `outline/volume-02.md` in this repository and none was invented. No chapter was restarted, no scene was rewritten, and no beat was removed.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-033 recorded what Volume 02 Batch 0003 wrote. **The review pass found that the batch's prose was, with two exceptions, clean, and that the state layer had taken a false figure written after the prose and promoted it to authority in eight files and in the next batch's brief** — the CR-023 failure mode, in its tenth appearance. Two of the eleven repairs are therefore not cosmetic: they correct a chapter, and everything else is the cascade.

**1. The weekday, A1, and it is Volume 01 that settles it.** Chapter 90 said the working timetable was *for the week of Monday the fourteenth of December*. **The fourteenth of December is a Sunday**: `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0060.md:61` puts Pell Harrow's dated chalk arrow up on *the Sunday night*, and the CR-019 chain the batch itself extends gives 1 January a Thursday, which makes 16 December a Tuesday — as the same sentence says, two words later — and the fourteenth the Sunday before it. **Chapter 90 now reads *the week commencing Sunday the fourteenth of December***, and the correction was carried into `state/continuity.md` item 28, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `state/open-threads.md`, which had all promoted the wrong weekday to authority.

**2. The Field Book, A2, and this is the one that mattered.** Chapter 89's entry claimed *the book has not written since the fourteenth of January and that is thirty-six days and that is the eleventh pair.* **Five chapters of the same batch contradict it**: entries on 1 February (Ch 84), 2 February at 13.20 (Ch 85), 2 February at 19.30 (Ch 86), 5 February (Ch 87) and 12 February (Ch 88). Chapter 81 also states the rule that makes the original claim impossible on its own terms: *I have not written since the fourteenth and the silence is fifteen days and I am not going to write the two ends of it down, because I have got a habit now of pairing silences and pairing a silence is how a book becomes a set of numbers.* **A silence is not a pair, and the book had not been silent.**

**The decision the review asked for — ten or eleven — is thirteen, and it is derived, not chosen.** The ten prior intervals are unchanged: one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, one eight, one twenty-five. February added exactly three written pairs, because the four one-day gaps and the same-day gap fall under the rule already on the page that a one-day interval is *looked at and is still not written*: **a three (2 to 5 February) and two sevens (5 to 12 February, 12 to 19 February). The three is a fifth three and is not one of the four.** Chapter 89 now says so, in his own voice, and the passage keeps its best line — *I have never once been wrong about the count and I have been wrong about everything else* — while dropping the false total. **There is no thirty-six and there is no eleventh pair.** The correction was carried into `state/continuity.md` item 27, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/current.md` (three places), `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/terminology.md` and **two places in `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0004/PROMPT.md`**, which had told the next batch the list was eleven, must not be read as ten or as twelve, and carried a thirty-six. That brief now names all three retired figures — ten, eleven, twelve, and the thirty-six and the two eights — as errors that must not come back, and it keeps the CR-032 warning about *two eights* alive.

**3. The residue line was six words, not seven, A4.** *Weather uses whatever door is open* is six. Chapter 89 said seven in two places, once in a bolded section header and once at the head of a numbered entry. **The count of six was carried into `state/continuity.md` item 27, `state/current.md` (two places), `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and the Batch 0004 brief.** The miscount is almost certainly a collision with the seven iron marks in the same chapter, which is a separate seven and is still there.

**4. A name that merged two women, A3.** Chapter 86 had *Hester Brann* where the chapter's own other seven instances and the whole volume have **Hettie Brann** — the clinic woman of sixty-one who takes the four copies. **Hester Coombe** is a different and live character, the depot clerk of fifty-one, and the error read as a fusion of the two. Corrected to Hettie Brann. This is the finding the review called out as reading like two people welded into one, the same class as CR-030 item 2.

**5. Meta language in a bolded header, A5.** Chapter 85's Field Book header read *the one he would want **a reviewer** to check against the page*, the last surviving instance of the class the writer's own pass claimed to have cleared. **It now reads *somebody else***, which is what the sentence means: he wants it checked by a party who is not him. No other instance of *reviewer* or *reader* survives anywhere in Chapters 81–90.

**6. Four internal repairs, B1–B4, all inside the batch and all small.** **B1:** Chapter 82 promised a reason for not naming the drainage man *at the end of this* and never paid it; the reason is on the page but was not signposted, so the sentence now names it and points at where it stands. **B2:** the allocation sheet's provenance was dated three incompatible ways — printed on *the same Friday-morning run*, figures transferred *on the Monday*, returned *on the Friday the thirtieth* — and only two of those Fridays can be the print run. **It is now printed on the Friday the twenty-fifth, a day before the week it is for begins, and the hand-transfer is on the Monday the twenty-sixth, the day the week commences**, which is the only reading that lets the sheet exist before the figures are on it and still carry the 29 January event. **B3:** Chapter 89's *I am not going to write it a fourth time* sat against Chapter 84's *I have now done it three times* in a sentence that could be read either way; it now says it has written that down three times and will not write it a fourth, which is the same fact and one reading. The same chapter's *the seventh one* — the seventh idea in the book — collided with *the seventh column*, the district's standing motif, inside ten pages; it is now *this one*, and the sixth-of-six word-count is the only six in the line. **B4:** Chapters 86 and 90 both called the batch's Field Book run *the ten in this month*, and it spans 29 January to 20 February; both now read *since the twenty-ninth of January*, and the count they carry — ten entries on nine dates, none on 21 January — is unchanged and still true.

**7. A dropped entry in a nine-value list, C1.** The token list in four files read 53, 54, 55, 56, 57, 60, 67, 74, 75 — **nine values for ten chapters.** Chapter 81 does carry a token count, phrased differently from the other nine (*The token was cold this morning and it is cold now. The fifty-third day since the seventh of December*), so nothing was missing from the manuscript: **the state layer had silently de-duplicated the repeated fifty-seventh, Chapter 86's, while the parallel page-home list correctly retains its repeated seventy-first.** The list is now **53, 54, 55, 56, 57, 57, 60, 67, 74, 75** in `state/current.md`, `state/canon-changes.md` CR-033 item 6, `bible/terminology.md` and the Batch 0004 brief. **All ten day-counts in both registers are now arithmetically correct and quotable from the chapter that owns the day**, which is the first pass in ten in which that is true of the token register.

**8. One list, three sizes, C2.** The same list of figures was called *five* in CR-033, *nine* in the Batch 0004 brief and *four* in `state/current.md`. **The CR-033 list is five items and is five; the brief now says five.** The `current.md` passage is **a different, four-item list** — the second tin, the Field Book entries, the doors, the things under the brick — and it now says so in words, so that the two are not merged or added together by a later reader.

**9. The self-audit verified nothing, C3, and this is the finding about the process.** `state/current.md` and CR-033 item 6 both reported that the writer's own pass corrected seven defects, among them two weekday errors and twelve sentences of meta language. **The review found one of each class still standing** — the Chapter 90 weekday, the Chapter 85 header, and the Chapter 89 word-count — so the self-audit is now marked **UNVERIFIED** in both places, with the three survivors named, and both places carry the instruction that every figure the block asserts is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns it and not an authority. **This is the tenth pass in a row in which a figure written after the prose has been read back as authority, and it is the reason the two prose errors out of eleven findings were the cheapest to find and the two state errors were the most expensive.**

**10. One craft repair, and the density figure beside it, D.** The review measured *about* at 424 occurrences across the ten chapters and named Chapter 89 at 62, Chapter 83 at 58 and Chapter 90 at 48. **This pass counts 465 by word boundary across the same ten files, and the two figures are not reconciled: they were taken by different methods and the difference is not a repair either way. The density point does not depend on which is right.** **The tic is the volume's voice and it is inherited — Volume 02's earlier blocks run 19 to 66 per chapter — so it was not stripped, because that would be a volume-wide style change made in a fix pass and would damage an approximation register that ninety chapters depend on.** What was repaired is the one place where the tic had become a template: **the sentence *Nobody wrote it down, and about four people noticed that nobody had* was verbatim in Chapters 70, 83, 89 and 90**, and **Chapters 89 and 90 both claimed to be *the fifth time* of it, in six weeks and in seven weeks, which is a contradiction — the same occasion counted twice with two intervals.** Chapter 89 keeps the plain construction as the fifth time in six weeks; **Chapter 90 is now the sixth time in seven weeks and is worded differently.** Chapter 70 is inherited and was not touched. No other state file carried either count.

**One inherited discrepancy found and deliberately not repaired.** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0062.md:148` dates the works-jacket sighting to *Monday the fourteenth of December*, and `state/character-state.md` reported it faithfully. That is the same wrong weekday as item 1, in an already-audited block. **It was not repaired, because the chapter is the authority on what the chapter says and reopening an inherited, audited chapter is not this phase's work; it was flagged in place in `state/character-state.md` with the correction and the instruction that a later batch must use Sunday.** This is recorded here so the flag is not mistaken for a repair.

**11. One more defect, found by this pass and not by the review, in the same class as CR-032 item 1.** Chapter 88 contradicted itself about its own Field Book entry. **The bolded section header said *about nine o'clock* and said *three paragraphs*; the Field Book's own heading said *21.40* and said *Three lines*.** Both cannot be true, and the batch settles which is which twice over: **nine of the batch's ten Field Book entries fall between 19.30 and 23.55**, the single afternoon outlier being Chapter 85 at 13.20, whose header and entry already agree; and the entry itself is the primary artifact while the header is the narrator's. **The header now reads 21.40, and the false *Three lines* is gone, because the entry is a lead line, an unnumbered block, and three numbered paragraphs, and the header's *three paragraphs* was the correct half and is now the only half.** `state/current.md` had carried the wrong time and now reads 21.40. **This is recorded separately and honestly because the review read all ten chapters and did not report it, and because a repair pass that silently folds its own findings into someone else's list is how a state layer stops being auditable.**
**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing and its adjournment to the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the permanent line with his name on it, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column, the SEAM EVENT ALLOCATION sheet and its hand-entered column, the first event's five words and two initials, the three nils about Mireward, the cover that rocks, the column headed *scheduled*, the second side of the sheet, the twenty-three words, the run number looked up, the company secretary's four correct paragraphs, the woman of sixty on the second landing, the two events, the fourth sighting, the Stage 2 cost's three instances, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031, CR-032, CR-033 and the manuscript left them. **What changed is arithmetic and names: a weekday, a word count, a person's name, an interval list and its total, a list length, and two sentences of a repeated refrain.**

## CR-035 — Volume 02 Batch 0004 wrote *A List Is Not A Hearing*: the *allocated to* column, a report that cannot be made, a correction made with a broom, nine doors and no names, a box headed *does the section stand behind the entry*, nothing printed for the fifteenth of April, a departmental list day, and a crew entered in a timetable for a week and not worked in it

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0004` (Chapters 91–100)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image and the volume outline's own midpoints are untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, grammar, remnant type, category, faction or antagonist was changed, and nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted. There is no `outline/volume-02.md` in this repository and none was invented. No chapter was restarted and no beat was removed.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-029 recorded what Volume 02's opening block fixed, CR-030 repaired it, CR-031 recorded the second block, CR-032 repaired it, CR-033 recorded the third block and CR-034 repaired it. **This block fixed six things a later batch would either contradict by accident or mistake for something it is free to change, and four of the six are new pieces of stationery or a new fact about a room, which is exactly the kind of thing two writers add twice.** The whole of the block's prose facts, item by item and quotable from a chapter, is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 91–100 fixed on the page," and this entry does not repeat that list.

**1. Two of the eleven weather-driven seam events have happened and nine remain, and the batch's answer to the season is now on the page in two working people's mouths and it is not a surveyor's.** **There is nothing printed in this city for the day after the fourteenth of April.** Four pages, dated, issued under the office of the First Surveyor, for a season from the second of January to the fourteenth of April, forecasting eleven weather-driven seam events; they arrive by hand, carry no reference number, are not in a register, and carry no date for the thing they stand on. **A man of about fifty in a room in Crown Heights has been in that room nineteen years, has seen one of them, has never seen a second, has never asked for one and has never known anybody who has — and the sentence is the finding and it is not sinister and not a scandal: *I am telling you that as a fact about a room and not as a finding, because I have never once in nineteen years been in a position to know whether a thing does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent.*** A district returns card is a machine and prints four a month forever, and **the question that found it was an ordinary returns question — *what do you print in the second week of May?* — and the extraordinary answer came out of the ordinary one.** Her answer to *what do you want to happen on the fifteenth of April* is **nothing**, and the reason is on the page: *a district that has spent the winter being careful not to write anything down is going to be standing in the weather on the fifteenth of April with no reason to think anything is different, and about nine people in eleven streets will be the only people in this city who do that, and they will do it out of a guess and for nothing.* **A later batch may not print a second outlook, may not have anybody ask for one in writing, may not explain the absence, and may not resolve what happened on the fifteenth.** And the fourteen days remain: **the season ends on the fourteenth of April and the hearing sits on the fourteenth of April, neither office has told the other, the collision cannot be put in writing by anybody who is allowed to write anything, and no batch may have either office learn of the other.**

**2. A printed column that has been empty in nineteen years is now in use three times and refused twice, and the difference between the two refusals is the volume's argument in miniature.** **The SEAM EVENT ALLOCATION sheet gains no row and its forecast column is still hand-transferred with a pen, and a man of about fifty has written the same five figures out five times and has not reduced the four in the river core and has not reduced the zero against Mireward.** **The fourth column, *allocated to*, is the only one of the four that is about a person, and it is a record of a promise being kept rather than the promise: it says that a person was handed a piece of paper on a day, and there is no day.** Three names went in off a wall board, unasked and untold. **Mireward's was refused, and the refusal is not the January refusal of *officer responsible* and is registered separately: there is nobody to hand it to, and a name against a man of fifty-eight with a rod and a chain would produce a better-founded version of the same harm in a different column.** **The river core box was left blank for a reason that is not a principle.** **A later batch may not fill either box and may not have anybody find out whose name is in the three that were.** **Mireward carries no new nil in this block: the three nils stand and a fourth is forbidden, and the three are a dash and three blanks on a printed card, a typed zero on the allocation sheet, and a nil in a district stability column entered by a desk that did not sign its name.**

**3. A report that could not be made, and a box that asks the wrong question, and a nil that is a lie.** **A permanent way defect report has five boxes and a maintenance length cannot be reported out of it**: there is no box for a thing that is not a place, a section's reckoning is not a chainage, and the *risk to passengers* box cannot be filled nil because a nil there means no passengers have ever been on any of the quay lengths, and thirty-one of them were on one of them in December with a conductor of thirty-eight and a driver of fifty-one. **Four refusals in one morning and all four are correct, and the reason is nine words of a works clerk of forty-six: the box wants a place it can send a man to, and a man with a barrow on a Tuesday cannot find six hundred yards of track that has not got a name and is not on the diagram, and the diagram was right to take it off.** A second form came back down to the same office with a box headed **DOES THE SECTION STAND BEHIND THE ENTRY?**, on a line of fifty-four words a clerk wrote in January and a foreman signed without reading, **and it does not ask what he meant** — the only honest answer is yes, the only answer that keeps eleven men out of a room in April is no, and there is no column for what a sentence meant, so the correct entry is a line through the box and a date. **And the second half of the finding is the worse one: nobody has ever been asked about the nil on the line above, because a man with a pencil and nine returns is not looking for a box about persons, he is looking for a line that is not work — which is the second time in six months this district has been protected by a clerk being fast.** A later batch may not answer the query, may not have the foreman read the line, may not have anybody query line five, and **the sixth line of the fourteenth of January is still in a file with no copy and Milo Rook still does not know it and this batch did not tell him.**

**4. A practice is not a day, and the instrument cannot be made the right shape, and this is the batch's deepening of the grammar identification and not an advance of the ability.** **The second side of the map of this district is a week, and its lines are on Mondays and Thursdays because the round that carries it is a working day, and the practices on its first side are daily** — the fishmonger at ten past five, the sweeper at ten past six, the coffee man at half past five, and none of the three has ever missed. **A blank on that side is a day when nobody stood in the weather, and the side has been blank on five days in seven since the second of February.** And then the harder one: **a man of about sixty is not on the row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war, and a blank on a day-side is not a fault, and a thing that is not wrong cannot be struck out, and there is no column on that paper for a man who is somewhere else** — so the sheet has been right, in its own terms, seven weeks running while a man stood in the weather eleven minutes away. **The correction is twenty-eight words under a rule that is not his, undated and uninitialled, written by the man of about forty who has swept that row since he was twenty-one, and the coffee man has not been told and is not going to be.** **A more advanced grammar remains useless without the patient observation of Stage 0: the man with the broom is the observation and the surveyor is the one who needed it.** The registered distinctions stand and are restated: **a length is a third kind of place, a row of frontages is a Keepsake remnant, a practice is not a place, and none of the four may be converted into another, and no new kind of place and no new grammar and no fourth instance of anything has been introduced.**

**5. A list, a chair, and a document that is not a hearing.** **A single printed side — HALL OF WARDS, DEPARTMENTAL LIST DAY, BUSINESS OF TUESDAY THE FOURTEENTH OF APRIL**, six items, with a fourth line saying it is not a notice of hearing and confers nothing — **was served on the district by hand because the district is a party, and no district in this city had ever had one.** Its item one is the hearing, with the reporting officer's name on it; **its item six is *maintenance returns, current year, presented — Permanent Way (quay section), presented*, and the whole of what that list says about six hundred yards of reserved tram route is the word *presented*, in a line about forms, four lines away from a hearing about a sentence that no one in this city can produce.** **A list is a thing nobody asks anything in and a hearing is a thing somebody asks questions in, and the only document in five months with this district's name in it asked for nothing and gave a chair.** And the standing rule: *if a man in an office reads that sheet in April and says *and what is item one about*, then somebody has to be in a room on the fourteenth and say it out loud, and that is not a document and it cannot be made into one.* **A later batch may not move or re-author the adjournment, may not turn the list into a notice, and may not expand item six.**

**6. The figures a later batch is most likely to get wrong, all of them quotable and all of them made from the manuscript, per CR-023.** **First, the weekday chain, extended and not re-derived:** 20th Feb Fri, 21st Sat, 22nd Sun, 23rd Mon, 24th Tue, 25th Wed, 26th Thu, 27th Fri, 28th Sat, **1st March Sun — so the first working day of March is Monday the 2nd — 31st March Tue, 1st April Wed**, and the fixed point **14th April Tue.** **Second, the day-counts, all ten quotable from the chapter that owns the day.** The page home, day one being the twenty-fourth of November: **92, 103, 108, 112, 115, 119, 123, 127, 128 and 129** on the twenty-third of February, the sixth, eleventh, fifteenth, eighteenth, twenty-second, twenty-sixth, thirtieth and thirty-first of March and the first of April. The token, day zero being the seventh of December: **78, 89, 94, 98, 101, 105, 109, 113, 114 and 115.** **Third, the second tin's contents:** nine weeks of carbon plus the one other page on the twentieth of February; **ten on the twenty-seventh of February, eleven on the sixth of March, twelve on the thirteenth, thirteen on the twentieth and fourteen on the twenty-seventh are carried arithmetic and are NOT quotable from a chapter**; **fifteen on Friday the twenty-seventh of March, which is printed in Chapter 99, and sixteen on Friday the third of April, which is written in advance and printed in Chapter 100.** **Fourth, the Field Book, which is the entry most likely to be miscounted, because this is the eleventh pass and it is the fourth block in which a pair count has been asserted and has been wrong.** **The dated pairs are TWENTY: one six, SEVEN threes, one nine, one two, TWO fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and FOUR fours, all four of them in March** — **which must not be read as thirteen, as twenty-one or as twenty-two; the four threes that were here in December are the four that were here in December and are not collapsed; the fifth was added in the first week of February, the sixth on the twenty-third of February and the seventh on the eighteenth of March, and none of the original four is one of the other three; the four fours are the eleventh to the fifteenth, the eighteenth to the twenty-second, the twenty-second to the twenty-sixth and the twenty-sixth to the thirtieth of March; the single-day interval from the tenth of December is still looked at and is still not written; and there are no one-day gaps in this block at all, the runs of nothing being two, eleven, three, two, three, three and three days.** There is one eight, not two. **Fifth, two counts inside the block that a later reader will meet and should not confuse with anything:** **the number on the outside of the envelope reads *1 of 4*, not *2 of 4*** — the four copies are three of one version and one of the other, and the count means the same on the sixth of March and on the first of April while meaning a different piece of paper — **and Chapter 100's schedule is twelve items, not eleven.** **The writer's own pass on this block found and corrected three defects in its own earlier drafts before the state layer was written: a page-home day-count that was a day short and its paired token figure, an internally inconsistent pair list that had the fourth March interval as a four and the third as a three, and an envelope count of *2 of 4* against a stated state of three-and-one.** That self-check is **UNVERIFIED** in the manner CR-034 established, and **every figure this block asserts is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns it and not as an authority.**

**7. The new thing this block was written to supply, and it is the district's own instruments being the wrong shape, and a cost paid by a clerk.** **A week sheet kept by a satchel that only runs on a working day; a printed column that is a record of a promise being kept on a day that does not exist; a defect form with no box for a length; a day-side with no column for a man who is somewhere else; a query box that wants to know whether a section agrees with a clerk instead of what a clerk meant; and a list of nine doors and no names, which is the first list anybody in this district has made that cannot be turned into anybody, and which its own holder wants wrong-shaped because the moment a thing can be used it is in use.** **And the cost, which is not curable and is paid in a room over a fitter's shop: a works clerk of forty-six was told no in one word by a foreman who asked to hear a line that was his, and left a box empty with a date through it, and the honest answer and the safe answer are not the same answer and there is no column on that form for what a sentence meant.**

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the hearing and its adjournment to the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the permanent line with his name on it, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the sixth line and its fifty-four words, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column and its three nils, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the two weather-driven events, the second side of the map and its blanks, the answer that a blank is a distinction that cannot be made, the Stage 2 cost's three instances before this block and its fourth in it, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031, CR-032, CR-033, CR-034 and the manuscript left them. **What is new is the second month of the season: an *allocated to* column, a cover that cannot be reported, a Wednesday the district's own map could not see, nine doors, a query box, a nil that has now survived being read, a list day that gave a chair, nothing printed after the fourteenth of April, and a crew entered in a timetable for a week and not worked in it.**

**Files updated:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0091.md` … `chapter-0100.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/current.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/world.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and this entry. **Deliberately left alone:** every chapter in `chapters/volume-01/`, `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0090.md`, `outline/series.md` (including the Volume 02 block this batch was written against), `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/ending.md`, `outline/batches/`, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `…-0002/PROMPT.md`, `…-0003/PROMPT.md` and `…-0004/PROMPT.md` (the instructions that produced this work, kept as the historical record), CR-001 through CR-034, `state/phase-ledger.json`, the review gate, `.opencode/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md` and `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`.

## CR-036 — Volume 02 Batch 0004 is repaired: the pair list loses a fabricated thirteen and reads twenty, three chapters lose a weekday error, one chapter loses a chronology inversion, and the counter's closing hours stop being used to explain visits they cannot explain

**Date:** the repair pass following the review of `866202d`, and before Batch 0005 is dispatched. **Written by the fixer, not the writer, and it is recorded separately and honestly for the reason CR-034 item 11 gives: a repair pass that folds its own findings into the writer's list is how a state layer stops being auditable.** The batch was not restarted, no chapter was rewritten, and the planned plot is untouched.

**Why this entry exists.** The review read the manuscript and found that the costliest pattern in this repository is **asserted figures carried forward as authority** — the pair count, the "nine days", the comparison date, the "*1 of 4*" — and that each had been propagated into `state/*`, `bible/power-system.md`, CR-035 and the Batch 0005 prompt, so that the next writer would inherit them as fact. `state/current.md` itself says the figure "is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns each interval and not an authority," and that check had not been performed. **The five critical findings are all fixed at the source in the chapters and re-derived in the state layer rather than corrected in the state layer alone.** Seven further findings of the same class, which the review did not report and which were found while verifying its arithmetic, are recorded as item 9 so that they are auditable too.

**1. The Field Book's pair list carried a *one thirteen* with no referent anywhere in the manuscript, and the total was therefore twenty and not twenty-one.** The inherited count at the end of Batch 0003 is thirteen (`state/character-state.md:535`: one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, one eight, one twenty-five, plus three added in February, a three and two sevens). **This batch writes seven pairs — `3` (ch91), `5` (ch93), `4` (ch94), `3` (ch95), `4` (ch96), `4` (ch97), `4` (ch98) — so 13 + 7 = 20.** A repo-wide search returns no thirteen-day interval anywhere in Chapters 1–100. **The list now reads: one six, SEVEN threes, one nine, one two, TWO fives, ONE eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and FOUR fours, all four of them in March. That is twenty.** The figure was wrong in three places at once — ch98 said "That is twenty" beside a list that summed to twenty-one, ch100 said "That is twenty-one", and `state/current.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, CR-035 item 6 and the Batch 0005 prompt all carried twenty-one. **All of them now read twenty, and the thirteen is now an explicitly retired figure in every one of them, with the reason recorded.**

**2. There is no thirteen-day interval in this block, and the two single-day intervals at its end are silences and not pairs.** Chapter 92's Field Book entry is 6 March and says in its own words: *I have not written since the twenty-third and that is the largest gap since the twenty-five of January and I am not writing the two ends of it down, because a silence is not a pair.* **Chapters 99 and 100 are one day after the last entry and are likewise silences.** The state layer had invented a "pair in front of it a thirteen" for Chapter 92 and had asserted "four one-day gaps" while listing three, one of which sat inside the 20→23 February pair. **There are no one-day gaps in this block at all: the runs of nothing are two, eleven, three, two, three, three and three days, and that is now what the state layer says.**

**3. Chapter 96 stated the opposite of Chapters 94, 97 and 98 about its own pair.** `ch94:109` is already *Four days off the eleventh*, and `ch97:113` says "three of the fours in this month" and `ch98:85` writes out the four March intervals. **Chapter 96's is the second four, not the first, and now says so in both the section lead and the entry.** This is the same defect class the project records at CR-032 item 9 and CR-034 item 11.

**4. Chapter 94 dated its own central scene to two different days.** The scene is Sunday the fifteenth of March, in the section lead, in the boy's line, in the Field Book lead and in the entry, and the undertaking was sworn on the sixth of February. **`ch94:79` and `ch94:119` said "It is the eighth of March" in the mouth of the character who has just done the thing; both now read the fifteenth of March, and the entry says the sum out loud, thirty-seven days.** The state layer's "nine days later" in `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and `state/open-threads.md` matched neither date and is now thirty-seven. `state/character-state.md` already had the fifteenth.

**One more date fault in the same list, found while checking the tin.** The state layer put a rack day on **Tuesday the thirty-first of March**, and the weekly sheet goes into the rack on the Friday. **The rack days in this block are six Fridays — 27 February, 6, 13, 20 and 27 March and 3 April — and the thirty-first was never one, in `state/current.md` or `state/continuity.md`.** The second tin's count was re-derived from the same error and is now **fifteen on Friday the twenty-seventh of March** in both files and in Chapters 99 and 100, which is the last duplicate actually to go in before the sixteenth lands on Friday the third of April.

**5. Chapter 99 put *three of four* on the outside of an envelope that reads *1 of 4* everywhere else.** The batch's own note in `state/current.md` records that the count was corrected from *2 of 4* to *1 of 4* in the writer's own pass, and Chapter 99 reintroduced a third figure in the batch's load-bearing object. **It now reads *1 of 4*, and the state layer and the Batch 0005 prompt both name *3 of 4* as a retired figure alongside *2 of 4*.**

**6. The second comparison of the four return-route copies moved four days off the second of March, and is now accounted for rather than asserted.** `ch90:65` and `ch90:103` fix it at Monday the second of March and the Batch 0004 prompt fixed the same three dates. **The batch wrote the second comparison on Friday the sixth of March with no accounting, and `state/continuity.md` then claimed that the second of March was included and that both dates were fixed and neither moved, which was false against the manuscript.** The date has not been moved back, because the sixth of March is the day the district returns card comes back and the whole chapter is built on it. **Instead Chapter 92 now says, in the sentence that opens the scene, that it is "four days later than the Monday she had it written down for," Chapter 100's schedule now states the rule as *once a month, on a day she fixes herself and writes on the envelope* instead of *the first working day of the month*, and `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt now record the move and the reason.** The Batch 0004 prompt is left exactly as it stands, being the historical record of the instructions that produced this work.

**7. Chapter 100's closing Field Book paragraph contradicted itself three times in twelve lines, and now does not.** It said the nine lines were there on "the eighth, and the **eleventh**, and the fourteenth" against "the eighth, and on the **twelfth**, and on the fourteenth" twice elsewhere in the same entry; it said a woman of sixty-one "has now written it out **four times**" and then that "a person I have never asked has now done it **twice**", against the scenes, in which she writes it **once**, in four seconds, on the first of April, and refuses the envelope. **The twelfth is now the twelfth and the writing is once, at all, and the refusal is still in the same paragraph.** The single 1,100-word closing paragraph has also been broken into three, which the review named as a defect in its own right.

**8. The closing-hours rule was being used to explain visits it cannot explain, and the repair is at the scene times and not at the rule.** The water clinic counter's half-hour closure between one and half past one is established before this batch: `ch83:81` has the counter shut at four on a Sunday, and Chapter 86's comparison is *in the half hour the counter is shut for*, which the Batch 0002 record places at 13.00. **The batch put four counter scenes at about four o'clock and described them as being in that half hour, and Chapter 91 explained a Monday-morning visit by a closure that happens on Tuesdays and Fridays.** All five scenes are now inside the closure: `ch91:106` at about ten past one, `ch92:86` and `ch96:43` at about ten past one, `ch100:3` at about ten past one, and the counter at `ch100:81` and `:83` at about half past one. **The rule is unchanged and the Tuesday-and-Friday claim is gone.**

**9. Seven further findings of the same class, found while verifying the review's arithmetic, and recorded here so that they are not this pass's hidden work.** **(a) Weekday errors in three chapters.** The chain fixes 18 March as a Wednesday, 19 March as a Thursday and 26 March as a Thursday. **Chapters 95 and 97 had shifted the weekday by one day and kept the date: "Tuesday the eighteenth of March" and "Wednesday the nineteenth of March" in Chapter 95, and "Friday the twenty-sixth of March" in Chapter 97 in three places, together with a "decide a thing on a Friday". All are now correct, and `bible/terminology.md:527` and `state/character-state.md` follow.** **(b) A chronology inversion in Chapter 97.** Its Field Book entry is 26 March at 22.15 and its own item three says "I said in a yard this morning", but the chapter's two yard scenes were dated "on the Sunday" and "on the Monday the thirtieth of March", which are 29 and 30 March. **Both are now on the afternoon of the twenty-sixth, and the answer that went to the district is on the Friday rather than "on the Thursday".** **(c) The annual return was filed under a weekly schedule.** `ch97:3` and `ch78:23` both put it in January; `ch100:98` had it as *Every Friday*. **It is now *Every January*.** **(d) An annual return's weekday.** `ch100:35` said "the third is a Saturday"; 3 April 1968 in this chain is a Friday and 3 May is a Friday. **It is now "the fourth is a Saturday", and 4 April is a Saturday.** **(e) Three weekdays in three small places.** `ch93:91` said the twelfth of the month is a Tuesday and it is a Thursday; `ch100:103` said Ola Petrossian's next round is "on the seventh" and 7 April is a Tuesday, so it is **now the fifth, which is a Sunday**; `ch98:93` and `ch100:107` said the second side has four lines in March and it has three, on the fifteenth, the twenty-second and the thirtieth. **(f) A man could not come off the round on a Sunday.** `ch94:97` has the man of about twenty-six come off the round on Sunday the fifteenth of March, and the round does not go out at a weekend — that is the chapter's own finding, written on the second line of the form. **He now comes off a shift.** **(g) Three garbled or softened sentences and one wrong ordinal.** `ch95:36`'s "the same good box that a clerk of forty-six stood at a window about four minutes over in January" does not parse and now reads "the same kind of good box as the one about persons that a clerk of forty-six stood at a window about four minutes over in January", which is what Chapter 78 says happened. `ch97:25`'s "he was not read it out" is now "it was not read out to him". `ch96:53`'s "now it is on a Saturday's memory in eleven streets" is now "on about nine memories in eleven streets". And **the 16 March sensory cost is its fourth instance, as `state/character-state.md` has said since the writer's own pass, not its third; "the third time in five months" is now "the fourth time in three months".**

**10. Three items the review raised that are answered rather than changed, and the reasoning is recorded so that a later pass does not undo them.** **(a) The two fifty-one-year-old men.** The allocation clerk in the room with the machine is fifty on the thirtieth of March and fifty-one in April, and Ivo Sarratt is the reporting officer, fifty-one, twelve years, named on a printed list. The prose never conflated them. **`ch98:25` no longer opens with a bare "a man of fifty-one next month" and now reads "the man of about fifty with the machine in it, who is fifty-one next month and has been in that room nineteen years", so that the two are separated by their room, their work and their sentence.** **(b) Nell Crale's dead relative.** The review reported that `ch93:95` says *her mother's boy* and `ch52:23` says *my brother's boy*. **Volume 01 is itself inconsistent and always has been: `ch36:67` says *my mother's boy* and `ch52:23` says *my brother's boy*.** Two of three support *mother's boy*, the earlier chapter supports it, and `bible/terminology.md` had already registered her that way, so the batch and the bible are left as the writer had them, **and the discrepancy is flagged here for the controller rather than being silently resolved by editing a closed and audited Volume 01 chapter.** A later batch may pick either and must say which. **(c) The Field Book's closing blocks.** The review measured the batch's closing Field Book entries at about 8,500 words, a quarter of the batch, re-narrating the chapters item for item, and counted 64 section breaks against a prior-batch norm of four to seven. **The section-lead device and the closing Field Book entry are this book's established spine and are not defects, and they were not restructured.** What was cut is the one place the review named as verbatim duplication: **Chapter 98's second item re-printed Hettie Brann's yard sentence almost word for word from twenty lines earlier, and it now says he is not going to write her sentence out a second time in a book, and points the reader at the yard.**

**11. Meta language inside the fiction is gone from the batch.** `ch95:62` and `ch97:23` said "the whole of this chapter"; `ch100:98` said "is not going to be told in this volume"; `ch100:104` said "it is the sixth time in this volume"; and `ch98:91` and `ch100:134` said "this volume" of the risk and of the method. **All six are rewritten, and the narrator's tic of "is on the page" — `ch94:103`, `ch95:106`, `ch96:5`, `ch97:25`, `ch97:49`, `ch99:38`, `ch99:56` — is gone from all ten chapters.** A search of Chapters 91–100 for "this chapter", "this volume" and "on the page" returns nothing.

**12. Two mechanical refrains were thinned where they had become the sentence frame, and the protagonist's own tic was left alone.** "About four people have said since that" appeared six times in five chapters and now appears three, the other three carrying the same fact in a different frame. **"I have written that down" is the man, not the frame, and is left as it is at about once a chapter.** The review's separate point that the batch reads as a beat sheet is answered in item 10(c).

**13. The dates the Batch 0005 prompt inherited are now the manuscript's.** The prompt's three load-bearing figures — the pair count, the one-day gaps, and the three comparison dates — were all wrong on arrival and are now right, and it no longer asserts that the first of April was chosen because "there is no first working day of the month in April before it," which is false in a chain where the first of April is a Wednesday and the working days run Monday to Friday. **The prompt is the only next phase and it was edited in place; no second phase was created and nothing was dispatched.**

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin and its page, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing and its adjournment to the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the permanent line with his name on it, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the sixth line and its fifty-four words, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column and its three nils, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the *allocated to* column and its two refusals, the two weather-driven events, the second side of the map and its blanks, the answer that a blank is a distinction that cannot be made, the cover that cannot be reported, the twenty-eight words under the rule, the nine doors and no names, the query box and the empty answer, the nil that has survived being read, the answer that nothing is printed for the fifteenth of April, the departmental list day and the chair it gave, the crew entered in a timetable for a week and not worked in it, the nine lines of a route home in a third party's hand, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031, CR-032, CR-033, CR-034, CR-035 and the manuscript left them. **What changed is arithmetic, weekdays, two times of day, a count on an envelope, and the shape of six sentences. No scene was added, moved, cut or re-staged, no character changed a decision, and the volume's final image is still unwritten.**
