## CR-037 — Volume 02 Batch 0005 wrote *A Continuation Of An Adjourned Matter*: the hearing sat and determined nothing, a party said yes in four seconds, a rule about four copies of a card turned out to be a rule about custody, a plate came off a fence, a woman of forty-five said the answer in nine words, a second list came with the matter off it, and a clerk in a timetable division said which column a number belongs to

- **Type:** clarification
- **Raised by:** the writer, `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0005` (Chapters 101–110)
- **Status:** applied in the same run. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image and the volume outline's own midpoints are untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, grammar, remnant type, category or antagonist was changed, and nothing in `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `bible/premise.md` is contradicted. There is no `outline/volume-02.md` in this repository and none was invented. No chapter was restarted and no beat was removed.**

**Why this entry exists.** CR-029 recorded what Volume 02's opening block fixed, CR-030 repaired it, CR-031 recorded the second, CR-032 repaired it, CR-033 the third, CR-034 repaired it, CR-035 the fourth and CR-036 repaired it. **This block fixed seven things a later batch would either contradict by accident or mistake for something it is free to change, and four of the seven are new pieces of stationery or a new fact about a department, which is exactly the kind of thing two writers add twice.** The whole of the block's prose facts, item by item and quotable from a chapter, is in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 101–110 fixed on the page," and this entry does not repeat that list.

**1. A hearing can end without being adjourned, and this is the volume's structural finding.** **On Tuesday the fourteenth of April the hearing on CO/6/BELL sat as a continuation of an adjourned matter, determined nothing, adjourned nothing, and set no date, and the reporting officer said in a room that he has no further date to give, that this is not a decision, that he has not got the standing to make it one, and that a department does not set a date for a matter it cannot hear — and asked for the distinction between *stood over* and *adjourned* to be minuted, and the minute did not distinguish them, and he asked anyway.** **A matter does not lose its date by being left off a list; it loses its date by being discharged, withdrawn or varied, and a suspended order is none of those three.** The registered limits: **a later batch may not discharge the order, may not vary or withdraw it, may not restore the public name to the street name index, may not restore the removed sentence, may not re-author or move the adjournment, may not treat the matter as re-adjourned, and may not put the matter back on any list.** And the consequence, registered with it: **on Thursday the twenty-third of April a second departmental list came to this district by hand and the matter was not on it, and there is no form in this city for a party being told that it is not on a list, and the only instrument that would have put the district in front of anybody again is a printed side of the business of a day, which is printed by a clerk of a list and not by a department.** A later batch may not restore the chair, may not add an item to the printed business of Tuesday the fifth of May, and may not make a document of a matter's absence from a list.

**2. A permanent protection can be conditional and the condition can belong to a person, and a department can be made to say so in a room.** **The line of the twelfth of January was read out and the reporting officer said: *It stands while the answer of the fourth of December stands. I have to say that it is a conditional line and that the condition is not mine, and that is a thing a department should not have to say in a room.*** **A later batch may not remove the line, may not vary its condition, may not have the condition lapse, and may not discharge the order by another route.**

**3. The cost that is not curable was paid in a room, in person, by the person who drafted the prohibition, and it is on the page in her words.** **A chair put the question to the party, the party said *yes* in about four seconds and said it again into the record, and said out loud that if the line goes then two hundred and fifty-two people go into one schedule and if it stands then one woman of sixty on a second landing, the ninth of twenty-six names in a Crown Heights file, cannot see it, cannot come off it and cannot ask whether her name is spelled right — and that she drafted the prohibition that is stopping her, and asked for that to be minuted, and said she knew exactly what the minute is going to do with it, which is print that she asked.** **The levy is unchanged and is not curable, and the woman of sixty is not named, is not in the room, and was not asked.** And the origin, which is new and is the cost inside the cost: **on Wednesday the eighth of April the woman of about thirty-four went up one flight and could not repeat twenty-three words word for word, and the condition she had set was word for word or not at all, so she did not do it.** A later batch may not soften any of this, may not have the woman of sixty be told anything, and may not have Lena Marr say the twenty-three words again.

**4. The changed method and the recovery, and they are the same person, and the method is a clause of a rule written in January that the protagonist had read four times without reading it as an instruction.** **The rule at the foot of the return-route envelope has read, in Hettie Brann's hand since the nineteenth of January: *if one of them is not the same, ask the person whose hand it is and nobody else.* On the twenty-first of April she said the second half of it out loud for the first time — *it is not about four people, it is about a hand* — and the method, in his own book: custody is a thing a person holds. Not a tin, not a coat, not a table in the rain, not an office, not a drawer in his own office, not under a brick. A drawer under a counter, which is wrong-shaped on purpose and cannot be chased, cannot be produced and cannot be given a reference, because a counter is not a repository.** **The third piece of paper under that counter was counted out loud in a yard, in front of about nine people, on a Wednesday, and the holder said in advance that she would not make a fourth without saying so out loud and has now said so twice. A later batch may not make a fourth without her saying so, may not put any of the three in a machine, and may not make a counter into a repository.** **The four pieces of paper, the four builds and the four versions of the return route are one arrangement and not two, and the holder is the same woman in both, and a later batch may not conflate the envelope's rule with the three papers' custody.**

**5. His failure, the fifth time, and the resolution is a half-filled box, and the diagnosis is now in a works clerk's mouth.** **A form headed *CO/6/BELL — MATTERS NOT DETERMINED* with a seventh column headed *why this matter was not determined*, two lines filled in and filled in well, and a works clerk of forty-six named the shape of it before the protagonist had got to the bottom of the page, in the words: *a column that wants to know why wants a person to put their name against the why. That is what it is for. There is no name on your page, and you are about to write one, and in about four years the name on that page is the name of a woman of about thirty-one who drafted the paragraph that caused the third matter, and you will have made her the officer responsible for a sentence nobody can produce.*** **He did not finish it, and a half-filled box in this authority is not an exception and generates nothing — it is the exact opposite of an exception, because an exception has to be cleared and a half-filled box does not.** His five failures are now on the page across five chapters. **He is not cured, the page is not a form about a person, and a later batch may not remove the page in the tin, may not have it read, may not have Ivy Marsden told, and may not use any of the five as a device to stop him writing.**

**6. The batch's deepening of the grammar identification, and it is not an advance of the transition, and it is the deepest the volume has gone.** **Four statements in the mouths of working people, none of them the protagonist's: every card in a hand index is a rateable place with a street on it and there is no card for a drain, a cover, a length, a row of shops, a fence, a woman, a market or a hole, and the index is not a record of what happened, it is a record of what is where (a reader of fifty-four, four years at the ninth desk on the left, and the same sentence said in December, in other words, by the index clerk of that same room, standing up, in front of her, unjoined and untold); nobody sweeps a room, and there is a broom in that office since the twelfth of March that has not been used, because the day she sweeps under the plan chest she is the person who sweeps that room; nobody keeps this row, and it is kept by about nine people doing it and not writing it down, and the moment you have a book of that somebody is keeping it and then it is one person with a book and nine people who have been asked (a fishmonger of about forty-five, nine words, in a doorway); and custody is a thing a person holds. The outline places field grammar across Volumes 2–3 and this block deepened the identification and did not advance the ability: the Stage 2 gain was used ZERO times, the Stage 2 sensory cost did not recur, and the first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 101–110. A more advanced grammar remains useless without the patient observation of Stage 0, and this block's evidence for that is a reader who reads whole cards and a fishmonger who has never once in nineteen years thought about what she does. The registered distinctions stand and were not re-litigated: a length is a third kind of place, a row of frontages is a Keepsake remnant, a practice is not a place and is not a day, a habit is not a calendar, a settlement plate is a plate, a fence is a fence, and none of the four may be converted into another. No new kind of place, no new grammar and no fourth instance of anything has been introduced.**

**7. The two unclaimed things, and the run number advanced one step, and the plant is advanced and is not turned into the volume's final image.** **A settlement plate with a name and a date on it came off two nails on a fence at the low end of Bridge Street on Monday the sixth of April, in wind, and is in the grass on the far side; nobody was hurt, nothing fell down, it is not a seam and it is not a length and it is not a remnant and it is not a place of any kind that anybody in this city has a word for; four doors are closed and all four are correct; a works gang came on the fortnightly inspection on the eighteenth and there is no column for a fence; and nobody has said out loud that it was one of the eleven, and there is no form on which to say it, and the protagonist has not said it and has written down that he is not going to. And a cast-iron cover that had been two inches proud since the second of February is flush, nobody reset it, nobody knows when it stopped, the condition column on the gang's weekly sheet has been empty since the twelfth of February, six walks in April carry nothing in it, and there is no form in this city that will take a sentence about a fault that stopped.** And on the run number: **a clerk in the timetable division said that the occurrence of the crew's number in the run column on the morning of Tuesday the sixteenth of December is the 07:52 booked working off Crown Heights, and the occurrence in the depot-move column is a light engine and a two-car set going to a gate, and it is not it — and that in nineteen years he has never once seen a crew in the run column for a morning when the car did not go out, and if a car does not go out the column is empty and the row is not there, because a crew that does not work does not happen.** **The card under the brick now carries a third line in that clerk's hand; the line on the front has not been taken off; there are three things under the brick and there have been three since the twenty-first of January; the protagonist has not written down what the clerk told him and has recorded that as a decision. No destination is named, no passenger's name appears, there is no thirty-one in it, and the volume's final image is not written in this batch and may not be written by a later one without the outline. A later batch may not have the protagonist, Pell Harrow, or any working person ask a timetable clerk anything else, and may not have the division say its refusal twice in the same building.**

**8. The figures a later batch is most likely to get wrong, all of them quotable and all of them made from the manuscript, per CR-023.** **First, the weekday chain, extended and not re-derived:** **4th Sat, 5th Sun, 6th Mon, 7th Tue, 8th Wed, 9th Thu, 10th Fri, 11th Sat, 12th Sun, 13th Mon, 14th April Tue, 15th Wed, 16th Thu, 17th Fri, 18th Sat, 19th Sun, 20th Mon, 21st Tue, 22nd Wed, 23rd Thu, 24th Fri, 25th Sat**, and the two dates a later batch needs and must not re-derive: **3rd May Mon and 5th May Tue.** **Second, the gang's week.** The walks in April are **the 6th, 9th, 13th, 16th, 20th and 23rd** and the rack days are **the 10th, 17th and 24th — three Fridays. The twenty-first of April is a Tuesday and is not a rack day, and the Batch 0005 brief listed four rack days including the twenty-first, and that was a weekday error in the brief.** The second tin held **sixteen** weeks of carbon plus the one page that is not a gang sheet on Friday the third of April (inherited, printed in Chapter 100), **seventeen on the tenth, eighteen on the seventeenth, and nineteen on Friday the twenty-fourth, printed in Chapter 109.** **Third, the day-counts.** The page home, day one being the twenty-fourth of November: **138, 142, 144, 148, 149, 150, 151 and 153** in the eight Field Book entries, on the tenth, fourteenth, sixteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-fifth of April, **and one further figure, 136, in the prose of Chapter 101 for the eighth of April.** The token, day zero being the seventh of December: **124, 128, 130, 134, 135, 136, 137 and 139.** The inherited registers are carried forward unchanged and are not re-derived. **Fourth, the Field Book: TWENTY-SEVEN dated pairs — two sixes, SEVEN threes, one nine, FOUR twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and SIX fours — and the seven new intervals in this block are 6, 5, 4, 2, 4, 2 and 2, and the three one-day intervals are silences and not pairs, and Chapter 101's list, dated the tenth of April, correctly reads TWENTY-TWO and must not be read against the closing list.** The retired figures that must not come back are twenty-one, twenty-six and twenty-eight, and the *one thirteen* removed at CR-036 must not return. **Fifth, two counts inside the block: the number of things under the brick is THREE, and the number of pieces of paper under the water clinic counter is THREE, and the number of comparisons of the four return-route copies is still THREE, and the next one is Monday the third of May, and the number on the envelope still reads *1 of 4. — 1.iv.*** **Sixth, two declared references and no more: a woman of sixty-eight on nothing is referred to TWICE in Chapters 101–110, in Chapter 103 and in Chapter 110, both declared, and those are the seventh and the eighth across eight chapters of this volume; the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned in these ten chapters at all, and the woman of sixty on the second landing is a different and unnamed resident and is the ninth of the twenty-six names.**

**9. The seven figures in the state layer that were wrong on arrival in this run, and one defect in the state layer and none in the prose on a pair count, which is new.** **A Boundary Commission was invented in Chapter 101's first draft and removed, because it would have been a new institution.** **Chapter 101's Field Book list read twenty-seven and eight threes on the tenth of April, when the figure on that date is twenty-two and the threes are seven and stay seven.** **A gang was said to be on the length on Friday the twenty-fourth, which is not a walk day.** **Lena Marr's visit was dated Wednesday the ninth of April and the ninth is a Thursday; it is now the eighth.** **There were TWO Atlas residue lines in the draft, in Chapters 104 and 108, against a limit of one appearance in ten chapters; the Chapter 104 line was replaced with a reader and a broom.** **Three constructions of "in this volume" and one "on the page" were meta language and are rewritten.** **Chapter 110's schedule had drifted into a third variant of the return-route rule and is now the one on the page.** **And the one that is a first in eleven passes: `state/chapter-summaries.md`'s entry for Chapter 100 said "twenty-one pairs" where CR-036 had already corrected the chapter to twenty, and the state file has been corrected to the manuscript and no chapter was touched.**

**10. The writer's self-audit is UNVERIFIED, and the reason is CR-034 item 9.** **Every figure this block asserts is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns it and not an authority, and the nine defects listed at item 9 were found by the writer and not by a reviewer, which in eleven passes has meant that a self-audit finds the cheap defects and misses the expensive ones.**

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin and its page, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing as held and adjourned in January, the three matters, Ivo Sarratt, the permanent line with his name on it, the seventh column in a tin, the box about persons, the sixth line and its fifty-four words, the two queries on it, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column and its three nils, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the *allocated to* column and its two refusals, the two weather-driven events of Batch 0003, the second side of the map and its blanks, the answer that a blank is a distinction that cannot be made, the cover that cannot be reported, the twenty-eight words under the rule, the nine doors and no names, the query box and the empty answer, the nil that has survived being read, the answer that nothing is printed for the fifteenth of April, the departmental list day of the thirty-first of March and the chair it gave, the crew entered in a timetable for a week and not worked in it, the nine lines of a route home in a third party's hand, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031 through CR-036 and the manuscript left them. **What is new is the hearing sitting and determining nothing, a party saying yes in four seconds, a conditional line named in public by the department that granted it, a plate off a fence and a cover that stopped with no form for either, a rule about four copies of a card turned out to be a rule about custody, a reader's sentence that every card in a hand index is a place, a fishmonger's nine words that nobody keeps this row, a second list with the matter off it, and a clerk in a timetable division who said which column a number belongs to and would not be asked again.**

## CR-038 — Volume 02 Batch 0005 is repaired: the batch's flagship motif is put back in the room it belongs to, a Field Book entry that could not have been written on the day it was dated now can be, and a duplicated pair of items, a duplicated number, a wrong week, a dropped word and a craft term in the narrator's mouth are gone

- **Type:** correction
- **Raised by:** the fixer, from the review of `2cf6fdf` (findings 1–7)
- **Status:** applied. **The planned ending, the antagonist ladder, the power-cost table, the relationship milestone list, the ten-volume structure, the volume's final image, Chapter 130's timetable, and the volume outline's own midpoints are untouched. No premise, world rule, power rule, cost onset, grammar, faction, antagonist or final enemy was added, removed or altered. No scene was added, cut, re-staged or moved between days, and no character changed a decision.**

**Date:** the repair pass following the review of `2cf6fdf`, before Batch 0006 is dispatched. **Written by the fixer and recorded separately, for the reason CR-034 item 11 and CR-036 give: a repair pass that folds its own findings into the writer's list is how a state layer stops being auditable.** The batch was not restarted, no chapter was rewritten from its beginning, and the good prose of all ten chapters — including the whole of Chapter 104's Wednesday, its working day with Bettina Sallow, her *ask me for something* rule, the broom in the corner since the twelfth of March, the FORM 4 that stopped asking about a sentence and started asking about a box, and Cyril Sale's two ruled lines — is preserved word for word except where a finding required a change.

**Why this entry exists.** The review read the manuscript against `state/chapter-summaries.md` and found that the batch's central motif rested on three facts that Chapter 63 contradicts, and that the motif had then been propagated as canon into six state and bible files and a CR entry. **The failure mode is the one this repository records in eleven consecutive passes — a fact written after the prose and read back as authority — and here the invented fact was a person, which is the most expensive kind, because a new named-adjacent figure in a room that already has a cast is how Volume 03's institution count starts to drift.** The second finding is the same class in a different costume: a Field Book entry dated at 22.40 on the sixteenth that reports a scene which the chapter itself puts at 11.20 on the seventeenth.

**1. The batch's flagship motif is re-anchored, and it is stronger for it. This is the load-bearing repair.** `ch104:59` and the Field Book's items Two and Three built the batch's answer on a *district index clerk* who said the same sentence to Milo Rook *in January* *from the other end of the same building* and whom Bettina Sallow had *never met*. **All three legs were false against Chapter 63.** The Survey School's repository has exactly two relevant people on the page: **Amos Trench, sixty-three, forty-one years at the index desk of that room**, and **Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years at the ninth desk on the left of the same room** — and `state/chapter-summaries.md:273` dates that chapter to **Wednesday the seventeenth of December**, not January. So it was one person, one room, one December scene, and the concealment beat at the old `:119` had him hiding from her that he had heard the other half of a sentence **she had said herself**.

**The repair keeps the motif and makes it exact.** The sentence the two of them share is the one about a *claim*, and both versions are already on the page: `ch63:27`, Trench, in a room of nine witnesses — *a copy of one of those is a claim. It says the work was done somewhere else… there is not a chain in this city long enough to hold a claim like that up* — and `ch104:47`, Sallow, at the same desk in April — *a card in an index is a claim that a document exists. It is not the document and it is not the absence of the document.* **A card is a claim and a copy is a claim and a claim is not the thing: one sentence, said in December and again in April, in one room, by two people who have never said one word to the other one about it — their single exchange being the nine words she wrote on a finding aid on the eighteenth of December and turned round to him, and he read it and said nothing (`ch63:71`, and she is *one of the nine* at `ch63:63` and `:69`).** The chapter now says this, names the shared word, and keeps the refusal to join them. **`two buildings` becomes `one room`, which is the harder and truer version: forty-one years and four years, nine desks apart, two hundred and forty years of field books in eleven presses behind them, and not one sentence.** The concealment is now a real act — he does not tell her that the man said it in front of her — and the Field Book's new item Three carries it, with the reason he gives: *I could put it in a book in about eleven seconds and it would be true, and it would be a document, and in about four years there would be two documents and the second one would be mine.*

**The state layer and the bible are corrected at the source and not patched in place alone.** `state/continuity.md` items 16 and 27, `state/current.md` (the calendar row, the standing-questions list and the pressure distribution), `state/batch-summaries.md` (the day table, the chapter note and the *won and real* list), `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` (her entry and Milo's *what he refused*), `state/open-threads.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `bible/world.md` and **CR-037 item 6 itself** now read as the manuscript reads. **The Batch 0006 prompt never carried the motif and was not touched.**

**2. Chapter 104's Field Book entry now falls on a day it could have been written on.** The entry was headed *Thursday the sixteenth of April, 22.40* and *two days off the fourteenth*, and its third item closed *the only correct thing either of us did **all morning*** — but the scene it reports is at 11.20 on the **seventeenth**. **The scene moves to Thursday the sixteenth** and the entry, its interval and its figures stay exactly where they were. **This is the cheaper of the two repairs the review offered and it is the only one that costs nothing in verified arithmetic:** moving the entry instead would have turned the 14→16 **two** into a **four** and the 16→20 **four** into a **two** in Chapter 104's own text, in `state/continuity.md` item 22, in `state/current.md` and in the day-count list that `state/current.md` and the Batch 0006 prompt both carry forward. **As it now stands the list is untouched and still sums: twenty-two on the tenth of April in Chapter 101 and twenty-seven in Chapter 110, two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, six fours; the two-days-off-the-fourteenth and the hundred and forty-fourth day both stand and are both correct (24 November is day one); the page-home marks on the eighth, twelfth, fourteenth, fifteenth and sixteenth stand; and the token's hundred and thirtieth day stands.** The chapter's own reviewer's note that a 22.40 entry on the sixteenth cannot report the seventeenth is now answered at the scene and not at the arithmetic.

**3. The Field Book's duplicate numbering is gone: the entry now runs One to Six.** `ch104` was the only chapter in the batch with **One, Two, Three, Four, Four, Five.**

**4. The two near-duplicate items are now two different things.** Old items Two and Three both restated the same Sallow scene — the same *every card in this room is a place*, the same nine absences, the same *two hundred years old and a hundred and forty*, the same *never met the other one* — which is the quality gate's *no duplicated paragraphs* hit head on. **Item Two keeps the woman and her sentence. Item Three is new and is about the thing he did not tell her, the two hundred and forty years of field books in eleven presses, and the document he would make of it in about eleven seconds.** The nine absences and the street list now appear once in the chapter's Field Book instead of twice.

**5. One mechanical refrain, stated three times in one chapter, is now stated once and referred to twice.** *A man who decides two things are the same in a book is a man who has started making the case instead of the record* appeared at `ch104:63`, `:87` and `:121`, and the sweeper-and-broom pairing appeared at `:7`, `:79`, `:87` and `:121`. **The aphorism is now spoken once in the room and referred to twice afterwards, and its count is internally consistent: four times on paper before, this is the fifth, no sixth.** The broom is now carried by item Four alone, and the man of about forty by the opening image and the live exchange where he belongs.

**6. A wrong week is corrected at the source.** `ch104:91` said the FORM 4 came back *on the Thursday the ninth of April, **in the same week***; the ninth is in the week of 6–12 April and the surrounding scenes are the fifteenth and sixteenth. **It now reads *five days before the hearing and six days before that round went out*,** which is 9→14 April and 9→15 April, both correct on the chain, and which gives the reader the date anchor the false clause was trying to give. The FORM 4, the box, item four, line five, the seven weeks and Cyril Sale's two ruled lines are untouched, and the sentence in the margin and the fortnight out of the twenty-sixth of March stand.

**7. Two small faults are closed.** **`ch108:66` read *and the do not have to* and now reads *and they do not have to*.** **`ch101`'s Field Book had two entry headers, *Fifth* and *Tenth*, with one set of numbered items under the second; the Fifth had a header and no body. Each header now carries its own body — the Fifth gets its one line about the thirty-first of March and the first of April, the Tenth declares its four items and the list** — and the sixth and the five, the twenty-two, the hundred and thirty-eighth day and the hundred and twenty-fourth day are all exactly as they were.

**8. One craft term is out of the narrator's mouth.** **`ch104:121` said *No residue came out of that book on that floor* — the only occurrence of *residue* in the volume's prose, and a leak of the Atlas-residue register into a first-person Field Book entry in which it had never been labelled.** The clause is gone. It was also a near-duplication of the sentence after it, and the sentence after it now carries the beat alone. **The one permitted Atlas residue in the batch remains Chapter 108's seven words, *It is dated before it is read*, and the count is still one in ten chapters.**

**9. The word counts in the state layer are re-derived from the files, because the repair moved them.** Chapter 101 is **4,053** and Chapter 104 is **4,253**, and the batch is **32,744 words** (4,053 / 4,288 / 2,354 / 4,253 / 2,815 / 2,473 / 1,999 / 2,779 / 2,555 / 5,175). `state/current.md` and `state/batch-summaries.md` carried 32,722 and 4,027 and 4,257 and now carry the figures `wc -w` gives. **Nothing else in the batch changed length, and Chapter 108's fix is a one-word correction inside a counted sentence.**

**10. Two things the review asked about that are answered rather than changed, and the reasoning is recorded so a later pass does not undo them.** **(a) Chapter 104 re-describing the inherited set piece as though new.** The thirty-four steps, nine desks, the plan chest, the window that does not open and the chair under it are `ch63:5` and are not new; what is new is that **he has been in the room four times since the seventeenth of December and has never once asked it for a thing.** The chapter now says that, names the December visit and the afternoon it cost him, and Sallow's *four years at the ninth desk on the left* is marked as how long **he** has known she was in the room — while the nineteen years on a different index in a different building stays what it was, a thing he has from the back of a form and has not used. **(b) *He has been in that room four times in six months* and *in that room for six months*.** Chapter 63 is his first visit and it is the seventeenth of December, so *in six months* was loose. **It is now *four times since December* in the scene, in Sallow's mouth, in both Field Book items, and in `state/character-state.md`,** and the six-month figure survives only where it belongs, to the instrument he has carried since March.

**What the review verified clean, and this repair did not disturb.** All Field Book arithmetic; all eight day-counts and the token figures; the weekday chain, extended and not re-derived; the three rack days and not four; `state/current.md`'s total, now re-derived; zero occurrences of Ada Fenn, Cedar Court, Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable, Tarin Kest or Soren Rook; exactly one Atlas residue line; Chapter 102's handling of the hearing; and the separation of Lena Marr from the unnamed woman of sixty on the second landing.

**Plot impact: none, and that is the point of the entry.** The hearing that sat and determined nothing, the permanent line conditional on a condition that is not the department's, the four seconds and the six-paragraph minute, the plate in a field, the six empty gang sheets, the FORM 4 asked twice, the nil on line five, the broom in a corner since the twelfth of March, the changed method about four copies of a card, the three things under the brick, Hettie Brann's *It is three*, the fishmonger's nine words, the second departmental list with the matter off it, the board in the corridor, Barrow and the two columns, the run number advanced one step and not to the image, the orientation ledger at ten, the crossing count at nine, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the fifth sighting and the fifth refusal both not made, the prohibition on Volume 01's settled questions, and the volume's final image in Chapter 130 are all exactly as CR-037 and the manuscript left them. **What changed is one invented person, one month, one building, one day, one duplicated item, one duplicated number, one false week, one dropped word and one leaked term.**
