## CR-039 — Volume 02 Batch 0006 wrote *What The District Is Carrying*: the punch roll, the open item, the boundary that is two people agreeing to have stopped, a head as the only repository that cannot be produced, and a run of twenty-six nights that did not move a count

**Plot impact: none of the kind this file exists to prevent. The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing of the twelfth of January and its continuation of the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the conditional line with his name on it, the seventh column, the box about persons, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column and its three nils, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the Stage 2 cost's instances, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, the run number, the card under the brick, and the planned ending are all exactly as CR-031 through CR-038 and the manuscript left them.**

**This entry exists for four reasons, and all four are the class CR-023 records: a figure or a date written after the prose and read back as authority, in the state layer and in this batch's own brief, in both directions.**

1. **The third of May is a Sunday, and every state file from CR-031 onward said Monday the third of May, and the fourth comparison is on Monday the fourth.** Batch 0005's own printed chain ends **25 April Saturday**; 26th Sun, 27th Mon, 28th Tue, 29th Wed, 30th Thu, 1st May Fri, 2nd Sat, **3rd May Sun, 4th May Mon, 5th May Tue**. The rule at the foot of the envelope says the comparison happens on the **first working day of the month**. **Chapter 118 states the correction in Milo's own voice on the page and stages the fourth and last comparison on Monday the fourth of May, and `state/continuity.md` item 2, `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `bible/terminology.md` all now carry the corrected chain and name the error.** The chapter wins, per CR-023, and the error was in the state layer and in the brief and not in any chapter. **The Batch 0006 brief printed the correct chain and then required the wrong date two sentences later, and its own second list of dates was internally consistent with 6 May being a Monday and therefore with 4 May being a Monday and 3 May being a Sunday — so the brief contradicted itself and the chain was the part that was right.** The Batch 0007 prompt repeats the chain in full and states the third of May as a Sunday.

2. **The Field Book's figure is still TWENTY-SEVEN and it is still twenty-seven for a reason a later writer must be able to check against dates and not against arithmetic.** Milo Rook writes an entry in his own book **on every one of the twenty-six nights from Sunday the twenty-sixth of April to Thursday the twenty-first of May**, so every interval since the twenty-fifth of April is a single day, and **a single day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it** — a clause printed in Chapter 120 precisely so that the two facts cannot be confused. **No interval of two days or more was written in this block. The composition is unchanged at two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens and six fours, and it sums to twenty-seven, and the retired figures — two eights, a thirty-six, an eleventh pair, a thirteenth pair, a twentieth pair, and now twenty-eight and twenty-nine — must not come back, and the four threes that were here in December are not collapsed and the February and March threes are not one of them and there is one eight.**

3. **The day-counts, and two of them are carried arithmetic and not quotable, and one figure appears once in each register.** **Page home, day one = the twenty-fourth of November: 155, 156, 158, 159, 160, 161, 162, 163, 165 and 179**, on the twenty-seventh and twenty-eighth of April, the thirtieth of April, the first, second, third, fourth, fifth and seventh of May and the twenty-first; **157 on the twenty-ninth of April and 164 on the sixth of May are carried arithmetic with no count written in the chapter that owns the day.** **Token, day zero = the seventh of December: 141, 142, 144, 145, 146, 147, 148, 149, 151 and 165**, on the same dates, with 143 on the twenty-ninth of April and 150 on the sixth of May carried arithmetic. **One hundred and sixty-five appears once in each register — page home on the seventh of May and token on the twenty-first — and the two are different registers and must not be merged, which is the same defect class as the dropped entry in CR-034 item 7 and the same shape of error in the opposite direction.** Twenty-five is the first of January, a hundred and fifteen the first of April, a hundred and thirty-nine the twenty-fifth of April, a hundred and seventy-nine the twenty-first of May.

4. **Eleven defects found in the chapters by the writer's own pass before the state layer was written, and one in the state layer, and the class is the twelfth appearance of it.** A **twelve-man gang in Chapter 111** where the gang is eleven; **a rack day on a Thursday in a Chapter 113 Field Book entry** with the second tin therefore given as twenty on the thirtieth of April, when the last rack was Friday the twenty-fourth and the next is Friday the first of May; **a man of about fifty standing at a siding for twenty-nine years in Chapter 112**, cut as an invention and as a third twenty-nine-year figure alongside Corin Mabey's twenty-nine and the flag-man's twenty-two; **the flag-man's twenty-nine years in a Chapter 114 Field Book entry**, corrected to twenty-two; **Chapter 120's second tin dated "By Friday the twenty-first of May"** when the twenty-first is a Thursday, corrected to the fifteenth; **Chapter 120's list called fourteen lines when it is thirteen**; **"I have proved that fourteen times"** where the run is twenty-six; and, the worst of the eleven, **Chapter 120 stating "I put that beside Lena Marr's sentence about a head, and beside a woman's sentence about a number" — which directly contradicts Chapter 119's entire beat, which is that he has three identical sentences and has refused to join any two of them**, corrected so that the join is refused in Chapter 120 as it is in Chapter 119. **Plus six sentences of meta-language in six chapters** — "in this chapter" twice, "in this volume" twice, "this batch did not tell him", and "on the page" in four Field Book entries — **and four stale elapsed-time counters**: "in nineteen days" where 3 May to 21 May is eighteen; "nine weeks now" for the line-five nil where the figure is eleven by the twenty-first; "eleven days" in Chapter 119 against a figure the same scene had set at twenty-six; and "the seventh Monday since" the twelfth of February where the printed fact is the seventh walk in April. **And a forward reference in Chapter 111 to "the Thursday" inside a scene set on the Monday, which was rewritten as a same-morning exchange in two speeches.** **The self-audit is UNVERIFIED and one of each class may have survived it: a reader should check every weekday noun against the chain at item 2, every day-count against item 3, and the second tin's four figures against Chapter 114, Chapter 119 and Chapter 120. Treat every figure this block asserts as a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns it and not as an authority.**

**What the block actually added, and none of it is a new rule, a new faction, a new antagonist, a new grammar or a new kind of place.** A **punch roll** — about nine inches of paper in a wooden case, a hole for every person that gets on, the only thing in this city that knows how many people were on that car and not who any of them are — and the finding that **the instrument a person cannot produce is the thing a person produces about nine hundred times a day and nobody has ever asked her to make it because they already have it**, which is **half an answer to the third part of the standing question and does not answer it.** **A return of service particulars with item six, four lines deep, and an item standing open** — not a fault, not a nil, not a zero, not a defect, not a query, and not a form about a person. **The line of the twelfth of January used for the first time in five months, as a shield, with the cost named by the person who drafted it and left deliberately unresolved as between a shield and a gag.** **A boundary as a place where two people agreed to have stopped**, and a peg as a point, and a plate as what a decision gets put on from the far side when the decision is made in the middle by two people walking towards each other and neither of them is at the fence. **A head as the only repository in this city that cannot be produced against anybody**, said by a party in a doorway and not by a surveyor. **A list read by the people on it and a minute read by nobody until somebody produces it.** **A version as a fact about a person, and a number that was not rewritten, and a record of a person deciding not to write something.** **A seventh resolution, written on purpose, in the same book as the sixth, and the recognition that a sentence with a why in it is the same shape as a seventh column.** And **twenty-six consecutive nights in a book in a coat in which a count of twenty-seven did not move, and a sheet with five correct blanks in it that cannot see any of it, and no line drawn between them and no reason written down.** **Atlas residue was used once, in eight words, in chalk on the inside of a gate: *Whoever holds the pen is not the writer.***

**The things this block did not do, and every one of them is on the prohibition list a later batch inherits:** it did not discharge the order, vary it, withdraw it or restore the public name to the street name index; it did not move or re-author the adjournment and did not re-adjourn the matter; it did not restore the removed sentence and did not ask the withdrawal reference a third time; it did not remove, vary or let lapse the line of the twelfth of January; it did not put the matter back on any list and did not add or remove an item from the printed business of the fifth of May, and it did not go and sit in that room; it did not answer the letter and did not have anybody supply a number or supply a refusal, and the item stands open; it did not print a second seasonal outlook, did not have anybody ask for one in writing, and did not explain the absence; it did not add a fourth nil about Mireward and nobody went there; it did not report the plate, put it back, write it down, tell the works, or say out loud that it was one of the eleven, and Milo Rook did not go on the far side of that fence; it did not put a word in the condition column about why; it did not answer the FORM 4 queries, did not have the foreman read the sixth line, did not have anybody query line five, and did not bring the sixth line to light; it did not cross, so the tally is nine and the remembered-route list is ten and the written line home is the hundred and seventy-ninth day and did not come back; it did not use the two sensory losses and gave them no face, place, person or name; it did not make a fifth sighting or a fifth refusal of the man in the works jacket, and did not join the three clues or add a fifth refusal to join them; it did not move the run number at all, and the card under the brick still carries three things and the same three lines; it did not add a reference to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing, and the count stands at eight; it did not name or tell Ivy Marsden and did not go near the north end of that row; it did not name Soren Rook, Sel Iri, Orrin Sable, Halden Vey or Tarin Kest, and added nobody above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect or a hearing panel; and **it did not write the volume's final image and did not set it up as a cliffhanger and did not turn the thirty-one names into anything.**

---

## CR-040 — Volume 02 Batch 0006 is repaired: the one standing Wednesday is un-multiplied and the fourth unshowable thing is the fourth again, a chapter stops narrating a day it is dated before, a boy of seventeen is twenty-one and not forty in the year a piece of paper is read, a counter that ran backwards inside one morning is put right, a walking day on a Friday becomes a walking day on a Monday, two elapsed-time counters and a corrupted quotation are corrected, and the two live sentences of meta-language are removed

**Plot impact: none of the kind this file exists to prevent.** The disappearance and its method, the reversal, the ninth crossing, the passengers' return, the record that cannot be recreated, the second tin, the custody register page and its undemanded and unconfessed state, the first stable field map, the returned names, the hearing of the twelfth of January and its continuation of the fourteenth of April, Ivo Sarratt, the conditional line with his name on it, the seventh column, the box about persons, the return route at version two and its holder, the Seasonal Seam and Greywake Outlook, the Mireward column and its three nils, the office of the First Surveyor with nobody behind it, the Stage 2 cost's instances, the persistent written line, the standing prohibitions on Ada Fenn and on Volume 01's settled questions, the antagonist ladder, the two money totals, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, the Lark Street grievance refused and unresolved, the three unjoined clues, the three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, the run number, the card under the brick, the batch's six moments of a wrong-shaped instrument, the batch's title, its shape and its refusal of every cure, and **the planned ending and the volume's final image** are all exactly as CR-039 and the manuscript left them. **No scene was rewritten, no character was changed, no beat was added or removed, and no sentence of good prose was replaced except where the sentence itself was the defect.**

**This entry exists for one reason and it is the class CR-023 records: a figure, a date, a count or a quotation written after the prose and read back as authority, in the state layer, in the brief and in the chapters, in both directions.** The review of commit `b283810` found **twenty-six defects** — seven load-bearing, thirteen in the date and figure table, and six craft — and **CR-039's own list of eleven defects was accurate as far as it went, which is exactly why the survivors below matter.** Where the state layer and the prose agreed on a wrong figure, the review was right that the item needed a decision and not a substitution; the decisions are set out and are reversible by nothing but another numbered entry.

1. **THE ONE STANDING WEDNESDAY IS UN-MULTIPLIED, AND THE FOURTH THING IS THE FOURTH THING AGAIN. This is the batch's largest unforced break and it is repaired at the chapter, in four places, and the state layer follows it.** Chapter 111 as written asserted that about nine people in eleven streets stood in the weather on the eighth, the fifteenth **and** the twenty-second of April, with a fourth coming on the twenty-ninth, and it said the same on Chapter 119's yard speech, on Chapter 119's entry for the sixth, and in Chapter 120's carried list. **That is wrong against the manuscript twice over.** `chapter-0109.md` line 45 fixes **exactly four** unshowable things in five months and names the fourth as *about nine people standing about in eleven streets on the fifteenth of April for nothing at all*, alone. `chapter-0095.md` line 74 and `chapter-0096.md` line 71 establish the sheet's standing verdict: the second side has been blank on every Wednesday since the second of February, **and it is right on all of them, because the only person who stood in the weather on a Wednesday is a man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks eleven minutes away, whom the sheet also cannot see.** Multiplying the fifteenth into five destroyed the finding, made the sheet wrong, and turned one unshowable thing into a series — which is the precise thing Milo Rook's whole book is about not doing. **The count of blank Wednesdays is untouched and is the number the batch is actually about: the eighth, fifteenth, twenty-second and twenty-ninth of April and the sixth, thirteenth and twentieth of May — SEVEN by the twenty-first, all seven correct, and the first of April is not one of them because a woman of sixty-one put nine lines on it.** What is now on the page in Chapters 111, 119 and 120 is the *refusal* to multiply: **a blank that is right about a day it cannot see is not a fifth thing**, and the five blank Wednesdays are one thing and not five. **A later block may not have anybody stand in the weather on a Wednesday, may not turn the fifteenth of April into a series, and may not make the blank Wednesdays a fifth thing.**

2. **THE FORK IN "THE THINGS IN THIS DISTRICT IN FIVE MONTHS" IS CLOSED AT BOTH ENDS.** Chapter 114 called the flag-man's hour from December *the fifth thing in this district in five months that cannot be shown to anybody* and Chapter 119 called the blank Wednesdays as a group *the fifth*, and the two could not both be the fifth. **Chapter 114 now says what is true: it is the fifth thing in this district he knows of that cannot be shown to anybody, it is the only one of the five older than the fifth of January, and he has four in five months and a fifth from December and the fifth arrived by accident.** Chapter 119 now says **four** things in five months and states in the yard, in his own voice, that **the five blank Wednesdays are not a fifth one, they are the fourth of them five times over**, and that is the whole of that speech's new content. **The canon figure is four, and it is four, and the fifth, if it is ever wanted, is from December and is about a yard man's hour in a book about times.**

3. **CHAPTER 120 STOPS NARRATING FRIDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND OF MAY.** The chapter is dated **Thursday the twenty-first** and said the returned envelope *came back on Friday the twenty-second with a date stamped on it*, which is the next block's rack day and cannot be known on the twenty-first. **Nothing in the volume is lost: the envelope still went back on Wednesday the twentieth, a day before the date the letter asked for, with the flap down and nothing written on the outside of it, there is still no form in this city for putting a letter back a day early, the item on the return is still standing open, and the point is now sharper, because nothing is going to come back stamped from that building until some time next week and nobody in this district was told.** `state/current.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and `bible/terminology.md` all carried the Friday-the-twenty-second version and all four now carry the manuscript's.

4. **A BOY OF SEVENTEEN IS TWENTY-ONE, NOT FORTY, IN THE YEAR A PIECE OF PAPER IS READ.** Pell Harrow said he would be forty *in that year* in a sentence whose own clock was *in about four years*, which put him at twenty-one. Both the spoken refusal and the Field Book entry in Chapter 117 now say **he is seventeen now and he will be twenty-one when that piece of paper is read and forty when it is destroyed, and a piece of paper about a morning is very much easier to destroy than a boy is to get out from under one.** The refusal, the nine seconds, the reason and the standing prohibition on sending the man from the round are untouched.

5. **THE COUNTER THAT RAN BACKWARDS INSIDE ONE MORNING IS PUT RIGHT, AND THE FIGURES AFTER THE THIRTIETH OF APRIL ARE ONE HIGH.** The *nobody wrote it down* chain runs 9th (10 Apr), 10th and 11th (Ch 105), and then Chapter 111 numbered its 10.10 office scene twelfth and its 09.45 yard scene thirteenth — backwards, in one morning, on one Monday. **The yard is now the twelfth and the office the thirteenth, and the chain runs 14th in Chapter 113 (30 Apr, fourteen weeks), 15th in Chapter 114 (1 May, fifteen weeks) and 16th in Chapter 115 (2 May, fifteen weeks).** This is the ninth appearance of this class.

6. **THE GANG WALKS ON MONDAYS AND THURSDAYS AND A CHAPTER STOPPED SAYING OTHERWISE.** Chapter 114's Field Book, written on **Friday the first of May**, said *there is one on this morning* and put a walk on a Friday; it now reads *there was one on Thursday and there is one on Monday* and names the seventh and eighth of April's walks as the twenty-seventh and the thirtieth. **Chapter 115 called the thirtieth of April *the first in May*** — it is the eighth walk in April — and, written on Saturday the second, said *the first walk in May is tomorrow*; it now says **Monday, and two days away.**

7. **THE FOURTEEN NIGHTS ARE TWENTY-SIX, IN BOTH PLACES.** Chapter 120 said *fourteen nights in a row* twice, against a run of **twenty-six consecutive nights, the twenty-sixth of April to the twenty-first of May, one entry every night**, which is the batch's load-bearing fact and the reason the count of twenty-seven did not move. **Both instances are now twenty-six**, and `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/current.md` are corrected with them. **The fourteen that remains in Chapter 120 is a different and correct figure: fourteen Field Book entries between the eighth and the twenty-first of May, listed one line each.**

8. **THE THREE CROSS-CHAPTER CONTRADICTIONS ON THE HEAD AND THE NUMBER ARE CLOSED AS AN ARC, NOT AS A DENIAL.** Chapter 119 has him work out, at a counter at about ten past six on the sixth of May, that Lena Marr's sentence about a head and Hettie Brann's sentence about a number are the same sentence; Chapter 120 then said he had not put them together, which contradicted the chapter before it. **He did put them together and he did not write it down, and that is now what both chapters say.** Chapter 119 now states the distinction in his own hand and in that order — *I worked that out … and I have not written it on a page, and I want the two of those set down in that order because it is the whole of it* — and puts the reason down **once**; Chapter 120 refers back to it by date, says he has now put it down a second time and that is the last time on purpose, and **the near-verbatim duplication of the six-months-earlier clause is gone.** Chapter 120's *I have had all three inside a fortnight* was also wrong, since the number is eight months old, and now reads *the first of them for eight months and the second for a fortnight*.

9. **THE PUNCH ROLL HAS ONE FIGURE AND IT IS NINE HUNDRED.** Chapter 112 said *nine hundred people a day* in four places and *four hundred* in three, in one chapter, for the same object. **All three are now about nine hundred**, which is what Chapters 112 and 120 both say elsewhere and what the riders are. **Chapter 69's four hundred is a different act — saying the next call out loud — and is earlier prose, is not this class, and stands; a later block may not silently merge the two numbers.** No System panel, rule, world fact or power cost is involved and none is touched.

10. **THE FOUR ELAPSED-TIME COUNTERS THAT SURVIVED CR-039'S OWN AUDIT ARE CORRECTED.** *The twenty-first is in nineteen days* and *chosen by a machine in another building in about nineteen days*, both in the **same** 3 May entry of Chapter 116, are **eighteen**; *the item stands open … in about nineteen days* in Chapter 117, written on the fourth, is **seventeen**; *the twentieth day of May being four days away* in Chapter 118, written on the fifth, is **fifteen days away**; and *I have been in that corridor twice in three weeks* for the twenty-third of April and the fifth of May is **twice in twelve days** in all three of its occurrences. **And the two 'week' counters for not telling Mara Quill about the season are one number: the fourteenth of April to the fourth of May is three weeks, which is what she is told in the corridor, so Chapter 116's ninth week and Chapter 118's tenth week are both now the third week** — and `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `bible/terminology.md` all follow.

11. **THE TWO LIVE SENTENCES OF META-LANGUAGE ARE GONE, AND THERE ARE NOW NONE IN CHAPTERS 111–120.** *No batch has asked for one in writing and none is going to* and *no batch is going to answer it* are now *Nobody has asked for one in writing and nobody is going to* and *it is not going to be answered in a year either*. **The writing-unit term does not appear in any of the ten chapters, and did not appear in Chapters 101–110 either.** This is the second time this repository has found that CR-039's removal of meta-language missed live instances.

12. **THE SIX CRAFT DEFECTS.** A **point-of-view slip in Chapter 120** — *Milo Rook does not know what is in the sixth line*, in the middle of a list introduced as in my hand and otherwise first person — is now *I do not know what is in the sixth line*. **The ramp insight is dated to the twenty-eighth of April, not the first of May**, which is the day Chapter 112 is set on; the Crown Heights yard is the first. **Eight words in chalk written at about half past four on Wednesday the twentieth of May had been carried for *three weeks* in a chapter dated the twenty-first**; they have now been carried for a day, and the text says when he wrote them. **The thirteen-line list said it contained a Saturday and a Tuesday and it contains neither** — one Wednesday, one Sunday, three Mondays and Thursdays, one Friday, one every day — and the discovery is now the content of the sentence rather than a mistake in it. **And `state/continuity.md`'s eleventh list carried a garbled date string, *3–4 April a.m. of May*, which is now *3–4 May (Ch 116, Sun–Mon a.m.).***

13. **A CORRUPTED QUOTATION OF A PROTECTED LINE, IN TWO FILES.** `state/continuity.md` item 28 and `workspace/volume-02/volume-02-batch-0007/PROMPT.md` both carried ***A trade outlays its pitch***. **The canonical form, from Chapter 66 and from the Batch 0002 through Batch 0006 prompts, is *A trade outlives its pitch.*** Both files now carry the canonical form. **A later block may not repeat any of the six residue sentences and this one is a sentence in the manuscript, not a note about the manuscript.**

14. **THE BATCH 0007 PROMPT'S THREE FIGURES.** The prompt said *five blank Wednesdays* with *a sixth, seventh and eighth … coming in June* — **seven are already on the sheet and the twenty-seventh of May is the eighth, and it is in May**; its Chapter 121 beat asked for *the sixth and seventh*, which are the thirteenth and twentieth of May and are behind it; and it said the written line home is *the hundred and seventy-ninth day on Friday the twenty-second of May*, when **one hundred and seventy-nine is Thursday the twenty-first and the twenty-second is the hundred and eightieth.** All three are corrected in the prompt, and the prompt's description of Batch 0006's returned envelope is corrected with them. **The prompt's weekday chain, its walk days, its rack days, the Friday trap and the second tin's arithmetic were checked against the manuscript and are correct and were not touched.**

**The things this repair did not do.** It did not discharge, vary, withdraw or re-author the order; did not touch the adjournment or the classification; did not restore the removed sentence; did not put the matter back on any list or go near the business of the fifth of May; did not print a second outlook or ask for one; did not add a nil about Mireward; did not report, replace or bury the plate; did not put a word in the condition column about why; did not answer the FORM 4 queries, have the foreman read the sixth line, or have anybody query line five; did not cross, so the tally is nine and the routes are ten and the line home is the hundred and seventy-ninth day and did not come back; did not use the two sensory losses and gave them no face, place, person or name; did not make a fifth sighting or a fifth refusal of the man in the works jacket, and did not join the three clues or add a fifth refusal to join them; did not move the run number, and the card under a brick still carries three things and the same three lines; did not add a reference to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing, and the count stands at eight; did not name or tell Ivy Marsden and did not go near the north end of that row; did not name Soren Rook, Sel Iri, Orrin Sable, Halden Vey or Tarin Kest, and added nobody above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect or a hearing panel; did not give the block a second Atlas residue; **and it did not write the volume's final image and did not set it up as a cliffhanger and did not turn the thirty-one names into anything.**

**And the finding underneath all of it, which is the fourteenth pass in a row and is the reason this file exists.** In thirteen of the thirteen passes before this one, the state layer was written from the manuscript and the manuscript was checked against the state layer afterwards, and in every one of them a figure had drifted in one direction or the other. **This time the drift was in the chapters and in the brief and in the state layer together, and the two live instances of meta-language were in the volume's closing chapter, and the largest single break was the volume inventing a fifth instance of the thing the volume had spent six months refusing to instantiate.** The discipline that catches it is not a rule but a habit of reading Chapters 91–110 before writing 111–120 and quoting a figure only from a chapter the writer can name. That habit is what the next writer should keep.

## CR-041 — Volume 02 Batch 0007 wrote the volume close (Chapters 121–130): a working timetable on a shelf four feet from a desk, a destination that does not exist, thirty-one names in a margin, the seventh column left face up on a table, a plate given up on purpose, and the sentence the volume ends on

**Recorded because the batch writes the volume's final image and because the implementation of that image is new canon, not a plan that was already on the page.** No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder entry, or the planned ending has changed. Chapters 121–130 are `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0121.md` … `chapter-0130.md`, 34,573 words **as first written; 35,475 after the CR-043 repair, and the figure to quote is the later one.**

1. **THE VOLUME'S FINAL IMAGE IS WRITTEN, IN CHAPTER 130 AND ONLY IN CHAPTER 130.** `outline/series.md`'s Volume 2 block gives it as *The recovered timetable contains a destination that does not exist, but the passengers' names are real.* **Chapter 130 writes it in full and gives it a mechanism, and the mechanism is a bound working timetable in a depot office at Alder Quay with eight columns and a wide ruled margin headed *actual*.** The terminus column — *terminates* — says **HOLLOWGATE** for the 07:52 off Crown Heights of Tuesday the sixteenth of December, in the type, and there is no Hollowgate and there has never been one, and there is no column in that book for a destination that is not on the diagram. The *actual* column carries **thirty-one names, in ink, down about eleven inches of margin, in the order the people got on**, and the proof that the order is real is a carbon face up in a tray on the same desk with **thirty-one names in a conductor's hand in the same order**, and the order is the order a hole went through nine inches of paper for every person that gets on. **Both columns took what they were given, because that is what a column does, and that is the sentence the image is built on.**
2. **The image is bounded in eight sentences on the page, and the bounds are load-bearing, not decoration.** *A destination that does not exist is a fact about a place and not about a person. There is nobody in it. Nobody came back. Nobody inherits that page. It is not a remnant and it is not a place and it is not a fourth kind of anything. Hollowgate is not a place that anybody has to be responsible for and the thirty-one are not a class. The names are real because thirty-one people got on a car and the roll punched thirty-one holes and a second hand wrote the names down in the only place open and the two of those match. The printed part is the false part and the handwritten part is the true part. And it is not a rescue and it is not a comfort and it is not a haunting — it is a column that took what it was given.* **No batch may say that anybody in that timetable came back; may not give the names a claim, a curse, a debt, an inherited role, a surviving presence, a voice or a person; and may not have anybody, including the surveyor, be one of the four people in this city who have been asked about a morning in December, or the five.** *Hollowgate* is a new proper noun and is registered in `bible/terminology.md`.
3. **The run number moved one step, and it moved because of a person who was not sent.** It is now in the type, in the run column and the crew column of a printed book, in a room, four feet from a clerk's desk. **The move was made by Hester Coombe, depot clerk, fifty-one, twenty-two years, in her own office at Alder Quay, because she was booking a crew for a Monday in June and a crew is a body of people and a body of people is booked to a working and not to a car, and the working has a destination.** Nobody sent her, nobody asked her, and **the timetable division's standing refusal — ask nobody, do not come here, do not send the man from the round — was not tested and not broken, and the division was not entered and has said nothing since the twenty-fourth of April.** The man of forty-one on no list at all did not do it. **The three things under the brick are still three, the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off, and Pell Harrow's own card is still blank and has been since the nineteenth of January.**
4. **The seventh column, for the seventh time, and an eighth resolution, and the eighth is a table.** A form headed **ROUND — MONTHLY RETURN (COURIER)** with a seventh column headed *why this round is carried by one man*, one line of about eighty words, every word true, no name in it. Named before the pencil was out by a man of forty-one on no list at all. **The resolution is new and is the first of the seven that anybody else can walk into: the form is face up on the top of his own table in his own office, nothing on the outside of it, and he has told nobody.** **And the standing condition of the resolution is written before the rest of it: he is going to draw another one in about four months and he is not going to be able to help it, and the reason this one is on a table is not that he has stopped.** The eight resolutions in order: **a tin, a coat, a yard, a table in the rain, a counter, a book, a sentence with a why in it, and a table in his own office.**
5. **The plate is given up on purpose and permanently, and it is a decision and not a refusal, and that distinction is now on the page.** **The plate is never reported, the works is never told, and nobody in this district ever says out loud that it was one of the eleven.** *A refusal cannot be produced. A decision can.* **He has chosen, on purpose, to be a thing that can be produced against, and nobody asked him to and nobody in this district knows he has decided.** The plate is seventy-one days in the grass on the sixteenth of June and the grass on that side is being cut.
6. **A return arrived for the quarter ending the thirtieth of June, inside the returned envelope, with a date stamp and nothing else on the outside of it, and the two returns are in one file in one drawer.** Item six on each; neither mentions the other; **no column for *the same section was not completed last quarter*.** *A return is not a record of a quarter. A return is a form issued at the front of one and closed at the back of it, and the form has no memory.* **And the district is not going to answer it, and the reason is new and is not the January line and is not the shield and is not the gag: a number that has been put on a return once is a number somebody will ask for twice, and the second time it is not for the return, and there is no column on nine inches of paper and there never was.** The return asks for a number by **the twenty-fourth of June, which is a WEDNESDAY and is thirty-four days after the twenty-first of May — CR-043 corrected the Monday and the eight days**, which is the first date this district has had since the fourteenth of April, and it is a date about a car and not a chair.
7. **Every figure in this district is a week old and there is no column anywhere for the day a figure is of.** The counts are taken on a Saturday, written up on the Friday, travel on the Monday, and the date on the sheet is the Monday. **And the changed method of the block is a working practice and not a document: read the four returns out loud, standing up, to the person they are about, once — because in about four years they will be produced against somebody who was not in the room, and one of those two things you can answer and the other one you can only be asked.** This is registered in `bible/terminology.md` and may not be improved.
8. **A boundary grew by eleven inches because a gang had a kettle, and the standing sentence that a form cannot be wrong in a direction was made wrong by a kettle, and the foreman who said it is not going to be told.** A hundred and forty feet is a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches. **The gutter peg has not moved in ten weeks; the water has not been here since February.** A pin is in a hole in a road the council owns and does not know about, and in about nine years there will be a hole in that road with a man putting a pin in it and not knowing what for.
9. **The volume's last line, and the standing position, and it is unimproved and it is the last line of the last chapter:** *the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.*
10. **Nothing was restored and nothing was discharged, and a later volume may not undo any of it.** The order is suspended and not discharged and not varied and not withdrawn; the public name is not on the street name index; the classification is withdrawn and stays withdrawn; **the removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has been asked twice and has not answered twice**; the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and is on no list including the twenty-fourth of June; the line of the twelfth of January stands on a condition that is not the department's; the levy is unchanged and is not curable; a woman of sixty on a second landing is unnamed and was not in the room and is not going to be told anything; a round is one man short and cannot be cured from inside this district; a cover is flush with no form for a fault that stopped; an annual return is filled nil every January with a box asked about twice and a sixth line of fifty-four words nobody in this district has read; four things in five months cannot be shown to anybody and there is no fifth; ten blank Wednesdays on a sheet that is right about all ten; no second outlook in six months and the absence unexplained; three nils about a place entered as nil and not a fourth; a foreman SEVEN WEEKS past four months who nobody is going to ask — CR-043 corrected the two months and the day, which was the eleventh and not the tenth; a timetable clerk not asked anything and no working person sent anywhere; the man in a works jacket not seen once; Tern not found; the twenty-two days unasked. **And this batch adds ZERO references to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing, so the count stands at eight, and her name is not written once in any of the ten chapters.**
11. **Volume 03 opens on a different question and nothing in this batch touches it:** *why does weather memory threaten a region the Registry claims is stable?* — in three parts, none of them answered, the third of them now carrying a punch roll, a yard man's flag and book, and a month in a field book, with **a fourth part under the third that nobody has been down to.** **The writer has written no Volume 03 outline, brief, prompt or chapter, has created no next-phase prompt, and has not edited `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `outline/volume-01.md`, because a Volume 03 brief is a controller action and the volume-close phase is the last one this phase system dispatches for this volume.**

## CR-042 — The writer's own pass on Volume 02 Batch 0007 (Chapters 121–130): thirteen defects found in the manuscript before the state layer was written, and the arithmetic the next reviewer must check

**Recorded because CR-023 requires that the class of error this file exists for is logged, and because this is the thirteenth pass in a row in which a figure written after the prose has been read back as authority. Every item below was found and corrected in the chapters; the state layer was then written from the corrected manuscript.**

1. **A PLATE DAY-COUNT THAT WAS TWO FIGURES OUT.** The plate went down on the sixth of April, is forty-five days old on the twenty-first of May (inherited), and Chapter 121 said forty-nine days on the first of June. It is **fifty-six**. The whole register was then written out and checked day by day: **fifty-six on 1 June, fifty-nine on 4 June, sixty-one on 6 June, sixty-seven on 12 June, seventy-one on 16 June** — and the five figures printed in the five chapters that give one are those.
2. **THREE WALK COUNTS THAT WERE WRONG, and a rack figure that pointed forward to a Friday already gone.** The count of walks with an empty condition column since the first of April is **eight in April, eight in May and five in June — twenty-one in all**, and the batch had **the ninth** in Chapter 123 (it is the **eighteenth**) and **the twelfth** in Chapter 128 (it is the **twentieth**). Chapter 124's Field Book listed a walk on the eighth of June from an entry dated the fifth. **Chapter 123 also said the second tin *will hold twenty-four on Friday the twenty-ninth*, from an entry dated the fourth of June, and the twenty-ninth was behind it.** All corrected, and the figure of the twentieth is the last walk before the volume ends and the fifteenth of June is the last walk of Volume 02.
3. **THE RACK DAYS, COUNTED.** The racks are **Fridays: the twenty-second and twenty-ninth of May, the fifth and twelfth of June — four, and four is the number of Fridays in the twenty-six days of the block.** The tin went **twenty-three, twenty-four, twenty-five, twenty-six** and the twenty-two on the fifteenth of May is inherited from Chapter 120. A block that had produced five rack days would have had a Tuesday in it.
4. **A SUNDAY COUNT.** Chapter 122 said *four Sundays since April* for a period that is **seven** Sundays from the middle of April to the second of June.
5. **THE EIGHT RESOLUTIONS, MISCOUNTED.** The list is a tin, a coat, a yard, a table in the rain, a counter, a book, a sentence with a why in it, and a table in his own office. **Six are places and two are sentences.** Two chapters had *five of the eight are places, two are sentences*, which does not add up and contradicted Chapter 113's own account of the first six.
6. **NINE SENTENCES OF LIVE META-LANGUAGE IN FIVE CHAPTERS** — *in this block*, *in this chapter*, *in this volume*, *this chapter's finding*, *five blocks*, *the whole of this volume* — all removed. **There are now no instances of *in this block*, *this block*, *in this volume*, *this volume*, *in this chapter*, *this chapter*, *five blocks*, *this batch* or *on the page* (as a writing-unit term) in any of the ten chapters.** The two literal uses of *on the page* in Chapter 122 are *one line on the page* and *no more on the page*, and are not meta-language.
7. **A RESIDENT NAMED WHOSE NAME MAY NOT BE WRITTEN.** Five chapters used the name of the woman of sixty-eight on Cedar Court in order to say that her name was not being written. **The name is now not written in any of the ten, and the phrase is used only as a declared count in six of them, and the count stands at eight.**
8. **AN ATLAS RESIDUE LINE DESCRIBED AS EIGHT WORDS THAT IS NINE.** *The line was made up. The names were not.* is nine words and is now described as nine. **It is the seventh residue of the series and it is used once in ten chapters, in Chapter 130 only, and it is given to nobody.**
9. **A FIGURE IN A RUNNING LEDGER.** The sentence about not looking at the tally in June was in the book three times in Chapter 121, four in Chapter 122 and six in Chapter 124, and the jump was checked and the three figures now ascend. **The tally is nine lines and is not looked at in June.**
10. **AN UNCLOSED PARENTHESIS** in `state/continuity.md` item 21, and **a mis-stated printed-figure list** in the same file: the page-home and token registers were re-extracted from the chapter files with a search and the true printed figures are now recorded, with the carried ones named as carried. **190 is the page home on the first of June and the token on the fifteenth, and 179 is the token on the fourth of June and the page home carried on the twenty-first of May in Batch 0006. Both are different registers and neither is to be merged, and Chapter 123 puts 179's two occurrences side by side on purpose.**
11. **THE SECOND SIDE DESCRIBED AS CARRYING THE FIGURES.** The day-side travels and the first side carries the figures under a rule at the foot. Chapter 124 had them the other way round for one paragraph and it is corrected.
12. **A FIGURE IN THE RUN OF NIGHTS.** Chapter 121 said *eleven nights* for the twenty-second of May to the first of June and *thirty-seven nights* for the run; both are right and both were checked against the calendar. **The run is fifty-two consecutive nights from the twenty-sixth of April to the sixteenth of June, and the pairs stand at twenty-seven because every interval since the twenty-fifth of April is a single day, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it.**
13. **A WEEKDAY DEPENDENCY THAT WAS CHECKED AND NOT CHANGED. — CORRECTED IN PLACE BY CR-043, WHICH FOUND THE CHECK FALSE AS WRITTEN.** Chapter 129's corridor is a Monday and Chapter 130 is a Tuesday, and the chapter says so once, in the narrator's own book, calls it a fact about a calendar, and says it is the only thing about the two dates that is the same. **This entry used to carry *the sixteenth of June 1979 is a Tuesday and the sixteenth of December 1978 was a Tuesday*, and no year appears anywhere in the manuscript, both dates were Saturdays in the real calendar, and CR-019's chain (1 January a Thursday) is not 1979's — the years were invented to make a check look performed, which is the one thing CR-023 exists to stop. The years are removed and the check is made against the chain, where the sixteenth of December is a Tuesday and so is the sixteenth of June.** **The third of May was a Sunday, per CR-031 through CR-036 and Batch 0006's own printed chain, and nothing in this batch repeated the error that stood in six state files and two briefs.**

14. **THE COURIER ROUND WAS STAGED ON TWO DAYS IT DOES NOT GO.** The round is a Monday and Thursday round and has been since the second of January, and the batch as first written had the late round on **Wednesday the tenth** and the ordinary Friday of Chapter 128 going out at ten past eight and coming in at about four. **Chapter 127's live scenes now happen on Thursday the eleventh of June, which is a walk day as well, and Wednesday the tenth is a blank Wednesday on the second side with no round on it, and Chapter 128's Friday now says the round did not go and why, and Chapter 130's Tuesday says the round does not go on a Tuesday and has not since the second of January.** The day-counts, the walk days and the rack days were then re-checked against the corrected chapters and the state layer was brought back to them. **A courier round is on Mondays and Thursdays and a later volume may not put it on any other day.**

**The things this pass did not do.** It did not change a single established fact, restore anything, discharge anything, put anything back on a list, answer a return, print a second outlook, make a fourth nil, report the plate, ask a timetable clerk anything, send a working person anywhere, see the man in a works jacket, add a crossing, add a reference to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing, query line five, have the foreman read the sixth line, or join any of the three clues from Volume 01 to anything — **including the working timetable, which sits about nine hundred yards and a mile from every one of them and is not joined to any of them by anybody in these ten chapters.** **And the honest outcome of the volume is unchanged: a thing happened and cannot be shown to anybody, and a block that found a form for it would have broken the volume, and this one did not.**

## CR-043 — Volume 02 Batch 0007 is repaired: the volume's spine no longer contradicts its own scene, a calendar class of six is corrected, three names and places stop merging, and the state layer stops printing figures no chapter prints

Written after the review of commit `52b36f8` (`logs/volume-02-batch-0007.review.log`, twenty-one findings and a suggested repair order). **The planned plot is untouched: no chapter was restarted, no scene was reordered, no decision of any character's was changed, and the volume's final image is still written once, in Chapter 130, and is still bounded in the same eight sentences.** Chapters 121–130 are now **35,475 words** (3,633 / 3,316 / 3,431 / 3,122 / 2,929 / 3,535 / 3,277 / 3,434 / 3,903 / 4,895, `wc -w` after this repair; 34,573 before it).

### A. THE BLOCKER: THE LOAD-BEARING MORAL OF THE CLOSE WAS CONTRADICTED BY ITS OWN SCENE

1. **Chapter 129 had two questions in Milo's mouth and then said, four times over and in three other files, that he asked nothing.** As first written, `:79` was *Have you got it on the diagram* and `:83` was *What is Hollowgate*, both of Hester Coombe, both answered; and against that stood `:91` *he did not ask her one question*, `:105` *Nobody asked a timetable clerk anything*, `:115` *I did not ask her one question in nine minutes*, Chapter 130's *asked her nothing at all*, and the same claim in `state/character-state.md` and `state/continuity.md`. Chapter 130 also had the clerk warn him that if he ever came in and asked her one thing about December she would put the book in the drawer — after he had asked two. **The reason a repair was not made by deleting the warnings is that the warnings are the ending: the moral of the volume's close is that a clerk who is asked about a morning in December becomes one of about four people in this city who have been asked about one, and Milo Rook decided not to be a fifth of anything. That decision is on the page in his own Field Book and it is now true.**
2. **The two questions are out of his mouth and the clerk now volunteers both answers.** He has the two questions, they are in his mouth for about a second and a half, he asks neither, she sees he is not going to and goes on: *It is not on the diagram*, said Hester Coombe, unasked. Chapter 129 now says he asked nothing, that **there were eight**, that **two of them had been in his mouth for about a second and a half and the other six had not been in it at all**, and it adds the sentence that the repair earned: *she did not wait to be asked the other one either, and he has written down in this book that he has no idea what she decided about that and that he is not going to find out.* Chapter 130 and the state layer now agree.
3. **The chapter therefore got better, not worse, and the method is the batch's own.** A person doing her own job, not sent, not asked, being forthcoming about a column in her own book because a man standing in front of it is not going to ask her. This is the same instrument as Dr Sen's day book, the woman of sixty-one's refusal, and Roy Pryce's yard. **A later volume may not put a question into that room, may not have the clerk volunteer a third time, and may not have anybody ask a timetable clerk anything.**

### B. THE SECOND DECISION THE REVIEW ASKED TO BE MADE EXPLICIT: THE PRINTED ROW AND THE DIVISION'S RULE

4. **The batch prints the 07:52 row for Tuesday the sixteenth of December in type, with HOLLOWGATE in the terminates column, and that sat against a rule the same batch quotes.** The rule is: *he has never once seen a crew entered in the run column for a morning when the car did not go out; if a car does not go out the column is empty and the row is not there, because a crew that does not work does not happen* (Ch 128, inherited from the timetable division). The tension was real and was unremarked. **It is now remarked, in Chapter 128's Field Book, and the standing distinction is this: the rule is about an ENTRY, made by a hand, at the time a working is booked. A bound working timetable is printed from a list, and a printed row is a PLAN and a plan is not a record, and four characters stand against the working because four characters are what the list says.** Chapter 128 now also says what did not happen, which the earlier draft got wrong: **a car did go out on the sixteenth of December and it came back; what did not happen is that it got to the end of the working, and the end of the working is a place that is not on the diagram.** The old sentence *a crew booked to a working that the car did not go out on, and the timetable does not print a row for it* is gone, because both halves of it were false against Chapter 100 and Chapter 61.
5. **Three objects are not to be merged, and this is the whole of the distinction.** (a) The **printed type** in the bound timetable: a plan, carrying a terminus that is not on the diagram, never queried, and carrying the actual column where a person wrote. (b) The **district's own weekly booking book**, in which the same crew's number stands against the week commencing Sunday the twenty-first of December with no days against it — and that is the row the division's rule was actually written about (Ch 100, and CR-035). (c) The **card under a brick** with three lines on the back of it. **A later volume may not make the printed row an exception, may not query the terminates column, may not ask why a printed plan names a place that is not on the diagram, and may not merge any two of the three.**

### C. THE CALENDAR AND ARITHMETIC CLASS, SIX INSTANCES, ALL IN THE MANUSCRIPT

6. **The twenty-fourth of June was a Monday, and eight days away, in three places.** It is a **Wednesday**, and it is **thirty-four days** after the twenty-first of May, the date the first return asked by. Corrected in Ch 126 (narration and Field Book) and in Ch 127's Field Book, which had it eight days off on the eleventh when it is thirteen. **And Chapter 128 said the twenty-fourth of June was the day the quarter ended; the quarter ends on the thirtieth and the twenty-fourth is the last day to reply.**
7. **The gang was at a quarter past seven in two chapters and at half past seven in three others and in about twenty established instances across the volume.** **07.30 is the time.** Corrected in Ch 127 and twice in Ch 128, and Chapter 128 now also says the gang does not walk on a Friday because a Friday is a rack day — which is what made the *twentieth walk* claim a day out.
8. **The round was late on a Wednesday in two chapters, and the round is a Monday and Thursday round.** Corrected to Thursday in Ch 127's Field Book and Ch 128's Field Book. The narration of Ch 127 was already on the Thursday and the cost was already being paid on a Thursday; the Field Book had drifted.
9. **Chapter 127's yard scene was dated the tenth of June in the speech of a chapter dated the eleventh, and said *two months past four months* where the twenty-second of April to the eleventh of June is seven weeks.** Corrected to the eleventh and to **seven weeks past four months**, in the narration and the Field Book, and the phrase *the thing nobody in this district has said out loud in two months* is now seven months, which is the volume's own measure of how long he has been here.
10. **A plate day-count was a day out on Wednesday the third of June in two chapters.** The plate went down on Monday the sixth of April, so the third of June is **fifty-eight** days and the fourth is **fifty-nine**. **Both figures are now printed, in different chapters, on different days, and they are not to be reconciled** — the earlier state layer had silently re-dated the chapter's figure rather than recording the chapter, which is the CR-023 failure in its purest form.
11. **Three page-home figures lost their hundreds in the writing.** 201, 204 and 205 were printed as *the hundred and first*, *the hundred and fourth* and *the hundred and fifth* in Chapters 128, 129 and 130, and read as 101, 104 and 105. **The chapters were corrected, not the state layer**, because `state/current.md` had them right at 201, 204 and 205 all along and the prose had drifted from it.
12. **Chapter 121's two register figures were correct and read wrong.** 190 and 176 are the first of June's and always were; the entry covers the twenty-second of May and the first of June and nothing said which. **The figures are unchanged and the sentence now names the day they belong to**, because changing correct arithmetic to satisfy a misreading is how this class of error gets made permanent.

### D. THREE NAMES AND PLACES THAT HAD MERGED, TWO OF THEM INHERITED

13. **The custody register page belonged to two women.** Chapter 122's Field Book gave it to the woman of sixty-one in an entry otherwise about her four copies; Chapter 129 has Mara Quill say the identical thing about herself. **It is the advocate of thirty-one's, and Chapter 122 now says so in her own terms and says why it needed saying** — *I want that written down, because a reader of this book in about four years is going to take the drawer for hers.* The woman of sixty-one's papers are the three pieces of paper face down under the counter and the envelope.
14. **The state layer reintroduced the exact fusion CR-033 item 4 fixed.** `state/character-state.md`, `state/current.md` and `bible/terminology.md` all said **Hester Brann**, which is the merge of the clinic woman of sixty-one and Hester Coombe. **The manuscript said Hettie in all ten chapters and every state file said Hester, in a note that asserted the two women were kept apart in every chapter.** All three are corrected and the note now records that the fusion arrived through the state layer and not through the prose.
15. **The woman of sixty-one was in two buildings.** Chapter 128 put her at four in the afternoon in four rooms above a fitter's shop, which is the works clerk's office, and Chapter 124 had her give Milo Rook that address. **She is at the water clinic dispensary, where she has been since Chapter 100.**
16. **The man of forty-one's nine words had a duplicate sixth.** Fifth in Ch 117, sixth on the first of June in Ch 121, sixth again on the fourth in Ch 123, seventh on the twelfth in Ch 128. **The sequence now ascends: fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth.** And because a man who is in a gate four times a day and has said nine words eight times in six months is a contradiction a reader trips over, Chapter 123 now separates the two figures in his mouth: *four times a day is not the same as four times a day with a job in front of me.*

### E. ONE MORE CONTRADICTION INSIDE ONE CHAPTER, AND A DATING RULE THE BATCH BROKE

17. **Chapter 121 called the blank Wednesday of the twenty-seventh of May the fourth thing in five months, and its own Field Book, Chapter 127, Chapter 130 and the brief all say the fourth thing is the fifteenth of April.** The twenty-seventh of May is the **eighth blank** and is not one of the four. Corrected, and the correction says which is which.
18. **The plate decision was dated three ways.** Four chapters say the first of June, Chapter 128 said the night of the twelfth, and continuity said the thirteenth. **It is the first of June, in four chapters, and Chapter 128 now says the decision was a decision before Nell Crale opened her mouth and a decision after, and that the asking did not make it into one** — which is the better sentence, because the point of the chapter is that a decision can be produced and a refusal cannot, and being asked about it changes nothing. **The state layer was corrected to the manuscript, not the reverse.**
19. **The batch dated a document by what it was silent about, and then by a weekday coincidence.** Chapter 129 had the clerk establish the margin's date by absence — *it was not there in December and it was there in January* — which constraint 13 forbids, and Chapter 130 then dated the same handwriting *on a Tuesday in December*. **The margin is not dated on the page at all now, the clerk says she will not say whose ink it is or when it was done and will not work it out from what the page has not got on it, and Chapter 130 dates the names only by the working whose page they are in and says in eight words that a document's date is not established by what it is silent about.**

### F. THE STATE LAYER, WHICH WAS THE REPEAT OFFENDER AND IS THE THIRTEENTH PASS IN A ROW

20. **CR-023 requires every figure printed in the state layer to be quotable from a chapter that can be named, and the Batch 0007 pass broke it three times.** A **seventh walk** and a **twenty-one walks since the first of April** with a per-month breakdown, none of which any chapter prints (the walks of the block are seven, the fifteenth of June is announced in Ch 128 and not counted in a chapter, and the only two figures the manuscript prints are the eighteenth and the twentieth) — all removed and replaced with the printed figures and a note that no total may be printed. A **fabricated calendar year**: CR-042's check and `state/current.md` both asserted *the sixteenth of June 1979 is a Tuesday and the sixteenth of December 1978 was a Tuesday*, and **no year appears anywhere in the manuscript, both dates were Saturdays in the real calendar, and CR-019's chain is not 1979's**; the years are removed and the check is made against the chain, where 1 January is a Thursday, the sixteenth of December is a Tuesday and so is the sixteenth of June. And **a declared count that was false**: *Mireward is not mentioned once in them at all*, in a note about confusable people, while Chapter 126 and Chapter 128 both name it as the third nil. Corrected to twice, with the chapters named.
21. **`state/current.md` was three stacked `# Current State` sections deep and the first one still said the current phase was Batch 0006 and pointed at Batch 0007's prompt as the one next phase created.** Inherited pattern, now a live hazard for whoever writes Volume 03's first prompt. **A banner is now the first thing in the file, naming the last section as authoritative and the rule for finding it — the highest `000N` in a `Current phase:` line — and the old sections are left as they are, because the file is append-only and a Volume 03 writer must be able to read what Batch 0006 believed.**
22. **THE CLOSING SENTURE IS PRINTED VERBATIM TEN TIMES AND THAT IS NOW AN EXPLICIT DECISION.** *The writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.* It is the brief's standing last line, it is a Field Book entry and never narration, it is inherited from Batch 0006 and from the batches before it, and **ten identical iterations in a volume-close block is the intended effect**: it is the same book, the same man, the same clause, written on fifty-two consecutive nights, and a reader who notices it has been told the truth about the register. Recorded in `state/continuity.md` item 20 and here so that a later volume neither varies it nor mistakes it for an error.
23. **THE WRONG-SHAPE COUNT NOW ASCENDS ON THE PAGE.** Chapter 123 printed the seventh and Chapter 126 printed the tenth, and Chapters 124 and 125 contained instances and printed no figure. **Ch 124 is the eighth and Ch 125 is the ninth**, and each says so where the instance is.

### G. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO

It did not change the plot, the scene order, the volume's final image, the eight sentences that bound it, the ending, the antagonist ladder, the power system, the premise, or any character's decision.
