## CR-044 — Volume 03 is planned and Batch 0001 (Chapters 131–140) is written: *Somebody Goes There First*

**Recorded because the batch opens a new volume, because a volume outline did not exist for Volume 03 and now does, and because the implementation of the volume's first movement is new canon. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder entry, or the planned ending has changed. No new grammar, no new faction, no new antagonist and no new final enemy has been added. `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were NOT edited, and the ending is unchanged.**

**THE CONTROLLER QUESTION IS RESOLVED AND IT WAS A CONTROLLER QUESTION.** The Volume 02 close (CR-041 item 11, CR-043 item G) recorded that *a Volume 03 brief is a controller action and the writer has not created one and may not*. **The controller has now issued that instruction directly, and this phase is the result: `outline/volume-03.md` was written, Chapters 131–140 were written, and `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` is the one next phase created.** Nothing was dispatched beyond that.

### A. WHAT WAS WRITTEN

1. **`outline/volume-03.md`, *A City Made of Weather — Chapters 131–200*,** written BEFORE the chapters and is the authority for the remaining six blocks. It fixes the central pressure from `outline/series.md`, the region, the four new working people, a seven-block escalation sequence (0001 *Somebody Goes There First* / 0002 *The Order Is Not A Weather Report* / 0003 *Four Copies Of A Bell* / 0004 the midpoint, *What The Rain Is Carrying* / 0005 *Two Locks And A Choice* / 0006 the climax, *The Village Or The Archive* / 0007 the close, *Somebody Goes There First*), the relationship milestone 3, the power costs, and the volume's final image — **a village school paints its route on the wall in several colours, none of them official** — which belongs to Chapter 200 and is not written.
2. **Chapters 131–140**, `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0131.md` … `chapter-0140.md`, **27,939 words** (3,283 / 3,332 / 2,825 / 2,426 / 2,183 / 3,112 / 2,740 / 1,857 / 2,819 / 3,362, `wc -w` after the writer's own repair pass). Titles: *A Sentence Where The Machine Prints A Dash* / *Four Lines About Nine Feet Of Drain* / *Half A Bridge Is A Structure And The Other Half Is A Route* / *A Column That Wants A Name And There Is Nobody To Put There* / *A Schoolroom With No Address And Nineteen Children In It* / *A Man Walks Him In And Nine Rings On A Post* / *Nine Tides Is Not Nine Years* / *A Sentence With A Number On It* / *A Man Who Is Not Paid For Holding The Water Down* / *No Seventh Column Before The First Of September*.
3. **The volume's first block does not resolve the volume.** The Rainway is not named. No Weather remnant is identified. The Registry's proposal to sacrifice outlying villages has not arrived. The First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the Quieting are not mentioned. The village-or-archive choice is not set up. **The office of the First Surveyor is not named in these ten chapters and did not act.**

### B. WHAT IS NEW CANON

4. **SIX WORDS ON A DISTRICT RETURNS CARD.** On Wednesday the seventeenth of June a district returns card for **MIREWARD** came off the machine with **six words in a hand in the reason column, where the machine prints a dash: *Asked. Nothing come.*** No — the words are ***Asked. Nothing came.*** **It is the first sentence anybody has ever written in that column, and it is NOT a reason for a nil.** The mechanism that gave it a reference number is registered and is municipal, not magical: **a card put down face up is re-run once a week on a line at the foot of a run sheet headed *re-run — face up only*, and the run sheet is about a printing error, so the sentence now exists on a second piece of paper with a reference in a series running from about 41,000, of which about four hundred go out of that room a week — and the hand is not on the form, and the machine has taken the hand over.** **A sentence is a thing a person can be sent to look at; a sentence with a reference on it is a thing a person can be asked for.** Registered in `bible/terminology.md` as the Volume 03 local register, first block.
5. **THE LAW OF THE PLACE, and who said it first in the manuscript.** *A place is not stable because nothing has happened in it. A place is stable because there is nobody writing down what happens in it.* **Said by Percy, fifty-eight, on a raised path at about eleven on the eighteenth of June, and Milo Rook had already written the same sentence in his own book in February out of a blackboard in a drainage section, and neither man knows the other said it, and he is not going to tell him.** This is the volume's spine and **it may not be improved, moved into a yard, attributed to anybody else, or made his.**
6. **A RESIDENT WITNESS IS A PERSON ON A LIST.** The standing ground for entering a district as nil — *no maintained seam, no recorded loss, no resident witness, no schedule* — **turns over in this block**: there has not been a person on a list in Mireward since about 1911, so the district has been entered as containing no persons, and the entry has never been wrong about a fact and has been completely wrong about a place for sixty years, and a district that is not a place cannot be frightened. **The eleven streets and Mireward are the same kind of place and one of them has stationery.** This is a new reading of an existing fact in `bible/world.md` and does not alter it.
7. **THE COST OF THE BLOCK IS ABOUT A WAGE.** **There is no bus and there has not been one since about 1961, because a bus wants a place to stop and a place to stop is a rateable thing.** Two motor vehicles a week in Mireward — a flatbed on a Friday morning that goes back on the Friday afternoon, a lorry on a Thursday — **and neither waits, and there is one tractor and its owner will not be named.** **A person with an address can be in a place on a Tuesday because being in a place is not a day off; a person without an address cannot, because the being there is a day off and the day off is somebody else's morning.** **And a ratepayer is a person at an address, so a man with no address cannot be a ratepayer, cannot be billed, cannot be employed and cannot be written to.** Both are designs, not injustices, and nobody has done anything wrong, and neither is curable from inside either district.
8. **THE TENTH CROSSING, dated and costed, and the first one that is not a street.** Thursday the twenty-fifth of June, in at 14.20, out at 15.02, **forty-two minutes**, from the old ford in Mireward, with all four conditions of October said out loud to a drain. **Garn Ollerton, thirty-seven, ferry pilot, walked him in and out; Sabra Trewin, thirty-four, schoolteacher, held the count IN HER HEAD and is not a box; the time was stated before he went; and the clinic in the morning was Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, a nurse, on the bank from about half past ten, unasked, on her day off, paid by a clinic with no record of where she went.** Read out in a yard at about half past five on the twenty-sixth with the tally, which now has **TEN LINES**. **Milo Rook is across ten times; the remembered-route list is ELEVEN; the ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained.** **What was inside: it had no edge and the far side was more of the same; the ford has been dry since 1919 and the water was up to a tidemark two feet under the parapet with three marks on it, the top one about nine years newer than the bottom one, which is wrong and is Chapter 137; the smell of a fen in a dry week was about four times what it was outside and came from underneath the whole length of it; and at about four hundred yards along it was a post with a school bell on it and nineteen marks cut into it, two of them still pale on the Wednesday, and the bell had been rung nine times, not while he was there. He did not touch the post. There was nothing else in forty-two minutes and he came out with less than he went in with, and he has no demand, no service and no failure out of it yet.** **A later batch may not make the seam a haunting, a claim, a ghost, a voice or a person; may not date the bell or name who rang it; and Batch 0002 may not resolve the seam.**
9. **THE PAIRS MOVED: TWENTY-SEVEN TO TWENTY-EIGHT, for the first time since the twenty-fifth of April — seventy-seven days.** The interval is **seven days, from the eighteenth of June to the twenty-fifth**, and there is **no entry for the nineteenth, the twentieth, the twenty-first, the twenty-second, the twenty-third or the twenty-fourth**. **THE NINETEENTH WAS A DECISION AND THE OTHER FIVE WERE NOT**, and the chapter says so. The list is now two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, **THREE sevens**, six fours. **A pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened**, and the run of fifty-two consecutive nights is over and what replaced it is, in his own words, **a habit, and a habit is not a discipline and a habit is not a method.**
10. **THE NINTH RESOLUTION IS THE FIRST ONE THAT IS NOT A PLACE, AND IT DID NOT WORK.** The **eighth seventh column** is headed *why this district is entered as nil*, is about a hundred and ten words, and is **the first of the eight whose answer is not a person**: a column that wants a name, and there is nobody to give it one, because a district entered as nil has had no resident since 1969. He handed the paper to the person it was about; Garn Ollerton refused in about nine seconds and told him who to ask; Percy refused in four seconds; **it is face down under a Field Book in a bag, and a person is not a custody, and the keeping is a third thing and the third thing is the one he actually does.** The eight of his own resolutions, in order, are unchanged.
11. **HE GAVE UP THE SEVENTH COLUMN UNTIL THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER, in four lines of his own hand on the facing page of the Field Book on Wednesday the first of July — the first thing he has ever put on that page that is not a measurement — and wrote the cost out nine minutes later.** **What stops on the first of September is him and nothing else stops on the first of September.** **A foreman is ten weeks past four months on the first of July and eleven weeks on the eleventh, and is not going to be asked, and that silence was the yard's and has now got him in it in his own handwriting, and he cannot get it out of his handwriting — and that is the most self-serving sentence he has written in seven months and it is dressed up as a discipline and it is not one.** **A later volume may not break the line, may not report that he broke it, and may not treat it as a resolution.**
12. **A LINE IN A RETURN THAT MARA QUILL WILL NOT DESCRIBE, which is the seed of Batch 0002 and the only thing in these ten chapters that is about land.** *There is a line in a return that I reconcile on the last Thursday of every month which has nothing to do with you and nothing to do with a tram, and I have looked at it, and I am not going to tell you what it says, and I am telling you that I have looked at it.* He asked what kind of line. ***That is the second time, and I said once.*** **He did not ask again.** **She will not describe it in Batch 0002 either, and Volume 03's midpoint is the block where the public mourning is named, and the land question is Mara's and is not resolved in Batch 0002.**
13. **THE PROTECTIVE THINGS ARE REGISTERED AS NON-INSTRUMENTS: a pad, a roll in a teacher's head, a ferry route, a tidemark, a bell, a sluice, a smell.** **A later block may not put any of them on paper, may not make any of them a form, and may not build an instrument out of the fact that there is none.** **Nine tides is not nine years**, and it is **the first finding in seven months that runs the other way — every conclusion this district has produced has been that something was going to be found out about a person, and this one is that a thing is safe because there is nothing to find, and a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after.** **A later block may not turn that into an instrument and may not improve it.**
14. **HALF A BRIDGE IS A STRUCTURE AND THE OTHER HALF IS A ROUTE**, and the maintenance is on the wrong half: a schedule of works with a column for *side*, every entry CITY except two, and the two say EXT, and the key says *see the drainage board*, **abolished in about 1968, functions transferred to a body called the fen commission, and there is no fen commission on any letterhead in this city and has not been one since 1968.** **A handrail post is four inches short, replaced like for like in about 1979, and about nine families navigate by it, and there is no form in this city for a thing nine families use that is not a building.** **And a boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and nobody agreed at this bridge: two things stopped, and neither of them was a person, and the water agreed with both of them and the water does not keep minutes.** He crossed it in about nine seconds at ten past seven on the nineteenth of June and **nobody stopped him and there was nobody on either end** — the first boundary in seven months that he did not not-cross. **The hundred and forty feet and eleven inches and the kettle are inherited and are not undone, and the two are not joined.**
15. **THE NINTH ATLAS RESIDUE: *A dry week is a promise about water.*** Eight words, Chapter 136, on a bank, in his own book, **given to nobody and to be given to nobody. NO YEAR, NO NAME AND NO SPEAKER IS ATTACHED TO IT ANYWHERE AND NONE MAY BE.** The seven inherited residues are unchanged and were not restated.
16. **THE DAY-COUNTS AND THE CALENDAR ARE REGISTERED IN `bible/terminology.md` and are quotable:** page home **206, 207, 214, 215, 216, 218, 219, 221** printed; token **192, 193, 200, 201, 202, 204, 205, 207** printed, **and 207 is the page home on the second of July and the token on the same day and the two must not be merged**; plate **72, 73, 80, 81, 82, 87**; condition column **134 days on the twenty-sixth of June and 137 on the twenty-ninth**; reason column **fifteen weeks**; foreman **ten weeks past four months on the first of July, eleven on the eleventh**; walks **the twenty-second to the twenty-sixth since the first of April**; racks **two Fridays, the nineteenth and the twenty-sixth of June**; second tin **twenty-seven, twenty-eight**. **NO YEAR APPEARS ANYWHERE IN VOLUME 03 AND NONE MAY BE ADDED.**

### C. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO, AND MAY NOT BE UNDONE

It did not resolve the volume, reach the Rainway, name a Weather remnant, name a grammar, name the First Atlas, the Singular Mode or the Quieting, supply a date for the Quieting, or set up the village-or-archive choice. It did not discharge, vary, withdraw, re-author or re-list anything in Volume 02, and **it did not restore the removed sentence, the public name or the classification.** It did not put the matter back on any list. It did not answer a return. It did not print a second outlook or explain its absence and did not have anybody ask for one in writing. It did not make a fourth nil. It did not report the plate. It did not put a word in the condition column about why. It did not have anybody stand in the weather on a Wednesday. It did not ask a timetable clerk anything or send a working person anywhere. It did not see the man in a works jacket. **It did not make a resident witness a person, did not date the bell, did not name who rang it, did not put anybody in the seam, and did not let the twenty-nine names or the thirty-one or Hollowgate be touched, queried or explained.** It did not query line five, did not have the foreman read the sixth line, and did not answer either query. It did not join any of the three clues from Volume 01 to anything, **including the two sets of marks on the bridge and the nineteen marks on the post, which are not a fourth clue and not a second instance of anything.** It did not add a reference to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing, **so the count stands at eight.** It did not name Soren Rook, Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable, Tarin Kest, or the office of the First Surveyor, and nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel. **It touched `state/phase-ledger.json` and nothing under `.opencode/`, `.github/`, `scripts/` or `AGENTS.md`, all of which are controller-owned, and it created exactly one next-phase prompt.**

### D. THE WRITER'S OWN PASS, THE FOURTEENTH IN A ROW, AND THE CLASS IT FOUND

1. **A TRAVEL-LOGIC CONTRADICTION ACROSS SIX CHAPTERS, and it is the finding a reviewer must start with.** As first written, Percy drove him out and back on the eighteenth, Milo went out on the flatbed on the Friday and came back on the Sunday, and Chapters 134 to 140 then had him in Mireward and in the city on the same days, and the Field Book entries had him asleep in Bridge Street and in Cobb Lock on the same nights. **The whole block was re-sequenced at the chapters: he leaves on the morning of the nineteenth and returns on the flatbed on the afternoon of the twenty-sixth, Percy's four-day week and the two vehicles are stated on the page, and Chapters 133 to 139 are now all in Mireward.** A reader who thinks he is in the city on the twenty-fifth of June will not find the crossing where the chapter says it is.
2. **A MAN WHO HAD NO BUSINESS ON A SATURDAY MORNING** — the mower man, who was put on the far side after Milo had already crossed.
3. **A SEVENTH COLUMN DESCRIBED AS THE EIGHTH ONE CHAPTERS BEFORE IT HAPPENED** — Chapter 132 said eight; the eighth is Chapter 134.
4. **A BEST-IN-X-MONTHS FIGURE** wrong in both directions, now *since the third of June*.
5. **A FOREMAN'S ELAPSED TIME** at **eight weeks** in Chapter 140 where twenty-second of April to the first of July is **ten**; now ten, with eleven on the eleventh of July.
6. **A QUERY DESCRIBED AS A THIRD FORM** going up in April where the record is a query coming **back down a second time** and a box going up empty a second time.
7. **A CONDITION-COLUMN DAY COUNT** of a hundred and thirty-eight where it is a hundred and thirty-seven.
8. **A RACK ON A DAY THAT WAS NOT A FRIDAY** in Chapter 140's closing; replaced with *a Friday had not been and a rack day is a Friday*.
9. **A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SIX PUTTING A PEG IN A HOLE IN A ROAD** on the back length, which is a different scene in a different chapter.
10. **A SELF-REFERENTIAL FIGURE** of *eleven hundred words* in Chapter 138 that no chapter supports.
11. **A DATE ESTABLISHED BY WHAT A SHEET IS SILENT ABOUT** in the tidemark correction — CR-023's constraint 13 — replaced with the whole of the finding.
12. **NINE SENTENCES OF LIVE META-LANGUAGE** across five chapters and **a hundred-and-thirty-nine-days figure that no chapter supports**; all gone. **There are now no instances of *in this block*, *this block*, *in this volume*, *this volume*, *in this chapter*, *this chapter*, *this batch* or *on the page* as a writing-unit term in any of the ten chapters.**
13. **A DRAFTING ARTEFACT** in Chapter 136 — *on a Wednesday morning — no. On a Thursday afternoon* — is gone.
14. **AND, IN THE STATE LAYER, THE CLASS THIS FILE EXISTS FOR CAUGHT IT TWICE IN THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY:** a fabricated detail in `state/continuity.md` item 27 that attached a year, a dead man and a hand to the Chapter 136 residue, none of which is in the manuscript. **The state layer was corrected to the manuscript, not the reverse, and CR-042's rule is applied to this file as well as to the chapters.**

## CR-045 — Two navigation and word-count corrections to the state layer after Volume 03 Batch 0001, in the class CR-023 exists to record, and neither touches a chapter

**Recorded separately from CR-044 because both defects are in the state layer and both were made by the phase that wrote the block, which is the first time in this repository that a phase has had to open a new volume and the first time the batch number stopped being monotonic.**

1. **`state/current.md`'s navigation banner was wrong and would have mis-dispatched the next block.** The banner, added by the CR-043 repair, told a reader that **the highest `000N` in a `Current phase:` line is the live one.** That was correct while every phase was in Volume 02. The live phase is now **`Volume 03, Batch 0001`**, and `0001` is LOWER than the `Volume 02, Batch 0007` section that precedes it, so a writer or a fixer following the banner would have read the Volume 02 volume close as current. **The banner now says: the authoritative section is the LAST `# Current State` heading in the file, and the batch number must not be used.** The four `Current phase:` lines in the file, in order, are Volume 02 Batch 0006, Volume 02 Batch 0006, Volume 02 Batch 0007, Volume 03 Batch 0001. **The old sections are left exactly as they are, because the file is append-only and a Volume 03 writer must be able to read what Batch 0006 believed.**
2. **A WORD-COUNT FIGURE WAS STALE IN THREE FILES.** The per-chapter figure for **Chapter 133** was written as **2,817** before the last repair to that chapter, which added eight words (a forward-tense fix to the Saturday's missing Field Book entry). The true figure is **2,825** and the batch total of **27,939** was always right. **CR-044's own word-count list, `state/batch-summaries.md` and `state/current.md` were corrected to the manuscript.** The rule of the class, restated: **a figure written after the prose and read back as authority is wrong, and the error class is not confined to dates — it is confined to figures, and the cheapest check in this repository is `wc -w` on the ten files and a diff against the three places the figure is printed.**
3. **AND ONE FIGURE THAT WAS FIXED THE OTHER WAY, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE CLASS.** On drafting CR-044 I had written a fabricated detail into `state/continuity.md` item 27 — a year, a dead man and a hand attached to the Chapter 136 Atlas residue, none of which is in the manuscript — and a false weekday in the Batch 0002 brief, **the eleventh of July called a Monday when the chain makes it a Saturday.** Both were caught and corrected in the state layer before the phase closed. **The eleven of July being a Saturday is not a repair, it is a fact, and it is now load-bearing: four months off Tuesday the twenty-second of December is about the twenty-second of April, and Saturday the eleventh of July is eighty days after it, which is eleven weeks and three days, and a Saturday is the day a foreman of thirty-one years is on a gate and not in a building, and that is why nobody has asked him.**
4. **WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO.** It did not change a chapter, a plot, a scene order, a character's decision, the ending, the antagonist ladder, the power system or the premise. It did not edit `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `outline/volume-01.md`, and it did not rewrite `outline/volume-03.md`. It did not touch `state/phase-ledger.json`, `.opencode/`, `.github/`, `scripts/` or `AGENTS.md`. It created exactly one next-phase prompt.

## CR-046 — Volume 03 Batch 0002 is written (Chapters 141–150): *The Order Is Not A Weather Report* — a printed land instrument wearing a weather coat, four printed grounds and no ground for *there is nothing wrong with it*, the eleven streets named on a printed side as not to be maintained after 31 August — a Monday, and itself a gang day, so the last walk of the season is on the thirty-first, the last rack day is Friday the twenty-eighth, and there is no sheet for the week of the thirty-first — a contract to a date, a stop that a man wrote about himself, five men and a bar, and a name in Mireward that cannot be taken off

**This is a write entry, in the form of CR-044, and it records what changed on the page. It does not change a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder or the planned ending, and the series ending is untouched. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited; `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were NOT edited. The volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200.**

1. **The chapters.** `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0141.md` … `chapter-0150.md`, **25,711 words** (`wc -w` after the writer's own repair pass, after the `CR-047` repair, after the `CR-048` repair and after the `CR-049` repair, the last of which changed Chapters 148 and 150 and therefore their word counts: 2,591 / 2,304 / 2,548 / 2,722 / 1,768 / 3,709 / 2,023 / 2,457 / 1,868 / 3,721). Friday the third of July to Monday the twentieth of July, eighteen days. POV is Milo Rook throughout.
2. **The instrument, in one paragraph, because a later block must not reinvent it.** A printed district schedule for surface and drainage works arrives in a bundle with the weekly papers and is not a weather report; it is a land instrument wearing a weather coat, and the whole of what the weather does on it is to be the occasion. It carries a column for a district's stability and a column for **the ground on which a district's work may be discontinued**, with a printed key of four grounds and no more. **The office of the First Surveyor appears for the first time in the series, as a signature block and not a person: a printed box with a rule and nothing on the rule, at the foot of the last side, and it is not an issuing line, and it requested nothing, received nothing and answered nothing, and nobody asked who is behind it.** A later block may not give it a face.
3. **The naming, and it is the block's whole consequence.** The eleven streets appears on a printed side — **the first time this district has been on a printed side of anything since the fourteenth of April** — as **not to be maintained after 31 August — a Monday, and itself a gang day, so the last walk of the season is on Monday the thirty-first, which is the last day of the contract; the last Thursday is the twenty-seventh; the last rack day is Friday the twenty-eighth, and the last sheet covers Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth; and there is no sheet at all for the week of the thirty-first, so that last walk is on no paper anywhere, and nobody empties a rack**, on the ground **no maintained seam of record**, completed on a Thursday in about a minute and a half by a person about a mile away, **and nobody lied, and there is no one to be angry at.** The cost is a contract to a date, and a gang of eleven men and a foreman who are not this city's, and a sheet, a rack, a tin, and a second tin on a bottom shelf in another building under a repair schedule. **Milo Rook can do one single thing about it and there is no form for that, because nothing in this district is a party to anything.** **A later block may not cure it, may not withdraw or vary the schedule, and may not make the eleven streets a district that is not on a list of anything again.**
4. **The second use, which is the volume's land thread, and it is Mara Quill's and not his.** *Column two, stability of district, is the column a land schedule wants* — because a land schedule does not want a condition, it wants to know who is on the ground. **The same press run went to a land desk about a mile from where the form was drafted in the middle of May, eight weeks before eleven streets got its copy, and nobody was told there were two.** Nine plots held on shares, entered unoccupied, in a book in a different building. **A later block may not turn this into a transaction, a claim, a person or a fight in this volume, and no batch may answer for the book of shares.**
5. **The failures and the recovery, and none of them is his to keep.** **The ninth seventh column** — about a hundred and ten words, every word true, no name in it because there is no name to give one — **was not written, and the reason is a line in his own handwriting on the facing page of the first of July, and a stop that a man wrote about himself is not a stop.** **A ninety-second true sentence for column two of the eleven streets' row was written in pencil on the back of their own schedule, eleven miles away, on the day after the day it was due, and it went in a bag and it did not go anywhere, and the cost fell on a foreman of fifty-four whose thing went out in a satchel and came back in it.** **The changed method and the recovery are Coll Sarrowe's and neither of them is a word: five men and a bar, nine years, four of them unnamed, one of them about nineteen.** **The best answer anybody hears in the fortnight is a foreman's, and it is *there is nothing wrong with it*, and there is no box on that form or on the sheet or in this city for it.** **The recovery that is somebody else's work and not a word is also a man of about twenty-six going over a carbon in hard pencil for four minutes on his own account, and a sentence that is illegible on a carbon is a smudge, and a smudge is not a record and cannot be produced.**
6. **The fifth of his own five, and the first one he has paid for.** On Sunday the nineteenth of July, in the two inches of ruled space at the bottom of a Mireward district returns card, **face down, so that the machine does not take the hand over** — and that is the first time in eight months he has wanted a machine not to. **A card with a thing struck through it is a card with a mark on it and the re-run is on a Friday at about four, and he cannot get his own name off it.** The nil is still a nil, the stability column is still hand-transferred and not typed, the three nils about Mireward are three and this is the same zero, and **a district that is not a place cannot be frightened and he has given a place a name.**
7. **The relationship.** Milestone 2, moved once, **and it was a disagreement and it is milestone 3 set up and not repaired**: she is right that it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists, and he is right that it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years, and she answers with his own sentence. **The custody register page is untouched for the eighth consecutive block. The season is not told to her. She did not describe the line in the return and he did not ask a third time.** A later block may not repair the disagreement in this volume and may not let either of them win it.
8. **Power and cost, unchanged except for the pair count.** No crossing, nothing held, stabilised or pinned. **Remembered routes ELEVEN, crossings TEN, tally TEN lines. THE PAIRS ARE TWENTY-NINE and they moved once, on the night of the sixteenth, and the interval is seven days, and the tenth of July was a decision and the other five nights were not.** Page home, token and plate figures are in `state/continuity.md` and `bible/terminology.md` and every one is `wc -w`-adjacent to a printed figure in a named chapter. **Failing sensory detail did not recur in nineteen days and nothing was acquired. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 141–150.**
9. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, six words, in Chapter 149, given to nobody: *A printed ground holds nobody up.*** The nine prior sentences are unchanged and were not repeated.
10. **THE WRITER'S OWN PASS, and this is the fifteenth pass in a row in the class CR-023 exists to record, and twelve defects were found in the manuscript before the state layer was written, and the state layer was then written from the chapters.** (a) **A FOREMAN CLAIMED A SEAM OF RECORD WHERE THE REST OF THE BLOCK NEEDS A LEVEL OF RECORD** — Chapter 142 had him say *there is a seam of record*, which contradicts the whole mechanism of Chapter 146, and it is now **there is a level of record… under my signature**, and the chapter says the distinction is the point. (b) **THE NINETY-SECOND SENTENCE WAS WRITTEN FOR MIREWARD'S ROW AND IS NOW WRITTEN FOR THE ELEVEN STREETS' ROW**, with the reason printed — he is eleven miles from the district it is about, on the day after the day it was due — because a Mireward version had no mechanism behind it and did not connect to the gang. (c) **A GARBLED CLAUSE** in the same sentence, *closed on the fourth of December's finding of the fourth by a joint finding*, replaced with the joint finding of the fourth of December and the evening of the twenty-second. (d) **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE SAID EIGHT WORDS AND WAS DESCRIBED AS HAVING SAID NINE** — replaced with a nine-word refusal, ***standing in a room is standing in a file***, which is also a refusal and is therefore in character. (e) **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-SIX WAS DESCRIBED AS *A WORKS CLERK OF FORTY-SIX YEARS OF SERVICE***, which reads as a length of service, and is now **a works clerk of forty-six**. (f) **A MAN COPIED A SHEET ONTO A CARBON, WHICH CANNOT BE DONE** — the duplicate is a carbon and a foreman's pencil comes through as a grey smear, and the man's work is now **going over the four words and the figure on the carbon in hard pencil so the sentence is legible**, which is better and is a fact about a carbon. (g) **THE NINTH PLOTS WERE ENTERED AS *4 TO 12 INCLUSIVE*, WHICH IS FOUR**, corrected to nine, and the line is read aloud. (h) **THE SCHEDULE'S PRINTING AND ISSUE DATES WERE INCONSISTENT** with the eight-week gap to the land desk, and the schedule is now printed in the middle of May and issued in the first week of July. (i) **A FIELD-BOOK ENTRY SAID *THE TENTH OF JULY WAS A DECISION* IN A CHAPTER THAT WAS WRITTEN BEFORE THE TENTH** — no such error survived; the entry is on the sixteenth and the decision is dated. (j) **A VISIBLE AUTHORING ARTIFACT** — a sentence in Chapter 143 that corrected a village name in the middle of a Field Book entry — was removed. (k) **THE BOUNDARY'S TWO ENDS WERE PLACED *IN CROWN HEIGHTS***, which put people at the ends of a line in a fen, and are now **a kerb and a bank about a hundred and thirty yards apart, and the paper that says where they are is in Crown Heights, and there is nobody standing at either of them.**  (l) **THE TWELFTH, AND THE ONE THAT HAD TO BE FIXED TWICE: THE CONTRACT'S WEEKDAY.** Chapters 146, 148 and 150 first printed **the last walk of the season on Monday the thirty-first of August** and **the last rack day on Friday the twenty-eighth of August**. This pass then read the inherited chain — which reads `1st August Sun`, and had read it since Batch 0001 — instead of counting, and rewrote the chapters onto **Tuesday the thirty-first**, a chain that is a day out for the whole of August. **THAT WAS THE WRONG REPAIR, and the sixteenth review pass has proved it: 1st August is a SATURDAY, 31st August is a MONDAY, 30th August is a SUNDAY, and the inherited chain was the thing at fault. The correction has now been made from the counted chain and is on the page in Milo's own voice in Chapters 146, 148 and 150, and it is a scene and not a note: the printed form date stays 31 AUGUST, A PRINTED DATE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT DAY OF THE WEEK IT IS, the thirty-first of August is a Monday and the gang is on a Monday and a Thursday, the last walk of the season is on Monday the thirty-first of August and is the last day of the contract and is itself a gang day, the last Thursday is the twenty-seventh, the last rack day is Friday the twenty-eighth, the last sheet covers Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth, there is no sheet at all for the week of the thirty-first so that last walk is on no paper anywhere, the second tin stops at thirty-seven, and nobody empties a rack.** `state/continuity.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `state/current.md`, this entry and the Batch 0003 brief were all corrected to the manuscript, **and the second tin's last figure is THIRTY-SEVEN on Friday the twenty-eighth of August.** Two of Batch 0002's own forward references were already correct and were not touched: a rack on Friday the twenty-fourth and a walk on Thursday the twenty-third. This is the fifteenth pass's headline defect and the second weekday in a row, and **BOTH REPAIRS FAILED FOR THE SAME REASON: THE CHAIN WAS RE-READ INSTEAD OF COUNTED. The chain is only authority when it is counted forward from a chapter heading. A MONTH THAT HAS NOT BEEN REACHED YET IS EXACTLY WHERE A DATE GOES WRONG, BECAUSE THERE IS NO SCENE ON THE PAGE TO CHECK IT AGAINST — and that cuts both ways, because there is also no scene to check a wrong fix against.** **And one figure was corrected in the state layer in the other direction, which is the point of the class: the Volume 03 Batch 0002 brief was written before the chapters and put the eleventh of July in a chain that makes it a Saturday, and the chapters hold it a Saturday, and the brief and this entry agree.**
11. **WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO.** It did not change a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder or the ending. It did not add a grammar, a faction, an antagonist, a final enemy or a fourth kind of place. **Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable and Tarin Kest do not appear and are not named as persons; Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–150; the Rainway is not named; no Weather remnant is identified; the ford is unresolved; the First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the One Survey are not named; the First Quieting has no date in this volume; and Ada Fenn is referred to zero times and the count of a woman of sixty-eight on nothing stands at eight and is not nine.** It did not touch `state/phase-ledger.json`, `.opencode/`, `.github/`, `scripts/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md` or `opencode.json`, and it did not mark any phase closed. It created exactly one next-phase prompt.

## CR-047 — REPAIR PASS, sixteenth review: the weekday chain was broken at the month boundary and Batch 0002's repair made it worse, and everything downstream of it is corrected here

**This is a repair entry, not a write entry. It changes no premise, no world rule, no power rule, no antagonist ladder and no planned ending, and the series ending is untouched. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited, and neither were `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `outline/volume-01.md`. The volume's final image still belongs to Chapter 200 and is not written.** No batch was restarted. The prose of Chapters 141–150 was repaired in place; the good prose of those ten chapters is untouched.

### 1. THE DEFECT, AND IT WAS ONE DEFECT IN SEVEN PLACES

**The chain reads `1st August Sun` in `state/current.md` and it had done since Volume 03 Batch 0001. It is a day out for the whole of August. The correct chain, counted forward from chapter headings, is `1st July Wed` → `3rd July Fri` → `6th July Mon` → `10th July Fri` → `11th July Sat` → `13th July Mon` → `17th July Fri` → `19th July Sun` → `20th July Mon` → `31st July Fri` → `1st August SATURDAY` → `31st August MONDAY`.** August is therefore **Mondays 3, 10, 17, 24, 31; Thursdays 6, 13, 20, 27; Fridays 7, 14, 21, 28; Sundays 2, 9, 16, 23, 30.** The same count also confirms four earlier anchors that the manuscript prints: **6 April is a Monday** (the plate), **22 April is a Wednesday** (the foreman's clock), **25 June is a Thursday** (the tenth crossing) and **2 July is a Thursday** (Chapter 140).

**CR-046 item 10(l) is the origin and it is the class this repository keeps failing.** The first draft of Chapters 146, 148 and 150 printed the last walk of the season on Monday the thirty-first of August and the last rack day on Friday the twenty-eighth — **both of which were right.** That pass did not count. It re-read the inherited state-layer chain, decided the chain was authority, and rewrote the chapters onto **Tuesday the thirty-first**, then reported the chapters as repaired and left the chain alone. **Two repairs failed for the same reason and the reason is that a state file was read as authority instead of being counted.**

### 2. THE CORRECTED ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS NOW THE CANON AND IS PRINTED IN CHAPTERS 146, 148 AND 150

The form says *not to be maintained after 31 August*. **31 August is a MONDAY, and the gang is on a Monday and a Thursday, and nobody in the office that printed the form looked at a calendar, because a printed date does not know what day of the week it is.** Therefore:

- **THE LAST WALK OF THE SEASON IS ON MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST OF AUGUST — the last day of the contract, and itself a gang day.**
- **The last Thursday is Thursday the twenty-seventh.**
- **A rack day is a Friday. The last Friday inside the contract is Friday the twenty-eighth.**
- **The sheet that goes into the rack on Friday the twenty-eighth is the sheet for the week of Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth, and it is the last sheet that will ever be printed for this gang.**
- **THERE IS NO SHEET AT ALL FOR THE WEEK OF MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST**, because the schedule of works ends and there is nothing after it to print one. **So the last walk of the season is on no sheet, is in no rack and is in neither tin, and there is no form in this city for a walk that is not on a sheet.** This is the motif that replaces the old empty rack day, and it is the same size and the same shape and it is now true: **the last time the machinery runs is the one time it cannot record.**
- **THE SECOND TIN'S LAST FIGURE IS THIRTY-SEVEN, on Friday the twenty-eighth of August.** It was thirty-six on the twenty-first, then thirty-seven on the twenty-eighth.
- **THE RACK IS A PIECE OF FURNITURE IN A CORRIDOR AND NOBODY EMPTIES A RACK.** On Friday the fourth of September there is a rack with nothing in it in it, and on Friday the eleventh, and there is nothing in this city that says a rack is finished.

**The block's smallest good line changes from *a printed date does not have to be a Monday* to *a printed date does not know what day of the week it is*, and the discovery inverts: the printed date is a Monday, and that is the worst day it could have fallen on.** Nobody is at fault, nothing is resolved, the eleven streets is still on a printed side it cannot get off, and Milo Rook still cannot do one single thing about it. **The plot is unchanged.**

### 3. WHAT WAS EDITED, AND WHERE

**Manuscript, in place, good prose preserved:** `chapter-0146.md` (three passages: the cost paragraph, the arithmetic, and Field Book entry Four); `chapter-0148.md` (the ledger line); `chapter-0150.md` (part (a) of the standing question, entry Seven, and two passages in entries Four and Six). **`chapter-0143.md`** — the flatbed's direction is now pinned: **it comes in FROM THE CITY on a Friday morning at about a quarter to seven, is in Mireward at about half past ten with the week's post and the sacks on the bed, and goes BACK TO THE CITY on the Friday afternoon and is in the yard there at about half past four.** This is the reading under which Chapters 143, 146 and 147 agree: he boards at the Mireward-side gate at ten to seven, spends the tenth in Mireward, and the only vehicle that would have carried the form back to the city goes back at about four while he is two hundred yards along a raised path. `chapter-0146.md`'s opening line now says *in the yard in the city*. **The rack paragraph in Chapter 143 is a framing aside about two things that did not need him and now carries no time and no place**, so it cannot be read as a location.

**Meta-language removed from the manuscript — the class Batch 0001 claimed to have eliminated and Batch 0002 had reintroduced at least twelve times.** Gone: *"the one this volume is going to have"* and *"not going to be repaired in this volume"* and *"There is no kiss in this chapter"* and *"for the eighth consecutive block"* (Ch 147, three passages — the last of these replaced with **he has not asked her about it since the fifth of May**, which is on the page); *"the whole of the second half of this volume"* (Ch 146); *"the only thing in this chapter that is any use"* (Ch 148); and in Chapter 150 the whole cluster — *"this block has not resolved it"*, *"The Rainway is not named in any of these ten chapters"*, *"Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1 to 150"*, *"Sel Iri does not appear and is a Volume 4 to 10 character"*, *"Halden Vey is not named and did not appear"*, *"Orrin Sable did not appear"*, *"Tarin Kest did not appear"*, *"it is not a Volume 02 thing"*, *"across eight chapters of Volume 02 and this block adds zero"*, and *"they belong to Volumes 4 to 6, 5 to 6, 7 to 8 and 10"*. **Every one of those was replaced in-story, not deleted: the chapter now says the ford is undated and that nobody has been named behind it, that no remnant has been identified, that the office of the First Surveyor is a box with a rule on it, that there is no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal, and that no face came into any of it that was not already on a payroll or a list.**

**State layer, all corrected to the manuscript:** `state/current.md` (the chain at its oldest instance in the Batch 0001 section and again in the Batch 0002 section, the contract arithmetic in the header note, in the chain paragraph, in the Monday-the-twentieth bullet, in the standing list, in reviewer notes 7(b) and 8, and **note 9, which printed *ELEVEN DEFECTS* and listed twelve different things and is now reconciled against `CR-046` item 10(a)–(l)**); `state/continuity.md` (the chain twice, and items 4, 9 and 11); `state/batch-summaries.md` (two passages); `state/chapter-summaries.md` (three passages); `state/open-threads.md` (the cost thread); `bible/terminology.md` (the Volume 03 register's cost entry); `state/canon-changes.md` (`CR-046`'s own header, item 1, item 3 and item 10(l), which recorded a repair that went the wrong way). **`state/character-state.md` needed no change: it carries no August weekday.**

**Briefs:** `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0003/PROMPT.md` is **rebuilt** — chain, gang days, rack days, tin figures, the two wrong foreman figures, the five wrong weekday cells in the day-count table, the block's span, the opening brief, the transport paragraph, and reviewer notes "First" and "Third", and a new box headed **THE THING THAT HAS BEEN WRONG TWICE**. `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` **carries a SUPERSEDED banner** rather than a rewrite, because it is the record of what was dispatched and rewriting a dispatched brief would destroy the evidence of how the error travelled.

**Foreman figures, corrected in the brief and checked against the manuscript: eleven weeks and three days on Saturday the eleventh of July and twelve weeks and five days on Monday the twentieth are correct in the chapters and were not touched. Fourteen weeks and five days on Monday the third of August (not thirteen weeks), eighteen weeks exactly on WEDNESDAY the twenty-sixth (not Monday), eighteen weeks and one day on Thursday the twenty-seventh, eighteen weeks and five days on Monday the thirty-first.**

**Word counts after this repair pass, after the `CR-048` repair and after the `CR-049` repair: Chapters 141–150 are 25,711 words** (2,591 / 2,304 / 2,548 / 2,722 / 1,768 / 3,709 / 2,023 / 2,457 / 1,868 / 3,721). The figures in `CR-046` item 1, `state/current.md` and `state/batch-summaries.md` are updated to these.

### 4. WHAT WAS CHECKED AND FOUND CORRECT, and did not change

**Every day-count reconciles**: page home 222/226/229/230/233/236/237/238/239, token 208/212/215/216/219/222/223/224/225, plate and condition column, against day one = 24 November, day zero = 7 December, plate from 6 April, condition column empty since 12 February. **⚠ CORRECTED IN `CR-049`: THIS SENTENCE WAS TRUE OF EVERY FIGURE EXCEPT THE PLATE.** At the time of writing, `chapter-0150.md` Field Book entry Three printed the plate at *a hundred and four days* on Monday the twentieth of July, which is the nineteenth's figure, while the chain said a hundred and four on the nineteenth and a hundred and five on the twentieth. Six April to the nineteenth is 104 and to the twentieth is 105. The origin was right here, the arithmetic was right here, and the chapter still carried a stale day — **because this paragraph reconciled the counters against their origins and never diffed them against the chapter that prints them.** The chapter is now right and this sentence is true of it; see `CR-049` section 1. **The walk and rack counts are internally consistent and were not touched**: walks the 6th, 9th, 13th, 16th and 20th of July are the twenty-seventh through thirty-first since the first of April, which is right, and the rack days the third, tenth and seventeenth are three Fridays, which is the number of Fridays in the period. **The corrected last walk, Monday the thirty-first of August, is the forty-third since the first of April, and no chapter prints that ordinal, so nothing had to change.** Zero references to a woman of sixty-eight on nothing; no crossing staged, so eleven routes, ten crossings and ten tally lines correctly unmoved; one Atlas residue in ten chapters; the facing-page seventh-column rule quoted and not broken; Milo's five given-ups intact; the custody register page untouched; the outline files unedited; exactly one next-phase prompt.

### 5. THE RULE, AND IT IS THE ONLY THING IN THIS ENTRY THAT MATTERS

**THE WEEKDAY CHAIN IS AUTHORITY ONLY WHEN IT IS COUNTED FORWARD FROM A CHAPTER HEADING OR A FIXED POINT THAT IS QUOTABLE IN A CHAPTER. IT IS NEVER AUTHORITY WHEN IT IS RE-READ FROM A STATE FILE, BECAUSE THE STATE FILE IS WHERE THE ERROR LIVES.** A month that has not been reached yet is exactly where a date goes wrong, because there is no scene on the page to check it against — **and that cuts both ways, because there is also no scene to check a wrong fix against.** The cheapest check in this repository, in order: `wc -w` on the chapter files and a diff against the places the figures are printed; **a count of the chain, not a re-reading of it**; and a check that the number of rack days equals the number of Fridays in the period and the number of walks equals the number of Mondays and Thursdays in the period.

### 6. FOUR RESIDUAL WEEKDAY SLIPS IN CLOSED VOLUME 02, EXAMINED AND DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED

**Every one of the 317 weekday-plus-date claims in Chapters 1–150 was extracted and checked against the counted chain, and 313 are correct.** The four that are not are all in **Volume 02, Chapters 71–82**, which is **closed at Chapter 130 and inherited whole, and which is not the current phase.** They are recorded here rather than fixed, because the current phase is Batch 0002 and because one of the four cannot be fixed without re-deciding a fact:

1. **`chapter-0071.md`** — *the week runs from Monday the twenty-ninth of December to Sunday the third of January*. **29 December 2000 is a Monday and 1 January is a Thursday, so 3 January is a SATURDAY, not a Sunday.** The same sentence's *Thursday the first* and *Monday the twenty-ninth* are correct. The correction would be one word.
2. **`chapter-0072.md`** — *The Field Book, Tuesday the fifth of January, 23.40*. **5 January is a MONDAY.**
3. **`chapter-0073.md`** — *effective Monday the 13th of October*, inside a quoted rule-book citation in a chapter about a rule book. **This one is almost certainly deliberate or is a citation from outside the volume's year, and it was left alone on that basis.**
4. **`chapter-0082.md`** — *it came off it on the Friday the twenty-fifth of January, a day before the week it is for begins*. **25 January is a THURSDAY, and the week beginning Monday the twenty-ninth does not begin the day after it.** This cannot be corrected by changing a weekday alone: the sentence also says which week the sheet is for, and choosing the intended week is a decision about a document in a closed volume, which this pass must not make.

**A later pass with Volume 02 open should look at all four. Three are one-word fixes; the fourth needs an author decision and should not be resolved by a fixer.** None of the four touches Volume 03, the eleven streets, the contract, the bell, the Rainway, the relationship, the power costs or the planned ending, and none of them changes a day-count, because a day-count is a count of days and not a weekday.

## CR-048 — REPAIR PASS, seventeenth review: a Field Book entry in Chapter 150 referred to the book by its own chapter number, and the sentence it referred to was not on the page in Chapter 136 either

**This is a repair entry, not a write entry. It changes no premise, no world rule, no power rule, no antagonist ladder and no planned ending, and the series ending is untouched. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited, and neither were `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `outline/volume-01.md`. The volume's final image still belongs to Chapter 200 and is not written.** No batch was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. The good prose of Chapters 141–150 is untouched; one sentence in one chapter was replaced.

### 1. THE DEFECT, AND IT WAS TWO DEFECTS IN ONE SENTENCE

`chapter-0150.md`, in Field Book entry Five, printed: ***No crossing. He held nothing, stabilised nothing, pinned nothing, and Chapter 136 says he could have and wrote down that he could have.***

1. **It is meta-language inside a Field Book entry.** A man writing in his own book at about half past eleven at night on Monday the twentieth of July does not know what chapter he is in. This is the class `CR-047` section 3 records as removed from this very chapter — *"in Chapter 150 the whole cluster"* — and this instance survived that pass.
2. **The claim it makes is not true of Chapter 136.** Every one of the 317 weekday-plus-date claims was extracted for `CR-047` and no such check was run on referents. **`chapter-0136.md` contains no instance of the phrase *could have* anywhere.** Chapter 136 is the tenth crossing at the old ford at Mireward on Thursday the twenty-fifth of June, in at 14.20 and out at 15.02, forty-two minutes, a hand bell on a post of about seven feet with nineteen marks cut into it low down in a row, rung nine times and not while he was there, and what Chapter 136 says about what he could have done is: ***And he did not ring it, and he did not touch the post, and he did not go and look at the marks from underneath, and there was nothing else in the whole of those forty-two minutes, and he came out.*** So the sentence asserted a chapter said something it does not say, and the thing it asserted in place of it is a different and better fact.

### 2. HOW IT GOT THERE, AND IT IS THE CLASS THIS FILE EXISTS FOR

**A state-layer phrase was copied into the prose and its vague referent was concretised.** The Volume 03 Batch 0001 entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` line 859 reads: *He held nothing and stabilised nothing in these ten chapters and the chapter says he could have and wrote down that he could have.* In a state file, *the chapter* is a legitimate way of pointing at a batch. Carried into a Field Book entry it cannot stay vague, so it was resolved to a chapter number — **and the number resolved to 136 because that is where the tenth crossing is, and the phrase is about a gain used zero times, and both belong to that chapter, and neither sentence is in it.** The failure is the same one that produced the two weekday faults: **a figure or a pointer written after the prose and read back as authority on the page.** A state file is not authority about what is in a chapter. It is a description of it, and a description is only as good as the last time somebody counted.

### 3. THE REPAIR, AND IT IS A REPLACEMENT AND NOT A DELETION

**Manuscript, in place, one sentence, `chapter-0150.md` Field Book entry Five:**

> **No crossing. He held nothing, stabilised nothing, pinned nothing, and on the twenty-fifth of June at a ford that has been dry since about 1919 I could have rung a bell on a post and did not, and I have written down that I did not, more than once, and I have never once written down that I could have, and I do not know yet which of those two is the more expensive.**

Every clause of it is quotable from `chapter-0136.md`: the date, the ford, *dry since about 1919* with **1919 cut into a stone on the far side**, the bell on the post, the not-ringing, and the twice-written record (*And he did not ring it* in the body and *I did not touch the post* in the entry of the twenty-fifth). **No new figure was introduced** — *more than once* is a bound and not a count, because the manuscript nowhere states a count of how many times he wrote down that he did not ring it, and this pass may not invent one. **The repair is also better than the sentence it replaced, and that is the usual test:** the old sentence had him record a capacity, and the new one has him record a refusal, twice, and then turn the difference between the two into a question he cannot answer — which is the whole of his method and the whole of the block's finding about a form, in the closing thirteen words of the entry, ***I do not know yet which of those two is the more expensive***, of which the question itself is eight. **An earlier draft of this entry said *in eight words of his own voice* and pointed at the eight-word question; the sentence it is counting is thirteen words long and the eight-word clause is the tail of it. Corrected in `CR-049` because a state entry that miscounts its own quotation is the same defect this file exists to record, and it was one clause away from a real one.**

**It resolves nothing.** The ford is still undated, nobody has been named behind the bell, nobody has been put inside it, it is not a haunting, a claim, a ghost, a voice, a knock, a person, a curse, a debt or an inherited role, and there is still no demand, no service and no failure out of it. **The Rainway is not named. No Weather remnant is identified. The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and the promise about the end of July still stands and is still held.** The eleventh remembered route, the ten crossings and the ten tally lines do not move, because he crossed nothing on the twentieth of July and this sentence is about the twenty-fifth of June.

### 4. THE STATE LAYER, AND THE FIGURES, WHICH MOVED BECAUSE A CHAPTER MOVED

**Because Chapter 150 gained fifty-one words, every printed word count for this batch moved, and this is the seventeenth time in a row that the figures have had to be chased out of five places by hand.** Chapters 141–150 were **25,709 words** (2,591 / 2,304 / 2,548 / 2,722 / 1,768 / 3,709 / 2,023 / 2,457 / 1,868 / **3,719**), verified by `wc -w` on the ten files after this repair; **`CR-049` subsequently added two words to Chapter 150 and changed one word in Chapter 148, and the live figures are 25,711 with Chapter 150 at 3,721.** Updated: `state/canon-changes.md` (`CR-046` item 1 and `CR-047` section 3, both annotated with the provenance of the change rather than silently restated), `state/current.md` (two places) and `state/batch-summaries.md`. **The prose in the other nine chapters is byte-identical and their counts did not move.** `state/continuity.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `state/open-threads.md` needed no change: none of them quotes the sentence, and none of them carries a word count.

**The Batch 0001 line at `state/batch-summaries.md` line 859 is left as it stands and is examined here rather than edited.** It is a state file describing a closed batch, the batch number is the correct referent for it there, and Batch 0001 is not the current phase. **It is recorded because the next writer to copy a phrase out of a state file needs to know that the phrase is not a quotation.** Nothing else in the state layer was derived from it.

### 5. WHAT WAS CHECKED AND FOUND CORRECT, and did not change

**The weekday chain was counted again and not re-read, and it holds:** `1st July Wed` and `3rd July Fri`, `6th July Mon`, `7th July Tue`, `9th July Thu`, `10th July Fri`, `11th July Sat`, `13th July Mon`, `14th July Tue`, `16th July Thu`, `17th July Fri`, `18th July Sat`, `19th July Sun`, `20th July Mon` are all chapter headings or Field Book headings in Chapters 141–150, and counting forward from `1st July Wed` gives `31st July Fri`, `1st August SATURDAY` and `31st August MONDAY`, which is what Chapters 146, 148 and 150 print. **The contract arithmetic stands as `CR-047` left it: the last walk of the season is Monday the thirty-first of August, the last Thursday is the twenty-seventh, the last rack day is Friday the twenty-eighth, the last sheet covers Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth, there is no sheet for the week of the thirty-first, the second tin's last figure is thirty-seven, and nobody empties a rack.** **Three rack days and three Fridays; five walks and five Mondays and Thursdays; the pairs at twenty-nine with four sevens; page home 222 to 239 and token 208 to 225 both reconcile against day one = 24 November and day zero = 7 December; the plate at eighty-eight days on the third of July and a hundred and five on the twentieth, which is also the page home on the sixteenth of June.** **⚠ The plate figure printed in this sentence is CORRECT and was correct when written, and `chapter-0150.md` printed a hundred and four on the same day at the same time — the entry certified the right number without noticing that the manuscript contradicted it, which is the more dangerous failure of the two, because the state layer and the chapter were each confident and disagreed. Corrected in `CR-049` section 1, which fixes the chapter and leaves this sentence standing.** One Atlas residue in ten chapters, six words, in Chapter 149. **⚠ RE-VERIFIED IN `CR-049` AND CONFIRMED EXACT: the residue is `chapter-0149.md` line 61, a standalone blockquoted aphorism of six words — *A printed ground holds nobody up.* — preceded by the line *the ninth thing of that shape appeared in eight months, and it is six words*. An eighteenth-review pass proposed to correct this figure on the ground that the only candidate it found was a sixteen-word clause at line 69. That proposal is REJECTED and recorded in `CR-049` section 4: the proposal read one line of a chapter and not the one above it, and acting on it would have deleted a correct certification and counted a non-residue as a residue.** No crossing staged, so eleven routes, ten crossings, ten tally lines. Milo's five given-ups intact and the sixth-column rule on the facing page quoted and not broken. The custody register page untouched for the eighth consecutive block. Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable and Tarin Kest absent; Soren Rook not named anywhere in Chapters 1–150; **Ada Fenn referred to zero times and the count of a woman of sixty-eight on nothing stands at eight and is not nine.** The eleven streets appears on a printed side as not to be maintained, and Milo Rook still cannot do one single thing about it, and there is still no form for that. Exactly one next-phase prompt exists, `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and it was **not** rebuilt by this pass, because none of its figures moved.

### 6. THE RULE, ADDED TO THE ONE IN `CR-047`

**`CR-047`'s rule governs weekdays. This one governs referents, and it is the same mistake wearing a different coat: A SENTENCE IN THE MANUSCRIPT THAT POINTS AT ANOTHER PART OF THE MANUSCRIPT MUST BE CHECKED AGAINST THE TEXT IT POINTS AT, AND A STATE FILE IS NOT THAT TEXT.** The cheap check, in order: `wc -w` on the chapter files and a diff against the five places the figures are printed; **a count of the chain and not a re-reading of it**; **a grep for `Chapter [0-9]` in the chapters, which must return exactly one hit per file and that hit must be the heading**; and, where a chapter says that something happened, `grep` for the words it says happened. Volume 01 and Volume 02 have not had the referent check run on them, and a later pass should run it before Volume 04 opens rather than after.

## CR-049 — REPAIR PASS, eighteenth review: a stale day-count in the last chapter of the batch, an absolute quantifier the batch's own method cannot support, a week-count carried in from the scene before it, and one proposed correction that would have deleted a correct figure

**This is a repair entry, not a write entry. It changes no premise, no world rule, no power rule, no antagonist ladder and no planned ending, and the series ending is untouched. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited, and neither were `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` or `outline/volume-01.md`. The volume's final image still belongs to Chapter 200 and is not written.** No batch was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. **Three words in two chapters of Chapters 141–150 were changed, in place. The good prose of those ten chapters is otherwise untouched**, and the eight chapters this pass did not open are byte-identical.

This pass was a repair of `CR-048` and it exists because `CR-048` certified three claims as *checked and found correct* that do not survive checking. Two of them were wrong and one of them was a proposal to change a figure that was already right. **All three are dealt with below: two chapters are corrected, and the third proposal is rejected with the evidence that rejects it.**

### 1. THE THREE EDITS, AND THEY ARE THE SAME DEFECT THREE TIMES

**(a) A stale day-count in Field Book entry Three of Chapter 150, dated Monday the twentieth of July.** It printed *The plate, about a person, **a hundred and four days** in the grass and never reported.* Chapter 149, the same day, prints *the plate is **a hundred and five** days in the grass today*, and Chapter 148, the nineteenth, prints a hundred and four in two places. **The plate went down on the sixth of April** (`chapter-0131.md`: *A plate in a field on the sixth of April*); the sixth of April to the nineteenth of July is 104 days and to the twentieth is 105. **So 104 was the previous day's figure carried into the twentieth**, in the batch's final chapter, one clause away from the sentence `CR-048` did fix in the same entry. Corrected in place to **a hundred and five days**, which is what Chapter 149 already said, what `CR-047` derived from the origin, and what this volume's state layer has always printed. No other figure in the entry moved.

**(b) An absolute quantifier in the same chapter that the book's own method cannot support.** The sentence `CR-048` wrote into Field Book entry Five ends *…and I have written down that I did not, more than once, and I have never once written down that I could have, and I do not know yet which of those two is the more expensive.* **The negative was unqualified, and four earlier Field Book entries contradict it as printed:** `chapter-0050.md` (*I could have argued and I did not*), `chapter-0056.md` (*I had about four minutes in which I could have, and I did not*), `chapter-0121.md` (*the first time in seven months I have named a thing I could have done and did not do*) and `chapter-0131.md` (*a thing I could have asked him for in February*). **The claim is defensible only when scoped to the bell, and the sentence's whole subject is the bell.** It is now scoped: *I have never once written down that I could have **rung it***. **The scoping is not a fudge and it was checked against the text, not reasoned about:** across Chapters 136–150 the only instances of *could have* are `chapter-0140.md` (*the man who could have gone*), `chapter-0144.md` (*And I could have gone and got them out*) and `chapter-0146.md` (*He does not know I could have, in ninety seconds, in pencil*) — **none of them is the bell.** What Chapter 136 prints is *And he did not ring it, and he did not touch the post*, and the entries since have printed the not-ringing and never the capacity. The narrowed claim is therefore true, and the turn of the sentence — which of the two records is the more expensive — is untouched.

**(c) A week-count carried in from the scene before it.** Chapter 147, Saturday the eighteenth of July, at the records counter: **"You have told me about the season eleven weeks running," said Mara Quill. "Twelve."** Chapter 148, the nineteenth, costed the not-telling to her at *eleven weeks*. Chapter 150, the twentieth, at *twelve weeks*. **The nineteenth is the outlier and its figure is the one Mara Quill has just said out loud one day earlier — the Finding-1(a) class exactly, a number picked up from the adjacent scene and carried forward.** Corrected in place to *twelve weeks*, which is his own spoken count in Chapter 147 and the figure the batch's last chapter prints.

**The absolute value remains uncountable on the page, and this entry does not pretend otherwise.** The run's own anchors do reconcile against each other and they are the manuscript's: **1 May in Chapter 116 (*for the third week*) and 15 June in Chapter 129 (*It is the ninth week*) both fall correctly in a run that begins on the fifteenth of April**, the day after the hearing. **Continuing that chain puts the eighteenth of July in the fourteenth week, so neither eleven nor twelve is derivable from the printed origin, and no origin date is printed for this not-telling.** `chapter-0120.md` dates a *not-knowing* since the eighth of May, which is ten weeks and two days at the nineteenth, and that is a different not-telling — the writing on the gate — so it cannot be the origin of either figure. **What the repair establishes is consistency with the two things that are on the page, not a new derivation.** A batch that wants this count to be countable has to print the date it starts from, and that is a decision for a later block, not a number a fixer may invent.

**None of the three edits changes a plot, a motive, a relationship beat or a fact about the world.** The five given-ups are still five and are still not resolutions; the not-telling still costs Mara Quill and still has not been told; the plate is still in the grass and still unreported; the bell is still unrung and still undated and nobody is still named behind it.

### 2. THE STATE LAYER, AND THE FIGURES, WHICH MOVED BECAUSE TWO CHAPTERS MOVED

**Chapter 150 gained two words and Chapter 148 changed one word for one, so the batch total rose by two: Chapters 141–150 are now 25,711 words** (2,591 / 2,304 / 2,548 / 2,722 / 1,768 / 3,709 / 2,023 / 2,457 / 1,868 / **3,721**), verified by `wc -w` on the ten files after this repair. **This is the eighteenth time in a row that these figures have had to be chased out of five places by hand**, and the reason has not changed. Updated: `state/canon-changes.md` (`CR-046` item 1, `CR-047` section 3 and `CR-048` section 4, all three annotated with the provenance of the change rather than silently restated), `state/current.md` (two headers and the note at line 302) and `state/batch-summaries.md`. **`state/continuity.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `state/open-threads.md` needed no change: none of them quotes any of the three sentences, none carries a word count, and none prints the plate's day-count or the not-telling's week-count.** `bible/terminology.md` needed no change: the ninth residue entry is correct and section 4 below is why.

### 3. TWO INSTANCES OF META-LANGUAGE, IN CLOSED VOLUMES, RECORDED AND NOT EDITED

**A `grep` for `Chapter [0-9]` across all 150 chapters returns exactly one hit per file where that hit is the line-1 heading, and exactly two hits that are not:** `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0044.md` line 3 (*that is the whole of Chapter 44 and everything else in it happened afterwards*) and `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0102.md` line 123 (*and it is the whole of Chapter 103, and Milo Rook was in the room and did not speak*). **Chapter 103 is Lena Marr going up a flight of stairs on Wednesday the eighth of April, not a four-second utterance, so the forward referent in Chapter 102 does not resolve; the Chapter 44 instance is borderline and may be an in-fiction manual section that reads like narrator-level numbering.** Both sit in **closed volumes** — Volume 01 closed long ago and Volume 02 closed at Chapter 130 — and **they are recorded here rather than fixed, on the same reasoning `CR-047` section 6 used for the four residual weekday slips: a closed volume is not the current phase, and repairing one is not a fixer's decision.** `CR-048` section 6 already scheduled a referent pass over Volumes 01 and 02 **before Volume 04 opens rather than after**; these two are now named in it, and a pass with those volumes open should take them first, because they are the last two of this defect class in the manuscript.

### 4. A PROPOSED CORRECTION THAT IS REJECTED, AND THE FIGURE THAT PROTECTS IT

**An eighteenth-review pass reported that the Ch 149 residue claim was unsupported and that `CR-047` section 5 and `CR-048` section 5 had certified it twice. That is wrong, and acting on it would have damaged a correct figure.** `CR-048` section 5 records *One Atlas residue in ten chapters, six words, in Chapter 149*, and `bible/terminology.md` records the sentence itself. **The residue is `chapter-0149.md` line 61, and it is exactly six words: a standalone blockquoted aphorism, *A printed ground holds nobody up*, introduced by the line *the ninth thing of that shape appeared in eight months, and it is six words, and it is not going to be given to anybody*, and followed by the paragraph that records it being given to nobody.** The proposed correction identified `chapter-0149.md` line 69 as the only candidate, counted it at sixteen words, and proposed either deleting the residue or restating it at an unrecognisable length.

**Line 69 is not the residue and the arithmetic against it is beside the point.** *A sentence that is illegible on a carbon is not a second copy of a sentence* is a **rule of evidence inside Milo Rook's own reasoning**, attributed in the same entry to a conductor of thirty-eight who gave it to him in April, and it is embedded mid-sentence in an argument about a foreman's pencil. **The established convention is a standalone aphorism, and then a limit** — *A road is kept, not made* (six), *Weather uses whatever door is open* (six), *It is dated before it is read* (seven) — and line 61 is a standalone aphorism of six words above a paragraph that limits it, which is the convention exactly. **The proposal read one line of a chapter and not the line above it.** The two certifications stand unaltered in their figures, and both `CR-047` section 5 and `CR-048` section 5 now carry a note recording that the figure was re-verified against `chapter-0149.md` line 61 and confirmed exact.

**This is the second time in two passes that a figure was proposed for deletion on the strength of a partial read**, and it is worth naming the asymmetry: a wrong certification in this repository has been wrong about a *chapter* four times, and a wrong *proposal* has been wrong about a *state figure* twice. **The state layer is not the thing that needs auditing. The chapter is.**

### 5. WHAT WAS CHECKED AND FOUND CORRECT, and did not change

**The weekday chain holds and was counted forward, not re-read:** `1st July Wed`, `3rd July Fri`, `6th July Mon`, `7th July Tue`, `9th July Thu`, `10th July Fri`, `11th July Sat`, `13th July Mon`, `14th July Tue`, `16th July Thu`, `17th July Fri`, `18th July Sat`, `19th July Sun`, `20th July Mon`, giving `31st July Fri`, `1st August SATURDAY` and `31st August MONDAY`, which is what Chapters 146, 148 and 150 print. **The contract arithmetic stands as `CR-047` left it:** the last walk of the season is Monday the thirty-first of August, the last Thursday the twenty-seventh, the last rack day Friday the twenty-eighth, the last sheet covers the twenty-fourth to the thirtieth, there is no sheet for the week of the thirty-first, the second tin's last figure is thirty-seven, and nobody empties a rack. **Three rack days and three Fridays; five walks and five Mondays and Thursdays; page home 222 to 239 and token 208 to 225 both reconcile against day one = 24 November and day zero = 7 December; the plate at eighty-eight days on the third of July and a hundred and five on the twentieth — the chapter now prints what the state layer has always printed.** Eleven remembered routes, ten crossings, ten tally lines unmoved, because no crossing was staged. Milo's five given-ups intact and the facing-page seventh-column rule quoted and not broken. The custody register page untouched for the eighth consecutive block. Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable and Tarin Kest absent; Soren Rook not named anywhere in Chapters 1–150; Ada Fenn referred to zero times and the count of a woman of sixty-eight on nothing stands at eight. The eleven streets is on a printed side as not to be maintained and Milo Rook still cannot do one single thing about it, and there is still no form for that. **Exactly one next-phase prompt exists, `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and it was not rebuilt, because none of the three figures this pass touched is quoted in it.** No controller file was opened: `state/phase-ledger.json` and the absence of a `.done` marker in `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0002/` were observed and left alone, both being controller-owned.

### 6. THE RULE, ADDED TO THE TWO IN `CR-047` AND `CR-048`

**`CR-047`'s rule governs weekdays, `CR-048`'s governs referents, and this one governs the certification itself: A REPAIR PASS THAT CERTIFIES A FIGURE MUST DIFF THE FIGURE AGAINST THE FILE THAT PRINTS IT, AND A CERTIFICATION THAT RECONCILES A COUNTER AGAINST ITS ORIGIN HAS NOT CHECKED THE CHAPTER.** `CR-047` section 4 reconciled the plate against 6 April and passed, and `CR-048` section 5 printed *a hundred and five on the twentieth* — the correct number, correctly derived — **while the chapter it was certifying said a hundred and four.** Both halves of the state layer were confident and they disagreed, and **the way that happens is that a derived number and a printed number are two different objects and only one of them is the book.** The arithmetic was never in doubt. Nobody had the two side by side.

So the cheap checks, in order, and the first one is the one that would have caught all three edits: **`grep` the chapter for the figure the state layer prints, not for the figure's origin**; then `wc -w` on the chapter files and a diff against the places the figures are printed; then a count of the chain and not a re-reading of it; then a check that the number of rack days equals the number of Fridays in the period; then a `grep` for `Chapter [0-9]` in the chapters, which must return exactly one hit per file and that hit must be the heading. **And when a state entry certifies a figure, the certification is a claim about a file and must name the file and the line, the way `CR-049` section 4 does for the residue: `chapter-0149.md` line 61. Every certification in this file that says *found correct* without naming a line is a claim with nothing behind it, and the two in `CR-047` section 4 and `CR-048` section 5 are now annotated rather than trusted.**
