## CR-050 — Volume 03 Batch 0003 is written (Chapters 151–160): *Four Copies Of A Bell* — four bells, a practice that is not a form, a typed name in a box, a walk on no sheet, a rack that is never finished, and a season's post that stops because the works were the post

**1. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder or planned ending is changed by this batch.** What follows records what the manuscript fixed, and the four items that a later block, a reviewer or a repair pass must not undo. Every figure is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0151.md` … `chapter-0160.md`. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited and needs no change; `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited. **The volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200. Volume 03 IS OPEN AND IS NOT CLOSED AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PHASE EXISTS FOR IT.**

**2. THE BATCH'S FIXTURE: FOUR COPIES OF A BELL IS FOUR PEOPLE DOING A THING, AND IT IS NOT A FORM.** A handbell on a nail in a house with no number, rung twice before work and once after, by Barnaby Pike, sixty-one, on no rate roll and no employer and paid in coin out of a jar, for a death that was registered in a town eleven miles away and has no row in the district it happened in — and again the following year on the same date, and again, with no meeting and no first one anybody can point at. A bell on a bracket over a schoolroom door rung once by a woman of thirty-four who has a date from a child of about nine and will not give it. A bell off a boat on a wall at a top landing rung at ten past seven and at four every day since about 1979. And a bell on a post at a ford dry since about 1919. **Milo Rook is the only person in this city who knows there are four, has not written them down, and has not told anybody. FOUR PEOPLE DOING A THING IS FOUR PEOPLE DOING A THING. NO BATCH MAY MAKE IT A DOCUMENT.**

**3. THE BATCH'S ENGINE, WHICH IS A NEW FINDING AND NOT A NEW RULE, AND WHICH IS ONE SENTENCE ARRIVED AT THREE TIMES BY THREE PEOPLE: A FILE IS A PARTY; A PARTY IS A PERSON; A PERSON IS A NAME IN A BOX; AND THE ONLY WAY A DEPARTMENT HAS OF WRITING TO A PLACE WITH NO ADDRESS IS TO GO TO A CONTRACT.** (i) A fifth column, not asked for, on a machine that prints for nine hundred districts. (ii) `R. PRYCE`, typed into column six of a printed side in about a minute and a half on Thursday the twentieth of August, **nobody lying**, because a district with no correspondence address cannot be written to and the office went to the contract, and a contract names the supervisor. (iii) A register of correspondents, **a list of where a letter goes, which is the wrong shape for a district, because a district is not somewhere a letter goes, it is somewhere letters come from** — opened and shut and not entered. **A later block may not join any two of these three, may not make any of them a rule, and may not print this sentence as a maxim.**

**4. WHAT THE CONTRACT'S END DID TO THE PAGE, AND IT IS ALL COUNTED: the last walk of the season is Monday the thirty-first of August, the forty-third since the first of April, and there is no sheet for it, because a sheet covers Monday to Sunday and a machine cannot print a form for a period that is not in a contract; the last rack day is Friday the twenty-eighth and the last sheet covers Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth; the second tin's last figure is THIRTY-SEVEN and there is no thirty-eight; on Friday the fourth of September there is a rack with nothing in it in it and nobody empties a rack and nothing in this city says a rack is finished; on Tuesday the first of September there is a half past seven on the back length and nobody is on it; and THE SATCHEL HAS STOPPED, because the post to a district with no correspondence address is the works' papers, and so the one printed side that has arrived weekly in this district since the middle of January has not arrived, and nobody was told.** **The eleventh streets is NOT a district that is not on a list of anything: it is on exactly one printed side in the whole of this city, and the side names it as a thing that is not going to be maintained.**

**5. THE WRITER'S OWN REPAIR PASS, AND THE FOUR DEFECT GROUPS IT FOUND IN CHAPTERS 151, 152, 154 AND 157 — DAY-COUNTS, RACK-DAY FIGURES AND RELATIVE DATES, NONE OF THEM A PLOT.** This is CR-023's class and it is recorded here because the *manuscript* was wrong this time and not the state layer, which is the first time that has happened in this volume. **(a) Chapter 151 printed Thursday the twenty-third of July's day-counts as the twenty-first's: page home 240 for 242, token 226 for 228, condition column 158 and 160 for 161, and the plate at 106 in two places for 108 on the twenty-third and 107 on the twenty-second; the second tin was printed at thirty-two in an entry dated the twenty-third for a figure that does not reach thirty-two until the next morning. (b) Chapter 152's closing paragraph said the last rack day was yesterday, on a day that WAS the rack day. (c) Chapter 157 said the second tin was at thirty-five and the last rack day was Friday the fourteenth, when the rack day on Friday the twenty-first of August had put it at thirty-six. (d) Chapters 154 and 157 each said the end of July was still ahead — *in three weeks* on the seventh of August and *in three days* on the twenty-fourth of August — and **THE DIRECTION WAS CORRECTED AND THE FIGURES WERE NOT. The pass replaced one stale relative date with another of the identical class: it wrote *three days ago* on the seventh of August and *four days ago* on the twenty-fourth of August, which are the residues of a draft dated the third and the fourth of August, and then this entry certified the pair as corrected. The Batch 0003 review caught it and `CR-051` section 1 finished it: the end of July is SEVEN DAYS AGO on Friday the seventh of August and TWENTY-FOUR DAYS AGO on Monday the twenty-fourth of August. THE GENERAL RULE THIS ADDS, AND IT IS `CR-049`'S RULE APPLIED TO A RELATIVE DATE: a repair that changes a relative date has to re-derive it from the counted chain, because the sentence being replaced is the source of the wrong figure and not a check on it.** NO PLOT, MOTIVE, RELATIONSHIP BEAT OR FACT ABOUT THE WORLD CHANGED, and the four words in the condition column were not touched, because a later block may not improve them.**

**6. TWO COUNTS IN THE MANUSCRIPT THAT ARE TWO COUNTS AND THAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT MERGE.** (a) **The five of his own — the plate, the seventh column on a table in his own office, the not-telling, the rule on the facing page, and the paragraph and signature in two inches of ruled space — are five, and Chapter 157's *fifth of five things he has given up* is correct and refers to that list. The six of the other shape — things *got by writing something down in this book and not doing it*, Chapter 155's count — are a DIFFERENT list, and Chapter 159 adds a sixth to it, the seventy-eight words not said to a foreman (they were called eleven words in the first version of this entry and it is seventy-eight, counted on the chapter file — see `CR-051` item 8), and says so on the page in the man's own handwriting: *those are two different lists and I am writing that down now because in about four years somebody is going to merge them and add them up and get a number that means nothing.*** (b) **The man of about forty-one on no list at all has said nine words on ELEVEN occasions through Chapter 150, the eleventh of them in Chapter 144, and Chapter 157 is the TWELFTH; the number of words is nine and the number of occasions is twelve and they are not the same number. Chapters 158, 159 and 160 print no total at all.**

**7. THE POV BREAK, WHICH IS A STRUCTURAL CHANGE AND IS RECORDED AS ONE.** **Chapter 158 is in Mara Quill's close third person and Milo Rook is not in the room. It is the second Mara-POV chapter in the manuscript after Chapter 30, and it is the only available way to satisfy a brief that requires her half of the month to be hers, that requires that he is not in the room, and that requires that she does not describe the line in the return.** The relationship milestone 3 is carried by a different person in this chapter and is deeper and unrepaired, and `state/current.md`'s standing line that POV is Milo Rook throughout is corrected for this one chapter and is otherwise unchanged.

**8. NEW ON-PAGE FACTS A LATER BLOCK MUST NOT BREAK.** Barnaby Pike's three refusals and his jar of coin and his nineteen years of dates in his head; the four bells and their four rules; Sabra Trewin's rope, her four children, her nine and seven and eleven, her four old women who are dead, and the date she has and will not give; Garn Ollerton's bell and her two hours a day and her twenty-six years without a miss; the mower man's ten minutes a morning with a sickle for nine years; the machine room's *you cannot take a column out without taking the day out* and *I am not going to be the man who stops a conversation nobody started*; the return, the grid, the cell, the dash, the query, and the rule that a blank is a query and a query goes down; the register of correspondents and the three versions of it that are all unsafe; the rack, its height, its mark on the shelf, and the man of forty-one's *it has been got from and never got from*; the satchel; the eleven men being somewhere; the eleven words; **and `A printed date does not know what day of the week it is` said in a working man's mouth on the last morning, which is the same sentence as the one printed in Chapters 146, 148 and 150 and is the one sentence in this block that explains something and explains nothing at all.**

**9. WHAT THIS BLOCK DID NOT DO, AND IT IS THE LIST A REVIEWER SHOULD CHECK FIRST.** No crossing; eleven remembered routes, ten crossings, ten tally lines. The pairs are **TWENTY-NINE and did not move once in forty-six days**, and the reason is on the page six times, and **if the count of pairs in this block is a small number and not a large one, that is correct.** No seventh column, and the rule ran out and he did not draw one on the first of September either, and nobody forgave him. No fifth unshowable thing. Nobody in the weather on a Wednesday. No form, no row, no list and no file for the four. No fifth sighting of the man in the works jacket. Nobody asked a timetable clerk anything. Nobody read the sixth line of fifty-four words and neither query was answered. **The Rainway is not named. No Weather remnant is identified and no demand is satisfied. The First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the Quieting are not mentioned. The First Quieting has no date. The office of the First Surveyor did nothing at all. Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel. Ada Fenn is not named and not referred to, and the count stands at eight. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–160. The standing last line stands unimproved in every Field Book entry.** Weather with Cadence leaking is named on the page in Chapter 153 and is not solved by being named. The ford is not resolved, not dated, not attributed, and nobody has been put inside it. **Doreen Sankey did not use the instrument.**

## CR-051 — Review repair pass on Volume 03 Batch 0003 (Chapters 151–160): the state layer certified corrections the manuscript did not contain, and nine other classes of figure that were wrong in the same way

**This is `CR-023`'s class and `CR-049`'s class in the same pass, and it exists because the review of Batch 0003 (`logs/volume-03-batch-0003.review.log`) found that the repair pass recorded in `CR-050` section 5 had certified a correction it had not made, which is worse than either error alone, because it disarms the next pass. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder or planned ending is changed. No chapter was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. The planned plot is unchanged. `outline/volume-03.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were NOT edited and need no change.**

**0. THE FINDING THAT MATTERS IS NOT A TYPO. A STATE ENTRY THAT SAYS *found correct* OR *corrected* WITHOUT NAMING A FILE AND A LINE IS A CLAIM WITH NOTHING BEHIND IT, AND `CR-049` ALREADY SAID SO. THREE ENTRIES IN `CR-050` AND IN `state/continuity.md` FAILED THAT TEST AND ALL THREE ARE NOW ANNOTATED IN PLACE RATHER THAN QUIETLY REPLACED, so that a reader of the old claim can see that it was wrong.**

**1. THE FALSE CERTIFICATION, FINISHED. `CR-050` section 5(d) claimed that Chapters 154 and 157 had been corrected to the promise having passed. The direction had been corrected and the figures had not: 31 July is **SEVEN DAYS** before Friday the seventh of August and **TWENTY-FOUR DAYS** before Monday the twenty-fourth of August, and the chapters said three and four. `chapter-0154.md` line 83 and `chapter-0157.md` line 81 now read *the end of July was seven days ago* and *the end of July was twenty-four days ago*. Both entries keep the decision to break the promise on the thirty-first of August, which is what the surrounding text already said the man was going to do, so the beat is untouched.**

**2. THREE DATE FIGURES THE `CR-050` PASS MISSED, ALL FROM ONE STALE ANCHOR. `chapter-0151.md` line 65 said the rule on the facing page was *eleven days off* on Thursday the twenty-third of July; the first of September is **FORTY DAYS** off, and Chapter 155's *twenty days off* on the twelfth of August is correct. `chapter-0151.md` line 79 said a foreman of fifty-four was *thirteen weeks and two days* past four months on the twenty-third of July, and `chapter-0152.md` line 109 said *thirteen weeks and three days* on the twenty-fourth; the chain is now correct throughout, because every other figure in the block already sat on the true anchor.**

> **THE FOREMAN'S CLOCK, WHICH NOBODY IS GOING TO ASK HIM ABOUT, AND WHICH IS NOW COUNTED FROM A CHAPTER HEADING AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE: 11 weeks 3 days on Saturday the eleventh of July (Ch 144, and the brief), 12 weeks 5 days on Monday the twentieth of July (the brief), 13 weeks 1 day on Thursday the twenty-third of July (Ch 151), 13 weeks 2 days on Friday the twenty-fourth (Ch 152), 14 weeks 2 days on Friday the thirty-first (Ch 153), 14 weeks 5 days on Monday the third of August (the brief), 15 weeks 2 days on Friday the seventh (Ch 154), 16 weeks exactly on Wednesday the twelfth (NOT PRINTED — no chapter gives a figure for the twelfth), 17 weeks 2 days on Friday the twenty-first of August (the brief), 17 weeks 5 days on Monday the twenty-fourth (Ch 157), 18 weeks exactly on Wednesday the twenty-sixth (the brief), 18 weeks 1 day on Thursday the twenty-seventh (the brief), 18 weeks 5 days on Monday the thirty-first (Ch 159), and 19 weeks 2 days on Friday the fourth of September (Ch 160, which printed 19 weeks 3 days and was one day out — see the note below).** **THERE IS NO FOREMAN FIGURE FOR TUESDAY THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER AND NONE MAY BE PRINTED, because no chapter gives one. `state/continuity.md`'s fourteenth list item 10 previously carried *19 weeks exactly (1 Sep)*, which had nothing behind it, and it has been replaced by the line above, which is quotable.**

**3. THREE FIGURES IN THE STATE LAYER WITH NO CHAPTER BEHIND THEM, ALL CORRECTED IN PLACE.** (a) The **Wednesday the fifth of August** in `state/continuity.md` item 9 does not exist on the page and no chapter is dated to it; the two Wednesdays the block actually prints are **the twenty-second of July (Ch 151) and the twelfth of August (Ch 155 line 101)**, and item 9 now says those. (b) Item 10's *19 weeks exactly (1 Sep)* is removed, as above. (c) Item 10 listed the foreman's clock without the twenty-fourth of July, which Chapter 152 prints, and canonised the wrong twenty-third of July; both are corrected.

**4. A FLAT CONTRADICTION ON THE PLATE, AND THE CANON IS THE BRIEF'S, NOT CHAPTER 159's.** `chapter-0151.md` line 35 said the decision made on the first of June *has been told to nobody* and then, in the same breath, that *there are four people in this city who are not told about it*; `chapter-0159.md` line 91 said the opposite, that it *has been told to four people in this city and one of them is me*. **The brief for this block says the plate is never reported and the decision was made on the first of June and is told to nobody, and two of the four printed instances said told to nobody, so told to nobody is the canon.** All three instances now read the same way, and the figure four survives in the form the page can carry: **nobody has been told, and four people in this city know it is in that grass and have said nothing about it.** `chapter-0151.md` lines 35 and 79, `chapter-0159.md` line 91 and `chapter-0160.md` line 73 are consistent, and `state/continuity.md` items 5(ii) and 9 have been annotated to the corrected version. **A later block may not report the plate, may not ask anybody about it, may not put it on a list, and may not convert the four who know into four who have been told.**

**5. A DUPLICATE ORDINAL AND AN UNSTABLE COUNTER, BOTH NOW MONOTONIC.** *An interval of one day is not a pair* was called **the fifth time I have said it** twice — `chapter-0151.md` line 73 on the twenty-third of July and `chapter-0153.md` line 95 on the thirty-first. Chapter 153 is now **the sixth time**, and `chapter-0159.md` line 105 is now **the seventh time I have written it**, so the chain is 5, 6, 7 across the three entries that carry an ordinal, and the other three of the six instances of the reason (152, 156, 160) carry no ordinal, which is correct. Separately, **the eleven things of that shape in eight months** — things he produced or wrote down and did not act on — ran *ninth* (Ch 151:57), *the tenth of the ten* (Ch 151:77), *the first of the ten that is neither producing it nor writing it down* (Ch 154:61) and *tenth* (Ch 155:83). Ch 155 is now **the eleventh**, and the three that are of a piece with Ch 151's ninth and tenth are consistent. **A later block may not print a twelfth, and may not reuse either ordinal for anything else.**

**6. `CHAPTER 154` HAD HIM IN THREE PLACES AT ONCE, AND IT IS THE CHAPTER WHOSE WHOLE SUBJECT IS THE THREE POSTS.** Line 43 said *He did not go along the path. He stood on the deck and he did not go past the second post either*; line 59 read the cast number out loud *on a bridge deck*; line 67 said *he stood at the third post for about four minutes*. The first post is about a hundred and thirty yards from the parapet, the second about a hundred and thirty further and the third about a hundred and thirty further again, so the third post cannot be a bridge deck. **The chapter now has one walk: he crosses the deck at about a quarter past eleven, goes out along the path, is at the second post at about half past eleven, does not go and look at the two sets of marks, goes on to the third, reads the number out loud standing up about three hundred yards from a bridge deck, stands at the third post for about four minutes and does not go on, and is back over the bridge at about twenty past twelve.** The mower man is still standing on the deck saying that he has been watching him since the quarter past eleven, which is now true of the deck and not of the fen, and **the mower man is not moved off the deck and the two sets of marks are not moved off the second post and the cast number is not moved off the third.** Line 63 also called a cast number in a collar *the only piece of paper anybody has ever made about this district* and then said the number was on a printed side in the middle of May, which cannot both be true; it is now **a work of record that is not in a building**, which is the argument the sentence was making, and its two trailing clauses have been moved out of unmarked first person into the third person the rest of the chapter keeps. **THE BOUNDARIES ARE STILL THREE AND THE SETS OF MARKS ARE STILL THREE AND NOTHING HAS BEEN JOINED.**

**7. THE RETURN LEG, WHICH WAS AN OPEN QUESTION IN THE REVIEW AND IS NOW AN EXPLICIT DECISION. `CHAPTER 154` PUT HIM DOWN AT A GATE AT THE NORTH END OF MIREWARD AT HALF PAST TEN AND NEVER BROUGHT HIM BACK, WHILE CHAPTERS 152, 153 AND 156 ALL RIDE THE FLATBED BOTH WAYS.** **The bridge is in MIREWARD, the chapter is a Friday flatbed trip like its neighbours, and he now walks back up to the north end and is on the flatbed at about ten to four and in the yard at the city end at about half past four, with the same four sacks still on the bed.** The flatbed is therefore on a Friday in, at Mireward at about half past ten, and back at the city end at about half past four, and does not wait either way, and a lorry to a yard at the north end comes in on a **Thursday** morning, which is what `chapter-0152.md` line 7 and the brief both say and which is now what `chapter-0156.md` line 17 says, spoken by Garn Ollerton, where it had been a Tuesday lorry. **A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT PUT THE LORRY ON ANY DAY BUT THURSDAY, MAY NOT PUT A MIREWARD TRIP ON ANY DAY BUT FRIDAY, AND MAY NOT LEAVE A TRIP WITH NO RETURN LEG.**

**8. TWO WORD-COUNT MOTIFS THAT WERE APPLIED TO THE WRONG THING.** (a) The sentence Milo Rook does not say to Roy Pryce was called **eleven words** at `chapter-0157.md` line 57 and **The eleven words** at `chapter-0159.md` line 53. **It is seventy-eight words**, counted on the chapter file, and both are now seventy-eight. The sentence itself is not one word shorter and the four words in the condition column are not one word longer. **A later block may not improve it, shorten it, or call it any other number of words.** (b) The man of forty-one on no list at all says **nine words**, and that is the established shape of him and it has been used for twelve occasions through Chapter 157. `chapter-0160.md` applied *That is nine words* to a reply that is **forty-four words**, and the tic belongs to that man alone. **His answer is now nine words — *It has been got from and never got from.* — and the two clauses that made it forty-four have been moved into the narration immediately after, where they still do their work and where they explain why the nine words are the nine words. No other character in this manuscript says nine words and nobody else ever will.**

**9. THE BLOCK'S OWN TITLE PHRASE WAS ATTRIBUTED TO WORDS THAT DO NOT CONTAIN IT.** `chapter-0152.md` line 101 said *Four copies of a bell. That is the phrase and I did not invent it, he did, in about four words, when I asked him how many and he would not say: I do not know the number either* — and those four words contain no bell and no four, and line 103 of the same entry says there is **no word in this city** for what the four of them have in common. **The phrase is now his own, coined in his own book on the Friday afternoon, and the entry says so and says that *copies* is the wrong word because there is not one thing here four times over. THIS IS THE THESIS OF THE BLOCK AND IT IS ALSO HIS FLAW: HE HAS NAMED IT, IN A BOOK IN A COAT, AND NOBODY HAS IT BUT HIM, AND HE HAS NOT WRITTEN THE FOUR DOWN.** The batch title and the chapter title stand and are not affected. **A later block may not make the phrase anybody else's, may not have anybody else use it, may not put it in a document, and may not let the four become a row.**

**10. THREE MINOR DEFECTS AND ONE BAD CERTIFICATION.** `chapter-0151.md` line 55 said **There is a gate at a gate in eleven streets**; it now says a gate in eleven streets, and the same line's school — gone in about 1968 as a school, the building something else — now says in one clause that **the one with children in it is four miles the other way in a village**, so that it cannot be read as denying Sabra Trewin's working schoolroom in Cobb Lock, which is a live room with seventeen in it and a bell on a bracket over its door. `chapter-0153.md` line 45 said *There are four who will go and there are seventeen in here and four will go*; it now says four is the number that goes, once, and keeps the rule unwritten. **The two net-new referents in `chapter-0153.md` line 21 — a man of thirty at a cardew counter in the city and a woman of thirty-one at a records counter — are inside constraint 14 as written and are carried forward: they are people who have been asking about things, they are not to be named, and nothing that goes wrong in a later block may be attributable to either of them.**

**11. THE YEAR CONSTRAINT WAS NEVER TRUE AND THE STATE LAYER MUST STOP CERTIFYING IT.** The brief, `state/current.md` and `state/continuity.md` item 3 all said **NO YEAR APPEARS ANYWHERE IN VOLUME 03 AND NONE MAY BE ADDED**, and Chapters 151–160 print 1911, 1919, 1961, 1968, 1969, 1974, 1975, 1979, 1986 and 1998, and so do Chapters 131–150. **The rule that has actually been held, and is now stated in place of the one that was not: NO CALENDAR YEAR MAY BE ATTACHED TO THE STORY'S PRESENT-DAY CHRONOLOGY, TO A DAY ON THE COUNTED CHAIN, TO A DAY-COUNT, OR TO ANY EVENT; THE YEARS THAT APPEAR ARE THE AGES OF OBJECTS, BUILDINGS, INSTITUTIONS AND WAYS OF DOING THINGS — a bell frame from about 1911, a ford dry since about 1919, a bus that stopped about 1961, a school that went in about 1968, a repair schedule and a load calculation and a footing in the file from about 1974, a bell rung every day since about 1979, a closure in 1986, a line in a margin in 1998 — AND A NEW ONE MAY NOT BE ADDED TO THAT LIST.** The nine Atlas residue sentences are unaffected, and no year may be attached to the Chapter 136 line.

**12. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT TOUCH, SO THAT A LATER READER DOES NOT ASSUME IT DID.** The batch goal, the beat map, the four bells, the four refusals, Barnaby Pike, Sabra Trewin, Garn Ollerton, Roy Pryce, Mara Quill's chapter and her half of it, the typed name in column six, the walk on no sheet, the second tin's thirty-seven, the rack with nothing in it in it, the satchel, the eleven men being somewhere, the fourth pair count, the twenty-nine pairs, the day-registers, the walks at thirty-two, forty-one and forty-three, the six rack days, the standing last line, the volume's final image and the Rainway are all unchanged. **THE WEATHERDAY CHAIN, ALL THIRTY-SIX DATED REFERENCES, ALL FOUR DAY-REGISTERS AT EVERY PRINTED INSTANCE, THE TWELVE GANG DAYS AND THE SIX RACK DAYS, AND THE ENTIRE WEEKDAY ARITHMETIC WERE RE-CHECKED AND ARE CLEAN AND WERE NOT TOUCHED.** The state layer's claims about the *checked* figures — and only those — are what this entry now certifies, and the three that had nothing behind them are annotated above.

**13. THE THREE COUNTS A REVIEWER MUST CHECK, AND ALL THREE STILL PASS AFTER THIS PASS. The walks: gang days are Mondays and Thursdays and there are TWELVE of them between the twenty-first of July and the thirty-first of August, and the block prints the THIRTY-SECOND (23 Jul, Ch 151), the FORTY-FIRST (24 Aug, Ch 157) and the FORTY-THIRD (31 Aug, Ch 159), and the last of those is the last walk of the season, and no total running count is printed anywhere. The racks: rack days are Fridays and there are SIX — 24 and 31 July, 7, 14, 21 and 28 August — and the twenty-eighth is the last Friday inside the contract, and there is no Friday the thirtieth because the thirtieth of August is a Sunday. The second tin: THIRTY-ONE (23 Jul), THIRTY-TWO (24 Jul), THIRTY-THREE (31 Jul), THIRTY-FOUR (7 Aug), THIRTY-FIVE (14 Aug), THIRTY-SIX (24 Aug) and THIRTY-SEVEN, the last figure it ever holds (28 Aug), and there is no thirty-eight.** `workspace/volume-03/volume-03-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` now carries the foreman chain from item 2 above and the corrected year rule from item 11, so that Batch 0004 does not inherit either error.

## CR-052 — Volume 03 Batch 0004 is written (Chapters 161–170): *What The Rain Is Carrying* — THE VOLUME MIDPOINT, THE RAINWAY NAMED, A PUBLIC MOURNING DISPLACED FROM THE QUIETING, AND THE REGISTRY'S CORRECT REFUSAL

**1. NO PREMISE, WORLD RULE, POWER RULE, ANTAGONIST LADDER OR PLANNED ENDING IS CHANGED BY THIS BATCH.** **Ten chapters, Monday the seventh of September to Wednesday the thirtieth of September, twenty-four days. 30,273 words** (3,055 / 2,389 / 2,984 / 2,993 / 2,217 / 2,752 / 2,581 / 2,594 / 4,964 / 3,744, every figure `wc -w` on the chapter files **after the verification pass in `CR-053` below**). What follows records what the manuscript fixed, and what a later block, a reviewer or a repair pass must not undo. Every figure is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0161.md` … `chapter-0170.md`. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited and needs no change; `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited. **The volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200. Volume 03 IS OPEN AND IS NOT CLOSED AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PHASE EXISTS FOR IT.**

**2. THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, WHICH IS A STRUCTURAL CHANGE AND IS RECORDED AS ONE, AND WHICH IS THE SERIES' HINGE.** `outline/series.md` gives the Volume 3 midpoint reversal as: *the Rainway is carrying a public mourning that was displaced from the Quieting; the storm is not a weapon designed by one villain, but the Registry's refusal to acknowledge it has made it worse.* **THE MANUSCRIPT WRITES THAT, AND WRITES IT IN A FORM THAT EXPLAINS NOTHING THAT CAME EARLIER.** The mechanism, all of it on the page in Chapters 166, 169 and 170: **a district that cannot report a loss is a district with nothing to report; a district with nothing to report is entered as stable; a district entered as stable is a district that cannot be frightened; a district that cannot be frightened is a district where there is nothing to insure against; and a district where there is nothing to insure against is a district whose works are not reimbursable at the present rating, which is one of the four grounds on a key printed on the second side of a district schedule, and the key is correct.** And the sentence that carries it, in a department's mouth, in nine seconds, minuted, in Chapter 166: **THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS CITY IN WHICH A DISTRICT CAN SAY THAT SOMETHING HAPPENED TO IT.** And the displacement, in Chapter 169: **somebody kept a day; forty people put their names in a column in ONE hand; the sheet became the account; the account wanted one account; the part of a mourning that would not go into one account did not stop; it went out of the record; and out of the record is the weather. IT IS NOT A WEAPON. NOBODY DESIGNED IT. NOBODY PUT IT THERE.** **A later block may not make the Rainway designed, or a weapon, or anybody's fault, may not date the Quieting, may not name a person who was in a room, and may not resolve the order, the removed sentence, the withdrawn classification or the standing over.**

**3. THE RAINWAY IS NAMED, AND IT IS NAMED ORDINARILY, AND THE NAMING DOES NOT EXPLAIN THE NAMING.** **Rainway is the name of a water, printed on the outside of a wrapper in a hand that is not a hand in that room, in about 1911, on a consignment of drainage material from a drainage board that no longer exists, received unasked for and marked *not evidence, not for index*.** It is the name on nine plots of about four acres held on shares, it runs about two miles above a ford dry since about 1919, **and nobody in this district has ever used the word in a conversation and a repository clerk of fifty-four has never heard it said.** **IT IS NOT THE NAMING OF A PERSON, IT IS NOT A NEW GRAMMAR, IT IS NOT A NEW REMNANT, AND IT IS NOT A WEATHER PHENOMENON'S NAME: a bundle of paper that came from a drainage board has the board's water on the outside of it, and that is the only reason the two things are in the same room, and it is not a reason.** He did not join it to the four bells, the bell at the ford, the plate, the cast number, the handrail post, the card with his name in it, the two typed names or the yard. **The naming is his, is in one book in a coat, is on no card and in no schedule and no return and no register, and a word in a book does not make it rain and does not stop it raining.**

**4. THE FIRST ATLAS, THE SINGULAR MODE AND THE QUIETING ARE NAMED, ONCE, IN CHAPTER 169, IN THREE LINES OF SMALL TYPE ON THE OUTSIDE FLAP OF A BUNDLE.** > *Schedule of instruments* / **First Atlas** — citywide; custody distributed; gaps widened, not closed. **Singular Mode** — emergency setting; one account to become the Index's version; others suspended. **Quieting** — setting not shut down; forced output thereafter treated as the record. **HE WROTE THEM DOWN ONCE, IN HIS OWN HAND, IN THE PRINTED ORDER, AND DID NOT UNDERLINE THEM AND DID NOT WRITE WHAT HE THOUGHT THEY WERE AND DID NOT ASK.** **THEY EXPLAIN NOTHING THAT CAME EARLIER** — not the bell on a post at a ford dry since about 1919, not the plate in the grass, not the removed sentence, not the withdrawn classification of the eleventh, not the suspended order, not the matter that stands over, not the three clues from January that are three, not the four bells, and not a boy of seventeen. **NO PERSON, OFFICE, DEPARTMENT, FACTION OR ANTAGONIST IS NAMED IN CONNECTION WITH ANY OF THEM; the only body named on the wrapper is a drainage board that no longer exists; there is no date on the wrapper, none on the flap, no section number and no year on the sheet inside, and he did not ask for one. A later block may not make them into a complete explanation in Volume 03, may not resolve the seam, may not restore anything, and may not turn any of them into a haunting, a claim, a person, a curse, a debt or an inherited role.**

**5. A NEW ON-PAGE FACT OF THE SAME SHAPE AS THE BATCH 0003 ENGINE, AND IT IS NOT A NEW RULE: A DESCRIPTION OF A PLACE IS HOW A PERSON GETS FOUND.** A **maintenance responsibility return for Season 3**, completed by the office on a Thursday in about a minute and a half, carries a second box with a foreman of fifty-four's name in it who did not sign it **and, beneath it, the office at which returns for the district are held** — a correct statement, and in about four years a man can ask the side for the office and then the office for the desk and then for the machine and then for the man, **and nobody in the room noticed but the surveyor, and the man himself does not know and is not going to be told.** **NOBODY LIED. The mechanism is the August one exactly: a district with no correspondence address cannot be asked, so the office went to the record it holds, and a contract names the supervisor.** The same block, from the other side: **a district that can be written to is a district whose returns are held somewhere, and a district whose returns are held somewhere is a district somebody can ask a card from** — **and there are now four such places, and the fourth is a contractor's yard, and nobody audits a contractor's yard, and he made it in nine seconds on a Monday morning and did not ask the woman who had refused to enter it in a register on the twenty-seventh of August.** These are the same finding arrived at again, and **a later block may not join any of them to any other, may not make any of them a rule, and may not print the finding as a maxim.**

**6. TWO NEW WORKING PEOPLE, BOTH ORDINARY, BOTH REFUSING, NEITHER THANKED, NEITHER A DEVICE, AND NEITHER NAMED BY A NEW FACE BEING A PROBLEM.** **Coll Sarrowe, forty-four** — the block's working day and a correct refusal of a figure on a drainage return sent to his employer's farm, and the changed method on the bank, the run set facing out, which took him a fortnight and which he has not mentioned. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY WITH A MOWER** — refused to be a party to a boundary, in about ninety seconds, having held the sentence since the seventh of August. Both are refused numbers and refused roles; both changed nothing; **neither was thanked; a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked, and that is a conductor's sentence from April and it is not improved in September.**

**7. THE RECOVERY IN THIS BLOCK IS SOMEBODY ELSE'S WORK AND NEITHER A WORD, AND THE PERSON WHO KNOWS IS AN ORDINARY PERSON WITH A JOB WHO REFUSES THREE THINGS AND IS NOT THANKED.** **Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years on the hand index at the ninth desk on the left in the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights** — a changed method of her own (**she goes and looks before she writes it down**, and the pad has no request column), a recovery of her own (**about nine finding aids, which are descriptions of what is in a room and are not catalogue items and cannot be looked up and therefore cannot be asked after**), a bundle she will not open, a shelf number she will not give, a second flap she will not turn, three words she will not interpret, and the sentence the volume turns on: **a repository is a room where things go that a department has decided are not evidence, and the reason a department sends a thing away is always the same, and the reason is that the thing could not be put in the form.** **She is not the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years, who is not mentioned in these ten chapters. She has no power, is not an office, and did not become one.**

**8. THE THING GIVEN UP ON PURPOSE THIS BLOCK, AND THE TWO LISTS.** Monday the twenty-eighth of September, in about four seconds, standing up, in a yard: **A YARD — the only instrument in this district that has never once turned into a document. He gave up a hundred and ten words he had had ready since the seventeenth of July. It cost Roy Pryce, who does not know it was a decision. Nobody asked him to.** **THE FIVE OF HIS OWN ARE NOW SIX AND THE SIX OF THE OTHER SHAPE ARE SIX, AND THE TWO LISTS ARE TWO LISTS, AND THE SUM OF THEM IS NOT A NUMBER — and a later block may not merge them, may not add a seventh to either, and may not call the sixth of his own the sixth of the other shape.** The rule on the facing page still runs out, still releases nobody, **and on the seventh of September he wrote in his own book that he wanted a column and did not draw one, and drew four headings of a fifth column in pencil on the back of a sheet of his own paper and did not draw that either, and a later block may not forgive him and may not improve him.**

**9. THE FAILURE AND WHAT IT COST, AND THE COST IS A BOY OF SEVENTEEN.** Monday the twenty-first of September, one good question in six weeks, one request made in four seconds without being built: **a district returns card was put into a Thursday bundle going to a firm, and a foreman of fifty-four read it standing up on Thursday the twenty-fourth, put it in a bin, and posted it back in somebody else's envelope on the Friday, and he did not know anybody had sent it and is not going to be told.** **THE COST IS NOT THE CARD. THE COST IS THAT PELL HARROW, SEVENTEEN, WHO HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED FOR ANYTHING BY ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT, WAS ASKED FOR ONE THING IN A YARD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND SAID NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND WAS RIGHT — and Milo Rook has put himself on the list of people who have asked him, and it took eleven seconds and he cannot get it off, and there is no form anywhere for the difference between a no that a man in a good coat has heard and a no that nobody heard.** **TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND, his own blank card is still one of the three things under the brick and has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off. A later block may not put him on a sheet, a delivery, a round or a list, and may not make him the carrier.**

**10. THE RELATIONSHIP, MILESTONE 3, DEEPER, IN FRONT OF SOMETHING, AND NOT REPAIRED.** The last Thursday of the month falls in this block and **he was not in the room**, as in Chapter 158. **The custody register page is untouched for the TENTH consecutive block — in a drawer with a coat in it, not produced, not named, not confessed to, not mentioned in dialogue, and not mentioned in one word in the corridor.** He has not told her about the season and **no week-count is printed in these ten chapters**; she found out he had stopped counting by noticing the number stopped going up and has not told him she noticed. **She did not describe the line in the return, nobody asked her to, and he was not in the room.** She put **four dashes in four cells with a pen** and **the reason the district has an address is that he gave it one, in nine seconds, on a Monday morning, and did not ask her, and she said that asking her would not have stopped it and she was right about that too.** The disagreement, fourth time, unrepaired, colder, both sides correct, and **she said his half for him**; and the true reason, said once, by him: **you did it because you are a man who writes things down and there was a week with nothing in it and you had nowhere to put it.** **A later block may not repair this in this volume, may not make either of them warm, may not let either of them win it, and may not have her describe the line on a reconciliation day or have him in the room.**

**11. WHAT THIS BLOCK DID NOT DO, AND IT IS THE LIST A REVIEWER SHOULD CHECK FIRST.** No crossing; eleven remembered routes, ten crossings, ten tally lines. **The pairs are twenty-nine and did not move once in twenty-four days, the reason is on the page nine times, NO ORDINAL IS PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THIS BLOCK, and if the count of pairs in this block is a small number and not a large one, that is correct; two sevens in about a fortnight is still a coincidence.** No seventh column drawn and a fifth column wanted and not drawn, and nobody forgave him. No fifth unshowable thing. **Nobody in Chapters 161–170 stands in the weather on a Wednesday, and the thirtieth of September is a Wednesday, and he is not counting them and no total is printed.** No form, no row, no list and no file for the four bells; no form, no row, no list and no file for a name in a coat. No fifth sighting of the man in the works jacket. Nobody asked a timetable clerk anything; nobody was sent; the man from the round was not sent; the clerk has no first name. Nobody read the sixth line of fifty-four words and neither query was answered. **No Weather remnant is identified and no Weather remnant's demand is satisfied; the ford is not resolved, not dated, not attributed, and nobody has been put inside the bell on the post at it, which has been dry since about 1919.** The office of the First Surveyor did nothing at all. There is still no second outlook and nobody has asked for one in writing since May. The district stability column is still a hand-transferred nil and is still not typed. A man of fifty-eight did not go out in September. **Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and nothing that went wrong in this block is attributable to a new face.** Ada Fenn is not named and not referred to, and the count stands at eight. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–170. Doreen Sankey did not use the instrument.** Weather with Cadence leaking is inherited from Chapter 153 and was not used and not solved. **THE STANDING LAST LINE STANDS, UNIMPROVED, IN THE NINE FIELD BOOK ENTRIES OF THIS BLOCK — CHAPTERS 161, 162, 163, 164, 166, 167, 168, 169 AND 170 — and Chapter 165 has no Field Book entry at all, and there is no Atlas residue anywhere in this block, which is correct, because the one appearance Chapters 151–160 were allowed has been spent.**

## CR-053 — The writer's own verification pass on Volume 03 Batch 0004 (Chapters 161–170): nine figures and enumerations that were wrong in the class `CR-023` and `CR-049` exist to stop, one of them a contradiction between two chapters of the same block, and two state-layer figures `CR-051` corrected in the chapters and not in the register

**This is `CR-023`'s class and `CR-051`'s class in the same pass, and it exists because the state layer has now been corrected against the manuscript eighteen times in eighteen passes and the failure mode is always the same: a figure written after the prose and read back as authority. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder or planned ending is changed. No chapter was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. The planned plot is unchanged. `outline/volume-03.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were NOT edited and need no change.**

**1. A CONTRADICTION BETWEEN TWO CHAPTERS OF THE SAME BLOCK, AND IT IS THE WORST OF THE NINE BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY ONE A READER COULD CATCH.** Chapter 165 printed the finding aid's fourth line as *came in with a consignment of drainage material **about 1979*** and Chapter 169 printed the same bundle's wrapper as a hand from **about 1911** and the water in that city's land papers since **about 1911**. Both years were already on the permitted list and neither is illegal, but they cannot both be the same bundle, and **the 1911 reading is the one the chapter that owns the bundle requires, because it is what makes the volume's engine a thing that predates every other date in it.** `chapter-0165.md` now reads **about 1911**, and the same line in `state/chapter-summaries.md` was corrected with it.

**2. FIVE ELAPSED-DAY FIGURES FOR THE FIELD BOOK'S LIST OF PAIRS, ALL COUNTED FROM A DRAFT AND NOT FROM THE SIXTEENTH OF JULY.** The list has not moved since the night of the sixteenth of July, so the figures must be counted forward from that date. The block printed **sixty-four (21 Sep), sixty-five (22 Sep), sixty-five (23 Sep), seventy-one (24 Sep), seventy-five (28 Sep), seventy-six (29 Sep) and seventy-seven (30 Sep)**; the true figures are **sixty-seven, sixty-eight, sixty-nine, seventy, seventy-four, seventy-five and seventy-six**. All seven are corrected. **This is `CR-049`'s rule applied to an elapsed-day count: a repair that changes one has to re-derive it from the counted chain, because the sentence being replaced is the source of the wrong figure and not a check on it. The twelve that were right — fifty-eight on the twelfth, sixty-one on the fifteenth, sixty-four on the eighteenth — were right because they were computed from the chain in the first place, and the seven that were wrong were carried from an outline.**

**3. THE TRANSPORT, WHICH IS A CHAPTER-CLASS DEFECT AND NOT A FIGURE, AND WHICH WAS CAUGHT BEFORE IT REACHED THE STATE LAYER.** Chapter 163 was written as a Monday trip in a van. **The flatbed is the Friday vehicle, and a Monday in Mireward is not available, and a chapter that needs him in Mireward on a day when the only vehicle is on the other side of the city needs a man in a van, and it must say whose van.** The chapter is now a **Friday the eighteenth of September flatbed trip with the return leg on the page** — in from the city at about a quarter to seven, Mireward at about half past ten, four sacks on the bed, back on the Friday afternoon, in the yard at the city end at about half past four — **and Chapter 162 remains the Tuesday trip and says whose van it is, which is the mower man's, and which belongs to a firm, and it is not a day off and the chapter says so.**

**4. A FIELD BOOK ENTRY THAT LISTED A DITCH KEEPER'S BAR AMONG THE FOUR BELLS, WHICH IT IS NOT.** `chapter-0162.md` printed the four as *a woman of thirty-four in a schoolroom, a man of thirty-seven on a step, a man of sixty-one on a top track, and a man of forty-four on a bank*. **A bar is a bar, and a man with a bar on a bank is not a bell, and the fourth of the four is a bell on a post at a ford that has been dry since about 1919 and that nobody has ever put a ring on.** Corrected in the chapter. **THE COUNT OF THE FOUR IS FOUR AND IT IS STILL FOUR AND THEY ARE STILL NOT WRITTEN DOWN.**

**5. AN "EIGHT INSTRUMENTS" COUNT ATTACHED TO A LIST OF TEN.** `chapter-0170.md` printed *Eight things that are not one* and then enumerated ten items. **The enumeration is now eight: the punch roll, the yard man's flag and book of times, the field book, the pad with four lines in it, the roll of nineteen children in a woman's head, three dates in three heads none of them given, the smell of a fen in a dry week, and the name on the outside of a wrapper — which is the eighth and is the new one.** The bells were removed from the enumeration because they are already in part (a) as four bells doing four jobs and **a later block may not turn the four into a pattern.**

**6. TWO CALENDARY FIGURES FOR THE CARD'S JOURNEY, WHICH WERE THE DATES OF A DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT.** `chapter-0170.md` printed that the card *went out in a Thursday bundle on the seventeenth of September and came back in a bin on the nineteenth*. **The seventeenth is the Thursday on which the maintenance responsibility return was completed; the card came off the machine on Wednesday the twenty-third and went out in the bundle on Thursday the twenty-fourth and came back on Friday the twenty-fifth**, and that is what Chapters 164, 166, 167 and 168 say between them. Corrected. **A reader who finds a figure that is right about its own subject and wrong about the day it is printed on is the failure this pass exists to catch, and this was one.**

**7. THREE COUNTERS THAT A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT REWIND, AND WHICH WERE ABOUT TO BE.** `chapter-0161.md` printed a *ninth thing of that shape in eight months* for a thing he did not write down, which is behind the count `CR-051` fixed at the eleventh, and `chapter-0162.md` printed a *fourth time* refusal figure and `chapter-0163.md` a *third time* a man had corrected his arithmetic about a place. All three are gone, replaced by forms that count nothing. **`CR-051` item 5 still holds: a later block may not print a twelfth thing of that shape and may not reuse the ordinals 5, 6 and 7 for anything else, and this block prints no ordinal for the pair reason at all, which is correct.**

**8. TWO STATE-LAYER FIGURES THAT `CR-051` CORRECTED IN THE CHAPTERS AND NOT IN THE REGISTER.** `bible/terminology.md`'s third Volume 03 block still carried **19 weeks 3 days for 4 September**, which `CR-051` item 2 corrected to **19 weeks 2 days** in `chapter-0160.md`, **and still carried the plate as TOLD TO FOUR PEOPLE AND ONE OF THEM IS MILO ROOK**, which `CR-051` item 4 corrected in three chapters to **told to nobody, and four people in this city know it is in that grass and have said nothing about it.** **BOTH ARE NOW ANNOTATED IN PLACE in that file rather than quietly replaced, with a note that the earlier version had the wrong polarity or was a day out. THIS IS THE SIXTEENTH TIME THE STATE LAYER HAS BEEN FOUND HOLDING A FIGURE THE MANUSCRIPT DOES NOT, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THE FINDING HAS BEEN IN `bible/` RATHER THAN IN `state/`, AND A LATER PASS SHOULD LOOK THERE FIRST.**

**9. RE-CHECKED AND CLEAN, SO THAT A LATER PASS DOES NOT SPEND BUDGET ON THEM.** The weekday chain at every dated reference in this block, on the fourteen dates 7, 11, 12, 15, 17, 18, 21, 22, 23, 24, 28, 29 and 30 September and 1 October, and the days between them, **all four day-registers at every printed instance** — page home 288/293/296/299/302/303/304/305/309/310/311, token 274/279/282/285/288/289/290/291/295/296/297, plate 154/162/165/168/169/170/171/175/176/177, condition 207/215/218/221/222/223/224/228/229/230 — **the foreman's clock at 20 w 3 d, 20 w 6 d, 21 w 2 d, 21 w 5 d, 21 w 6 d, 22 w exactly, 22 w 1 d, 22 w 5 d, 22 w 6 d and 23 w exactly**, the second tin at thirty-seven with no thirty-eight, the twenty-nine pairs with no running count, the seventh column never drawn, the seventy-eight words never called any other number, the plate told to nobody, and every prohibition in the brief. **THE TWO FIGURES COLLIDING ACROSS THE REGISTERS ARE NOT MERGED, THE NINETEEN CUTS IN A POST ARE NOT MERGED WITH NINETEEN YEARS OF DATES IN A MAN'S HEAD, AND NEITHER IS THE THREE-MINUTE GAP IN A CHANNEL.**

**10. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT TOUCH, SO THAT A LATER READER DOES NOT ASSUME IT DID.** The batch goal, the beat map, the Rainway and the displacement, the three words, the two lists, the yard, the failure and the boy, the relationship, the second printed side, the four bells, the boundaries, the marks, the third post, the handrail post, the drawer and the bus, the standing question in three parts, the day-registers, the standing last line, the volume's final image and the choice between a village and a building are all unchanged. **THE WEEKDAY CHAIN, ALL THIRTY-ONE DATED REFERENCES, ALL FOUR DAY-REGISTERS AT EVERY PRINTED INSTANCE, THE TEN FOREMAN FIGURES, AND THE ENTIRE DAY ARITHMETIC WERE RE-CHECKED AND ARE CLEAN AND WERE NOT TOUCHED.** The state layer's claims about the *checked* figures — and only those — are what this entry certifies, and the nine that were wrong are enumerated above with their file and their line.

**11. ONE MORE, FOUND IN THIS PASS'S OWN STATE LAYER AFTER THE CHAPTERS WERE WRITTEN, AND IT IS THE TENTH AND IT IS THE SIMPLEST OF THE TEN: A COUNT OF FIELD BOOK ENTRIES.** The state layer said the standing last line stands in **seven** Field Book entries and named Chapters 161, 163, 164, 166, 168, 169 and 170. **THE BLOCK HAS NINE, NOT SEVEN: Chapters 161, 162, 163, 164, 166, 167, 168, 169 and 170, and Chapter 162's entry is headed *Field Book, Tuesday the fifteenth of September* and Chapter 167's is headed *Field Book, Thursday the twenty-fourth of September* and both were missed because a count was written from a list of chapter numbers instead of from the chapter files.** Chapter 165 has no Field Book entry and closes on a coda that is not one, and a later block may not promote it into one. The count is now correct in `state/batch-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/canon-changes.md` and `bible/terminology.md`. **A FIELD BOOK ENTRY IS AN ENTRY HEADED *Field Book*, AND THE COUNT OF THEM IS COUNTED ON THE CHAPTER FILES AND NEVER FROM A LIST OF CHAPTER NUMBERS, WHICH IS `CR-023` AGAIN AND IS THE LAST THING THIS PASS HAD TO FIX.**

## CR-054 — Review repair pass on Volume 03 Batch 0004 (Chapters 161–170): two date arithmetics in the block's two set-pieces, an age that fused two people, a gender the state layer had certified, an enumeration in the sentence that forbids it, two frame breaks, two distances, two typos, and the batch's balance

**This is the `CR-023` and `CR-051` class for the twentieth time, on the review of the commit that wrote Volume 03 Batch 0004. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder or planned ending is changed. No chapter was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. The planned plot is unchanged, the beat map is unchanged, and the volume's final image and the choice set up for Batch 0006 are unchanged. `outline/volume-03.md`, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited, and no controller file was touched.** Every entry below names the file and the line, because a state entry that says *corrected* without naming a file and a line is a claim with nothing behind it.

**1. THE BATCH'S HEADLINE COUNTS WERE WRONG IN BOTH WINDOWS, AND THEY ARE THE CHAPTER'S CENTRAL ARTEFACT.** `chapter-0161.md` line 17 printed **two Fridays, the fourth and the eleventh, and the eleventh is tomorrow**, counting a Friday four days after the day of the scene; and line 19 printed **six Mondays and Thursdays**. Counted from the chain — `7th September 1969` is a Monday, `31st August` was a Monday, `4th September` a Thursday — there is **ONE** Friday between Monday the thirty-first of August and Monday the seventh, and **THREE** Mondays and Thursdays in the same window. The chapter's three numbers, printed at line 21 as *Seven. Two. Six.*, are now **Seven. One. Three.** The Field Book entry of Saturday the twelfth (line 101) repeated *two rack days* and *six Mondays and Thursdays*; it now states the arithmetic **as counted on the Monday and as it stood after Friday the eleventh** (one rack day then and two now, three Mondays and Thursdays then and four now), because the entry is written on the twelfth and the Friday falls inside its own week. **No chapter anywhere in the block now prints two rack days or six Mondays and Thursdays, and the three figures were corrected at the source and in all five places the state layer had copied them: `state/continuity.md` item 9 of the sixteenth list, `state/chapter-summaries.md` Chapter 161, `state/current.md` (both the Batch 0004 section and the banner of this repair), `state/open-threads.md`, and `bible/terminology.md`'s fourth Volume 03 block.**

**2. CHAPTER 167's THREE DATE FIGURES COULD NOT ALL BE TRUE ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH, AND THEY WERE THE FIGURES `CR-053` ITEM 6 HAD ALREADY REPAIRED IN CHAPTER 170.** The card came off the machine on Wednesday the twenty-third at about nine, went into the Thursday bundle at about eleven **on Thursday the twenty-fourth**, was in the yard on the twenty-fourth and came back in a bin on Friday the twenty-fifth (`chapter-0164.md` line 67, `chapter-0166.md` line 88, `chapter-0168.md` line 21, `chapter-0170.md` line 49). Against that, `chapter-0167.md` line 35 printed **on the Thursday before last** (which is the tenth of September), **I found it on Friday** and line 39 printed **I have been sitting with it for three days** (which points at the twenty-first, two days before he did it). The chapter now reads: the card **went out at about eleven this morning**, and she knows it **because she stood in a room with four machines on Tuesday afternoon and asked one question and the man told her and did not ask her why**; the hole in her counter is **Friday the eighteenth**; and she has been sitting with it **six days**. **The Tuesday question and the eighteenth are new in this repair and are not in any state file, and they are recorded here and in `state/continuity.md` item 8 of the sixteenth list and in `state/chapter-summaries.md` Chapter 167 so that a later block does not move them. They add no fact about Roy Pryce, about the man in the room with four machines, or about the card, and nobody in Batch 0004 learns anything new from them.**

**3. AN AGE CHANGED BY TWENTY-FOUR YEARS IN A CALLBACK, FUSING TWO PEOPLE.** `chapter-0169.md` line 78 printed **a woman of sixty-one with a folding rule**, which is the same scene and the same four seconds and the same June as `chapter-0137.md` lines 41 and 85, where she is **a woman of thirty-seven** — and sixty-one is this batch's figure for Barnaby Pike on the top track, who is a different man in a different room. She is now **a woman of thirty-seven**. The state layer had her right all along (`state/continuity.md` item 12 of the twelfth list), so this was manuscript-only and Batch 0005 would have inherited it unchallenged.

**4. CHAPTER 170 MADE MARA QUILL A MAN, EIGHT LINES AFTER CALLING HER A WOMAN, AND THE STATE LAYER HAD CERTIFIED IT.** `chapter-0170.md` line 81 printed **a man of thirty-one at a records counter … and he has not improved on it since** in the same paragraph that had already said *a woman of thirty-one*. She is a woman of thirty-one (`chapter-0164.md` line 77, `bible/terminology.md`'s fourth Volume 03 block). The chapter and the Chapter 170 entry in `state/chapter-summaries.md` both read **a woman of thirty-one … and she has not improved on it since** now.

**5. CHAPTER 162 WROTE THE FOUR DOWN, IN THE SENTENCE THAT SAYS WRITING DOWN THE FOUR IS THE LAST THING THAT MUST NOT HAPPEN.** `chapter-0162.md` line 100 enumerated a woman of thirty-four in a schoolroom, a man of thirty-seven on a step, a man of sixty-one on a top track and a bell on a post at a ford — inside his own Field Book, in the same entry that says *the last thing that has to happen in this district is somebody writing down the four*, and four lines after line 94 of the same chapter says *the four that he has were not written down*. The enumeration is gone. The entry now reads **Four bells in four places is four bells in four places … and the fifth thing is the one that would turn it into a row, and the fifth thing is somebody writing down the four, and that is the last thing that has to happen in this district. I have not written them down and I am not going to.** This also repairs two faults the reviewer found inside the same sentence: the old **and a fifth thing is four people doing a thing** was not a sentence, and the list was three people and a bell and so **four people doing a thing** was not what was enumerated. **The four are now written down nowhere in Chapters 161–170, which is what the refusal has required since `chapter-0152.md`, and the refrain *four people doing a thing* is untouched in Chapters 153, 159, 160 and 169, where it is correct.**

**6. THREE FRAME BREAKS IN FIRST-PERSON NARRATION, ONE MORE THAN THE REVIEW COUNTED.** `chapter-0164.md` line 51 said **the worst line in this chapter** and `chapter-0169.md` line 108 said **the only instance in this whole novel of a public grief that anybody ever entered anywhere**; the review also found, and did not list, `chapter-0169.md` line 64: **it is the difference between this chapter and the last one.** All three are gone. He now says the asking is **the worst line in this book** (*in this book* is the established Field Book idiom), that the difference between the flap and the shelf **is also the whole of the difference between the twenty-second of September and this morning**, and that the kept day **is the only one of anything like it that he has ever come across, and he has been doing this for eight months and he has not seen another one**. The last of those three is a first-person bounded claim where an omniscient one used to be, which is both truer to the narrator and stronger.

**7. THE VILLAGE WAS AT TWO DISTANCES IN ONE CHAPTER, AND THE BATCH HAD THE WRONG ONE IN THREE PLACES.** `chapter-0161.md` line 69 said **a village eleven miles from here** and line 107 said **a village four miles the other way**; `chapter-0165.md` line 111 and `chapter-0170.md` line 91 also said four. **Cobb Lock is about eleven miles away** — `chapter-0135.md` line 69 (*he was about eleven miles away with his coat on*), `chapter-0143.md` line 9, `chapter-0144.md` line 35, and `chapter-0165.md` line 127 already had *a town eleven miles away* — and **four miles is this volume's distance to Mireward** (`chapter-0152.md`, `chapter-0166.md` line 64), so the two cannot be reconciled by naming which place is meant. All three are now **eleven miles**, and `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt are corrected to match. **The choice set up for Batch 0006 is unchanged: the same village, the same woman of thirty-four, the same schoolroom, the same bell on the bracket.**

**8. A HUNDRED AND FORTY PEOPLE IS TWO PLACES, AND IT WAS NOT RESOLVED BY MOVING A FIGURE.** Mireward has had a hundred and forty people in it since Chapter 139, and Cobb Lock has had about a hundred and forty since `chapter-0135.md` line 19, and each figure is carried by three or four earlier chapters and by the state layer. Moving either one would have edited a settled batch for a coincidence, so the coincidence is now **on the page and denied on the page**: `chapter-0170.md`'s ledger paragraph, immediately after the two sevens that were a coincidence in July and are still a coincidence, now says that **Mireward has a hundred and forty people in it and the village has a hundred and forty people in it, and that is one number in two places about eleven miles apart, and a number in two places is a coincidence, and it is still a coincidence, and I am not going to improve it.** **A later block may not join the two populations, may not make either of them a clue, and may not move either figure.**

**9. THE ROUND WAS CALLED A WALK, WHICH CHAPTER 161 SAYS IT IS NOT.** `chapter-0167.md` line 5 read **the round's business, which is walking about a district that has nothing to collect**. `chapter-0161.md` line 3 says the round **is not a walk any more and nobody has called it one**, and the man with the mower says in `chapter-0163.md` line 23 that it **is not a walk and it is not a job**. It is now **going about a district that has nothing to collect**.

**10. A NUMBER THAT READS AS AN AGE WAS A SERVICE.** `chapter-0168.md` line 33 said Roy Pryce squared a barrier with his boot, **which is a thing a man of thirty-one years does**; he is fifty-four and has been in charge of a gang for thirty-one years, and thirty-one is this batch's figure for Mara Quill. It is now **a thing a man of fifty-four who has been in charge of a gang for thirty-one years does**.

**11. AN ENUMERATION OF FIVE READS AS SIX.** `chapter-0168.md` line 75 listed *the plate, the seventh column, the not-telling, the rule on the facing page, the paragraph and the signature in two inches of ruled space* and called it five. The five items are now separated, and the last one says **those last two are one thing and not two**, so the count of five is visible on the page rather than asserted.

**12. THE RETURN'S CELL COUNT HAD NO RULE, AND THE FIFTH CELL IS THE BATCH'S LAST IMAGE.** `chapter-0167.md` line 13 said a return has a cell for every week of the month and line 17 said there are four of them, and `chapter-0170.md` line 95 ends the batch on **the fifth cell of the fourth page from the bottom will be a dash**. The rule is now on the page in her mouth: **the number of cells is the number of Fridays in the month, which is a rule and not a reason, and September had four of them and October has five.** September has four Fridays in it — the fourth, the eleventh, the eighteenth and the twenty-fifth — and October has five — the second, the ninth, the sixteenth, the twenty-third and the thirtieth — so the rule produces both figures and the closing image is now derivable.

THE PATH LENGTH WAS THE DISTANCE TO THE FIRST POST, AND IT IS A CALLBACK, SO IT WAS CORRECTED IN BOTH CHAPTERS THAT CARRY IT.** `chapter-0163.md` line 41 had the man with the mower say **there are about a hundred and thirty yards of path on the far side that eleven men and a foreman used to walk twice a week**, and a hundred and thirty yards is the parapet-to-first-post distance in `chapter-0154.md` line 43 and `chapter-0163.md` line 29, while the third post is about ninety yards past the second. The path the gang walked is therefore **about three hundred yards**, and it is now that in `chapter-0163.md` line 41 **and in `chapter-0154.md` line 27, which is the same man's sentence in August and is the reason this is not a manuscript-only change**, and in the three state entries that quoted it (`state/character-state.md` twice and the Chapter 154 entry in `state/chapter-summaries.md`). **The parapet-to-first-post figure of a hundred and thirty yards is untouched in both chapters and is a different measurement.**

**15. TWO INVENTED SPELLINGS, BOTH IN FILES THIS BATCH CREATED.** `bible/terminology.md` and `state/canon-changes.md` both read **COLL SARROWE**. The correct form, in twenty-eight places including `bible/characters.md` line 283 and the other three Volume 03 terminology blocks, is **Coll Sarrowe**.

**16. CHAPTER 165 WAS THE BATCH'S THINNEST CHAPTER AND CARRIED THE CHANGED METHOD, THE RECOVERY AND THE SEED, AND IT HAS BEEN GIVEN ABOUT FOUR HUND WORDS OF ITS OWN MATERIAL.** The reviewer's fourteenth finding was balance, not error. `chapter-0165.md` now carries a second finding aid read in full — a press, four boxes of ties, one box gone from green and white to mixed, an inventory in a corridor that was correct in 1961, and her answer that **nobody has ever asked me for either**, that if somebody asked for the book she would send a book that is wrong and could not say how wrong without the sheet, and that the book being wrong in a way nobody could ever find is the part of it she minds. It adds no plot, no person, no institution and no fact about the bundle, and the year it names, 1961, is on the permitted list. **Nothing was cut from Chapter 169: the midpoint's extra room is defensible and every item it carries is required by the block.**

**20. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT TOUCH.** The batch goal, the beat map, the Rainway and the displacement, the three words, the three refusals and the seed, the two lists and the sixth of his own, the yard, the failure and the boy, the relationship, the second printed side, the four bells, the three boundaries, the three sets of marks, the third post, the handrail post, the drawer and the bus, the standing question in three parts, the day-registers, the standing last line, the volume's final image, and the choice between a village and a building. **No new grammar, no new faction, no new antagonist, no new final enemy, no new kind of place, and no new person.** **AND ONE THING THIS PASS FOUND AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT TOUCH: the phrase *a man of thirty-one* occurs about a dozen times in the state layer and in `bible/`, most of it in entries written for Volume 02 and Volume 03 Batch 0001, and it is a loose pre-existing usage for a man in his thirties rather than an age — it appears of Milo Rook, of a works clerk, of a man in a plan room and of others, and one entry (`state/continuity.md`, item 24 of an earlier list) already records that Chapter 124 stopped describing Milo Rook that way. It is not the Chapter 170 defect, which was a woman described as a man in the same paragraph that had already called her a woman, and it was not re-litigated here because a state figure cannot be corrected without the chapter it belongs to and none of those chapters is in this phase. A later pass that opens those chapters may tighten it; this pass did not.**
