## CR-059 — The writer's own verification pass on Volume 03 Batch 0006 (Chapters 181–190): the weekday chain, the day-registers at every printed instance, the vehicle on every day he leaves eleven streets, the Field Book entry count, the pairs, the two registers colliding at 352, and eleven frame breaks and two self-inflicted echoes the pass found and fixed

**This is the `CR-023` and `CR-051` and `CR-053` class for the twenty-third time, and it exists because the state layer has been corrected against the manuscript twenty times in twenty passes and the failure mode is always the same: a figure written after the prose and read back as authority. No premise, world rule, power rule, antagonist ladder or planned ending is changed. No chapter was restarted and no chapter was rewritten from the top. The planned plot is unchanged and the beat map is unchanged. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited and needs no change, and no controller file was touched. Every item below names the file and the passage, which is the rule `CR-051` sets.**

**1. THE WEEKDAY CHAIN, COUNTED FORWARD FROM `1st October Thu` IN `chapter-0171.md` AND NEVER FROM A STATE FILE, AND ALL ELEVEN RIGHT.** 31 Oct Sat, 1 Nov Sun, 2 Nov Mon, 3 Nov Tue, 9 Nov Mon, 10 Nov Tue, 16 Nov Mon, 20 Nov Fri, 23 Nov Mon, 24 Nov Tue, 26 Nov Thu, 30 Nov Mon. **AND FROM THE SAME CHAIN: 17 Nov Tue, 27 Nov Fri, 1 Dec Tue, 2 Dec Wed, 3 Dec Thu, 4 Dec Fri, and DECEMBER'S FOUR FRIDAYS ARE THE FOURTH, ELEVENTH, EIGHTEENTH AND TWENTY-FIFTH, NOT THE SIXTH, THIRTEENTH, TWENTIETH AND TWENTY-SEVENTH, which are November's.** The two sets are not the same four and the fifth-cell rule is a rule and not a reason. The batch's ten chapter dates are 31 Oct–3 Nov (Ch 181, Sat/Tue), 3 Nov (Ch 182, Tue), 9 Nov (Ch 183, Mon), 10 Nov (Ch 184, Tue), 16 Nov (Ch 185, Mon), 20 Nov (Ch 186, Fri), 23 Nov (Ch 187, Mon), 24 Nov (Ch 188, Tue), 26 Nov (Ch 189, Thu), 30 Nov (Ch 190, Mon).

**2. THE FOUR DAY-REGISTERS AT EVERY PRINTED INSTANCE, RE-DERIVED, AND ALL EIGHT CHAPTERS THAT PRINT THEM CLEAN.** The counted chain carries 30 Oct = 341 / 327 / 207 / 260 / 27 w 2 d, which is what `chapter-0180.md` prints, and every later figure in this block is that plus one per day. **Printed: 342 / 328 / 208 / 261 / 27 w 3 d (31 Oct), 344 / 330 / 210 / 263 / 27 w 5 d (2 Nov), 345 / 331 / 211 / 264 / 28 w exactly (3 Nov), 351 / 337 / 217 / 270 / 28 w 6 d (9 Nov), 352 / 338 / 218 / 271 / 29 w exactly (10 Nov), 358 / 344 / 224 / 277 / 29 w 6 d (16 Nov), 362 / 348 / 228 / 281 / 30 w 3 d (20 Nov), 365 / 351 / 231 / 284 / 30 w 6 d (23 Nov), 366 / 352 / 232 / 285 / 31 w exactly (24 Nov), 368 / 354 / 234 / 287 / 31 w 2 d (26 Nov), 372 / 358 / 238 / 291 / 31 w 6 d (30 Nov).** The elapsed-day figures for the Field Book's list of pairs, from the sixteenth of July: 107, 109, 110, 116, 117, 123, 127, 130, 133, 137, and 131 for the twenty-fourth, which Chapter 188 does not print. `chapter-0181.md` prints three of them and its Field Book entry is the Monday's, which is 109 and not 110, and the two are on the page and are not the same day. **NO FIGURE WAS CHANGED IN THIS PASS; EVERY ONE WAS RIGHT AT THE CHAPTER.**

**3. THE TWO REGISTERS COLLIDE IN THIS BLOCK AT 352, ON A TUESDAY, IN TWO CHAPTERS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED".** **THE PAGE HOME IS THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO ON TUESDAY THE TENTH OF NOVEMBER, IN `chapter-0184.md`, AND THE TOKEN IS COLD ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO ON TUESDAY THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF NOVEMBER, IN `chapter-0188.md`.** Both are on the page, both are labelled, fourteen days apart, and they are the same number in two different registers about two different things, **which is the eighth such collision in this manuscript after 190, 179, 192, 198 and 207 and the pair 221.** A later pass must carry both and must not merge them and must not read the one as the other. `state/continuity.md` item 3 of the eighteenth list records it.

**4. THE VEHICLE ON EVERY DAY HE LEAVES ELEVEN STREETS, CHECKED CHAPTER BY CHAPTER, BECAUSE THIS IS THE CHAPTER-CLASS DEFECT `CR-053` ITEM 3 EXISTS FOR.** **Saturday the thirty-first, Sunday the first: no vehicle and none. Monday the second: the round, on foot, out at ten past eight, in at about four. Tuesday the third: a CITY BUS, at about five past eight, back at about twenty past four. Monday the ninth: the round. Tuesday the tenth: THE MOWER MAN'S VAN, a white panel van with a rack for mowers, a company name in a colour that has gone the colour of the weather, two stops then the yard, at the yard at about twenty past two, and it is not a day off. Monday the sixteenth: the round, four flights of stairs to a counter. Friday the twentieth: A CITY BUS at ten past ten, and HE WAS NOT ON THE FLATBED, which came in at a quarter to seven and went back on the afternoon and is in the yard at the city end at about half past four and does not wait either way. Monday the twenty-third: the round, on foot, and he was on the far side of the bridge and back from about four o'clock to about twenty past five and got into eleven streets at about ten past six. Tuesday the twenty-fourth: a city bus at ten past ten, back at about half past three. Thursday the twenty-sixth: the round. Monday the thirtieth: the round.** **NO SCENE IN THIS BLOCK PLACES HIM IN MIREWARD, ON A WEDNESDAY, ON A SATURDAY OR ON A SUNDAY, AND NO LORRY APPEARS ON ANY DAY, WHICH IS CORRECT BECAUSE THE LORRY IS A THURSDAY-MORNING VEHICLE AND NO THURSDAY-MORNING SCENE LEAVES THE DISTRICT IN THIS BLOCK. THE VAN IS A TUESDAY AND IT STOPPED BEING ONE AFTER THE TENTH.**

**5. THE COUNT OF FIELD BOOK ENTRIES, WHICH IS EIGHT, MADE ON THE CHAPTER FILES.** `grep -c '^\*Field Book'` gives **1 in 181, 0 in 182, 1 in 183, 1 in 184, 1 in 185, 1 in 186, 1 in 187, 0 in 188, 1 in 189, 1 in 190.** **BOTH ABSENCES ARE ON THE PAGE AND BOTH REASONS ARE PRINTED: 182 because a day I spent in a schoolroom is not a day I had, and the reason is written in 183's entry; 188 because I said a thing out loud in a room for the first time in eight months and I am not going to write it down, and the reason is written in 189's entries one and two.** `grep -c 'the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything'` gives **1 in each of those eight and 0 in 182 and 188**, and the standing sentence is not varied in any of them. This is `CR-053` item 11's rule and the count was made on the files and not from a list of chapter numbers. **A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT PROMOTE EITHER OF THE TWO NON-ENTRIES INTO ONE.**

**6. ELEVEN FRAME BREAKS, ALL FIXED AT THE CHAPTER, ALL OF THE `CR-056` ITEM 8 CLASS.** `chapter-0182.md` had *this is one of the two districts in this story* and *the only reason this chapter exists*; `chapter-0183.md` had *he is not in this chapter*; `chapter-0185.md` had *it is the worst-shaped one in this book*; `chapter-0186.md` had *the best four lines in this book*, *for the rest of this book*, *the only thing in this story that a man has ever used as a repository*, and *nobody in this block has stood in the weather on a Wednesday*; `chapter-0187.md` had *the oldest sentence in this book* and *nobody in this block*; `chapter-0189.md` had *nobody in this block*; `chapter-0190.md` had *the oldest thing in this book* and *anything in this book*. **ELEVEN, AND ALL ELEVEN ARE GONE.** They are now *this city*, *this year*, *the oldest thing he owns*, *the best four lines he has written this year*, *the oldest sentence he has got*, *the book on the table*, *anything he has written in eight months*, and *nobody has stood in the weather on a Wednesday in November*. **THIS IS THE THIRD BATCH IN A ROW IN WHICH FRAME BREAKS HAVE BEEN FOUND, AND `CR-057` ITEM 6 RECORDED ONE INHERITED FROM BATCH 0004 THAT WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT, AT `chapter-0170.md` LINE 47, WHICH CARRIES *the whole of this volume* INSIDE A FIELD BOOK ENTRY. IT IS STILL THERE. IT IS INHERITED, IT WAS NOT A FINDING IN THIS PASS, AND REPAIRING ANOTHER BATCH'S CHAPTER IS NOT THIS PHASE'S WORK. IT IS RECORDED HERE FOR A LATER PASS AND IT IS THE ONLY KNOWN FRAME BREAK LEFT IN THE MANUSCRIPT.**

**7. TWO SELF-INFLICTED ECHOES FOUND AND CUT, BOTH IN CHAPTER 182, BOTH BEFORE THEY COULD BECOME CLUES.** (a) The first draft had him write *Asked. Nothing came.* on the back of a sheet at the end of the village day. **That is the three words on the card that came off a machine in June, and putting the same three words in his hand at the end of a village day would have manufactured a resonance and dressed it as a coincidence, and the chapter would have had to disclaim it, which is the shape of a clue. IT IS CUT AND REPLACED** with *She was right about the question. I was not, and I asked it anyway.* (b) The same draft had a boy announce his name in the schoolroom without any way of knowing it. **He now gives his name at the door at about a quarter to eleven because a woman of about sixty asked what to call him, and the chapter says that a name given at a door in a place with no address is the smallest thing he has done this year and is not nothing, and is not a party, and is not a letter.** That is a new on-page fact and it is recorded here and in `state/character-state.md`.

**8. THE SENTENCE AT THE TOP OF THE INSTRUMENT, WHICH MAY NOT BE REPEATED AS A MAXIM.** `chapter-0181.md` printed *a schedule is a thing that is either maintained or it is not maintained* three times. **IT NOW APPEARS VERBATIM ONCE IN THE WHOLE BLOCK, and the other two places say *the sentence at the top of the instrument is the office's own words*.** A later block may not print it again.

**9. THREE COUNTERS OF THE SHAPE `CR-053` ITEM 7 EXISTS TO STOP, ALL FIXED AT THE CHAPTER.** (a) `chapter-0183.md` claimed *three chances not to ask somebody and I have not asked on three of them* and only two had happened; it now says *I did not ask. He does not know that a piece of paper exists, and there is no answer I could give him that is not a sentence about a person, and that is going to be the reason every single time, and a reason is not a rule.* (b) `chapter-0184.md` claimed *the same four words he has used three times this month* where the count ran on a different basis; it now says *the same four words every time*, and its Field Book entry carries *three times in two days* for the two days it can be counted on. (c) `chapter-0185.md` printed *the fifth time in nine days* for a run that is four occasions between the ninth and the sixteenth; it is now **four times in seven days** in both places. **A LATER PASS SHOULD NOT TRUST A COUNTER OF NOT-ASKING ANYWHERE; IT IS NOT A STATISTIC AND THERE IS NO COLUMN FOR IT.**

**10. THE BALANCE, WHICH IS `CR-054` ITEM 16'S FINDING APPLIED AGAIN.** Chapter 184 is the thinnest at **2,685** and carries the gate, the third refusal-not-given, the water in a lane and the Tuesday given up at a gate. **Chapter 188 was at 2,387 and was given about seven hundred words of its own material — the part where each of them puts her own half on the table, a cell with four cells in it and four marks that mean nothing came, and the exchange about the thirty-four steps, and the part where neither of them says the word** — and is now **3,091.** The batch is **35,076 words** and every chapter is between 2,685 and 4,916. Chapter 190 is the longest because its extra length is the volume's question in three parts and the close, and Chapter 187 is the set piece. **NOTHING WAS CUT FROM ANY CHAPTER TO MAKE A COUNT COME OUT.**

**11. RE-CHECKED AND CLEAN, SO THAT THE NEXT PASS DOES NOT SPEND BUDGET ON THEM.** The four printed sides and their four dates and the three-and-one split; Roy Pryce's three places and the *none of record* box that is Cobb Lock's and is not his; the second column of the schedule; the mechanism ordinal; which district the 1961 belongs to; the two yardages and the rate; the second tin at thirty-seven; the rack; the plate told to nobody; the condition column at no new cumulative figure; the four unshowable things at four; the twenty-nine pairs with no ordinal; the two lists at six and six with nothing added to either and nothing merged; the nine words in the handcart answer with no total printed; the seventy-eight words not called any other number; the Rainway not joined to the schedule and not joined to the flood; the four bells not written down and not counted; the three Volume 01 clues unjoined; the three boundaries and the three sets of marks unjoined and nobody past the third post; the office of the First Surveyor a box with a rule and nothing on the rule; the district stability column a hand-transferred nil with no row added and nothing typed; no Ada Fenn and no ninth reference to a woman of sixty-eight; no Sel Iri, Halden Vey, Orrin Sable, Tarin Kest, Neris as a person, or Soren Rook; **no calendar year anywhere in the present-day chronology, and the only years named are about 1911, about 1961 and about 1968, all of which were already on the permitted list and none of which is new**; POV unbroken at Milo Rook in all ten chapters; and no crossing staged.
